## 1.4.223 - 2026-07-15
- **New: Channel-privacy cache + UNRESOLVED surfacing (GH-383).** Added a per-workspace channel-privacy cache (`ChannelPrivacyCache` in [src/channel-privacy-cache.js](src/channel-privacy-cache.js)) to cache Slack `conversations.info` privacy lookups, preventing transient Slack API outages from silently dropping `ask-reminders` matches. Lookups route through `ResolvePrivacyAsync(channelId, liveResolver, nowMs, ttlMs)` with a 1-hour default TTL. In case of API failure or `null` resolver results, return `UNRESOLVED` to preserve the fail-closed security invariant (treating unresolved/failed channels as private). Surfaced the unresolved count in `HandleAskRemindersCommandAsync` by returning a result object `{ matchedCount, unresolvedCount }` and replying with a warning copy (`Couldn't verify N channel(s) right now — results may be incomplete, try again.`) when no matching tasks are found but at least one channel lookup failed. Added comprehensive unit tests. [src/chat-commands/ask-reminders-command.js](src/chat-commands/ask-reminders-command.js), [tests/channel-privacy-cache.test.js](tests/channel-privacy-cache.test.js), [tests/ask-reminders-command.test.js](tests/ask-reminders-command.test.js), [package.json](package.json).

## 1.4.222 - 2026-07-16
- **New: `BaseModule` — a first-class per-workspace module primitive ([src/base-module.js](src/base-module.js)).** Codifies the tenant-isolation convention that `ChatModule`/`RemindersModule`/`ListsModule`/`NotionModule`/`StatsModule` follow by hand, and makes it structural: the owning `SlackApp` is **mandatory** at `super(ArgSlackApp)` (throws if absent, or if its `WORKSPACE_NAME` is empty — a nameless workspace would collide on shared per-tenant paths), and workspace state is reached only through getters — `this.SlackApp` / `this.Logger` / `this.WorkspaceInfo` / `this.WorkspaceName` — plus the owned `this.CommandRouter` (route snapshot via `GetRegisteredCommandRoutes()`, the same method name the catalog validator already calls on ChatModule/Reminders). This removes the exact temptation behind #384 (resolving a per-workspace `SlackApp` from a process-global). Provides handler-registration helpers (`RegisterAppMention/Message/Action/ReactionAdded`, which `.bind(this)`), a `RegisterCommandRoutes()` hook, and `StartAsync()` / `StopAsync()` lifecycle hooks (constructors stay synchronous; disk I/O in `StartAsync`). Getters (not inheritable `#private` fields) are deliberate — `#private` is invisible to subclasses. New modules extend `BaseModule`; the five existing modules keep the hand-rolled convention and migrate incrementally (each a small, independently reviewable swap). Self-check ([tests/base-module.test.js](tests/base-module.test.js)) regresses the #384 isolation property directly: two modules sharing one logger keep separate per-workspace route bindings. Documented in [docs/module-primitive.md](docs/module-primitive.md) and AGENTS.md §0.1.2. No existing module changed — additive only.

## 1.4.221 - 2026-07-16
- **CRITICAL fix (#384): `ask-reminders` answered from the wrong workspace in a multi-workspace deployment.** `ask-reminders` was the **only** command route not registered inside ChatModule's `#RegisterCommandRoutes()` (where every other route closes over its workspace's `this.#SlackApp`). Instead it was registered externally in [src/catalog-regex-aliases.js](src/catalog-regex-aliases.js) and resolved its SlackApp from a **global registry** — `global.__sleuthaskreminders__.slackApps.find(app => app.Logger === ArgLogger) || slackApps[0]`. Because every workspace is constructed with the **same shared `logger`**, the `find` always matched the first entry, so **every** workspace's `ask-reminders` ran against `slackApps[0]` — the first-loaded workspace. In production this meant a `neochrome` query (`@sleuth what's open for Client A?`) read the *first* workspace's reminders (no Client A → "No matching tasks") **and** tried to post the reply with that workspace's bot, which isn't a member of neochrome's private channels → `channel_not_found` → the user saw nothing. A multi-tenant isolation break that only fail-closed by accident (the wrong bot couldn't post). Diagnosed on prod via `/debug-mantra`: same neochrome instance's startup/shutdown/🔧-triage posts to the same channel all succeeded — only the ask-reminders path failed, because only it used the global registry. **Fix:** `#RegisterCatalogRegexAliasesAsync` now passes `this.#SlackApp` into `RegisterCatalogRegexAliases`, and the dynamic `ask-reminders` route binds to that per-workspace instance directly (legacy global lookup kept only as a fallback when no app is passed) — bringing it in line with every other route. Regression test ([tests/ask-reminders-workspace-binding.test.js](tests/ask-reminders-workspace-binding.test.js)) proves the route binds to the passed workspace's app, not `slackApps[0]`, under the shared-logger condition that caused the bug. Full suite green (80 suites / 1338 jest + 30 node). [src/chat-module.js](src/chat-module.js), [src/catalog-regex-aliases.js](src/catalog-regex-aliases.js).

## 1.4.220 - 2026-07-15
- **8 new clients/projects in the client mapping + `ask-reminders` gains an assignor (creator) filter.**
  - **Clients:** added Client B, Client D, Sleuth, Client F, HiQS, PDDA, XYZ, and Meetup Groups to [data/static/client-channel-mapping.json](data/static/client-channel-mapping.json) (each with aliases + channel-name/GitHub-repo patterns). **Client B is ordered before Client A** because its repo (`ClientA/ClientB-Analytics`) also matches Client A's `client-a` pattern — first-match wins in `ResolveClientIdentity`, so ordering keeps Client B tasks tagged `client-b`, not `client-a`. Query resolution (`what are the Client B tasks?`) and reminder stamping both verified.
  - **Assignor filter (#380):** `ask-reminders` now filters the user dimension by **sender/creator** (`OriginalSenderID`), not just assignee — enabling *"what tasks did I create yesterday?"*. `reminder-query-engine` gains a `senderIds` filter (AND-combined with `userIds`); `ParseReminderQuery` detects a creation verb (create/made/set up/raised/filed/logged — deliberately excluding "open"/"add"/"assign" to avoid colliding with "what's open for X" and the assignee sense) and routes it: a mention → that creator, first-person (`I`/`we`/`my`/`me`) → the asking user (`selfUserId`), subject-less ("what was created yesterday?") → no user filter (time window only). Assignee remains the default for "tasks @X has". Completion history has no stored sender, so this is active-queue-accurate.
  - Tests: query-core sender + AND-combined filters, `ParseReminderQuery` creator routing (self/mention/passive), handler sender-filter; existing web-api export assertion updated (a `sleuth-app` PR now maps to the new `sleuth` client). Full suite green (79 suites / 1336 jest + 30 node).
  - **Caveat (not auto-applied):** the new mappings tag **newly-created** reminders and legacy `undefined`-clientId reminders (resolved at read time). Reminders already stamped `null` or `client-a` are **kept as-is** by design, so existing Client B/Client D/etc. tasks won't move buckets until re-stamped. A one-time re-stamp is a separate, opt-in step.

## 1.4.219 - 2026-07-15
- **Fix (#378): the 🐛 bug-report reaction filed GitHub issues with mangled titles.** `ChatModule#BuildBugReportIssueTitle` built the issue title from a blind `(text).slice(0, 15)` — 15 raw characters of the reacted Slack message, cut mid-token with markup unresolved. When a *show-reminders digest line* was reacted to, titles came out as `A.) <@U072BF6K4` (#374) and `B.) <https://ne` (#373) — a `<@U…>` mention and a `<https://…>` link sliced mid-token. Now reuses the shared, tested `SlackFormatUtils.ExtractCleanSummary(text, { maxLength: 72 })`, which resolves Slack markup (`<@U…>`→`@id`, `<url|label>`→`label`, `<!date…>`→fallback), strips quote blocks + `Key task(s):` boilerplate, collapses whitespace, and truncates readably. Guards both a blank message and the helper's reminder-specific `'Untitled reminder'` fallback (for input that strips to empty, e.g. a quote-only message) → `'Sleuth bug report'`. Reviewed via a Codex + agy `/consult` (both [Pass] on the approach; the `Untitled reminder` guard + quote-only test came from their [Should]). The full reacted message is still preserved verbatim in the issue body. Tests: markup-resolution regression (no raw `<@`/`<https`/`<!date`, length bounds), blank + quote-only fallbacks; existing 15-char assertion updated. [src/chat-module.js](src/chat-module.js). *(Symptom issues #373/#374 to be renamed/closed.)*

Added one integration test ([tests/reminders-integration.test.js](tests/reminders-integration.test.js)) that force-schedules a long (≥4-sentence) message through the **real** pipeline — analyzer → length-aware synthesis → `#ComposeReminderMessageAsync` (full original in a `>` blockquote) → append `Key task(s):\n• <synthesized>` → store — then renders the stored reminder through the **real** digest builder (`BuildCompactTextForReminder` → `ExtractCompactSummary`, the same path show-reminders uses) and asserts the line reads the synthesized task, never the quoted lead. ## 1.4.217 - 2026-07-15
- **Fix (GH-337): the reminder digest showed the raw quoted-message lead instead of the synthesized task.** For a long message, a reminder keeps the full original in a `>` blockquote **and** appends a synthesized `Key task(s):\n• …` bullet (v1.4.163, `6e4fafd`). `ExtractCompactSummary` (GH-337 Phase 3, `500470a`, 2026-07-03) returned the quoted-original excerpt **before** ever checking the `•` bullet — so whenever both were present, the digest rendered the raw quote lead while the scheduling confirmation had shown the clean task. Prod symptom: *"Get the Woocommerce plugins done"* surfaced in the daily digest as *"com. I'll follow the same method we used before on Client C. minor versions we have already tested…"*. Reordered so the synthesized Key-task bullet wins; the quoted-original excerpt remains the fallback for reminders without a bullet (a quoted line always starts with `>`, so a bare `•` line is only ever the appended task). Regression test added; the existing GH-337 quoted-excerpt tests (quote, no bullet) are unaffected. [src/reminders-display-utils.js](src/reminders-display-utils.js). *(Note: distinct from the 1.4.216 multi-task confirm fix — this is the display-layer bug behind the reported screenshot; the reminder in question was single-message auto-scheduled.)*

## 1.4.216 - 2026-07-15
- **Fix (GH-360): confirming a multi-task proposal stored the raw source message, not the synthesized task.** When a user confirmed a whole-thread task proposal (`:white_check_mark:`), `#HandleConfirmMultiTaskProposalAsync` set each reminder's text to `LiveSourceText || Candidate.title` — the *verbatim source Slack message* the task was extracted from, falling back to the title only if that message vanished. Since the source message essentially always exists, the synthesized title (`Candidate.title` — e.g. "Get the Woocommerce plugins done", exactly what the proposal showed and the user approved) was **discarded on every confirm**, and the full multi-paragraph chat message became `ReminderMessageText`, which the digest's `ExtractCompactSummary` then mangled into an unreadable excerpt (prod: a Client A plugin-update note surfaced as *"com. I'll follow the same method we used before on Client C. minor versions we have already tested…"*). The "edit-before-confirm" re-read that produced this was misconceived — it read the human source message (not an editable proposal field) and pasted it verbatim as the task — so it's removed; the reminder now uses the confirmed `Candidate.title` (WYSIWYG with the proposal). Proposal copy updated to stop promising an edit path that never worked. **This path had no confirm-flow test at all** (extraction fixtures used source text identical to the title, hiding the bug); added a regression test that confirms a proposal whose title differs from a long raw message and asserts the raw text never leaks into the reminder. Verified against production (reminder `fe00d1cc…`). [src/reminders-app-mention-handler.js](src/reminders-app-mention-handler.js).

## 1.4.215 - 2026-07-15
- **Fix (GH-367): `ask-reminders` could crash on a completion with a malformed timestamp.** `AssembleCandidates` emits `timestampMs: NaN` for a completion record whose `completedMs` isn't a number (and for an active reminder with an unparseable `CreatedOn`), and `NaN` slips through `FilterCandidates`' time guard (`NaN < start` / `NaN >= end` are both false). `RenderCitedCandidates` guarded the completed-timestamp render with `typeof === 'number'` — but `typeof NaN === 'number'`, so `new Date(NaN).toISOString()` threw `RangeError: Invalid time value`, taking down the whole command. Now gated on `Number.isFinite` (omits `completedAt:` rather than throwing). Regression tests added. [src/chat-commands/ask-reminders-command.js](src/chat-commands/ask-reminders-command.js).

## 1.4.214 - 2026-07-15
- **GH-367 Phase 2 (export enrichment) + Phase 3 (local "talk to reminders" skill).** Extends the deterministic query core (1.4.213) out of Slack and into a fast, zero-network local surface.
  - **Phase 2a — `clientId` on the reminders export.** `#BuildRebalanceReminderRecord` in [src/web-api.js](src/web-api.js) now surfaces `clientId` on every `format=rebalance` record: it prefers the value stamped at creation (GH-361), and **falls back to resolving it from `client-mapping` at export time** (new `#ResolveExportClientId`) so reminders created before stamping still carry a client — matching what the Slack Q&A path sees. Null (present, backwards-compatible) when no client matches; never throws the export.
  - **Phase 2b — bounded completions export.** New pure, unit-tested [deploy/reminders-export/completions-payload.js](deploy/reminders-export/completions-payload.js) (`BuildCompletionsContent`) turns the on-disk completion history into a bounded (`SLEUTH_COMPLETIONS_WINDOW_DAYS`, default 90d), deterministically-shaped `completions-<workspace>.json` with the same hourly-rounded `exportGeneratedAt` idempotency heartbeat as the active mirror. [deploy/reminders-export/publish-reminders-export.mjs](deploy/reminders-export/publish-reminders-export.mjs) refactored to a reusable `publishFile()` helper and now publishes **both** files (active first; a completions failure never sinks the active mirror). Reads the completed file directly from disk (same no-inbound-access pattern as the memories load). 8 new tests.
  - **Phase 3 — `talk-to-reminders` local skill.** New [skills/talk-to-reminders/](skills/talk-to-reminders/) (`SKILL.md` + `query-reminders.mjs`, symlinked into `~/.claude/skills`). The script reads the published mirror(s) and runs the **same `src/reminder-query-engine.js` core** the Slack command uses — deterministic membership by `--client` / `--user` / `--user-name` / `--keywords` / `--since|--days` (window resolved in the workspace tz), optional `--include-completed` for history — and prints the narrowed, cited candidate set; the model synthesizes the answer, never inventing rows. Zero network, admin-local (full-workspace visibility, no channel-privacy scoping needed off the Slack surface). Client filtering activates once the server republishes the enriched export.
  - Full suite green (**79 suites / 1324 jest + 30 node**); new files `tsc`-clean. See [PROJECT/2-WORKING/GH-367-RICHER-REMINDER-QUERIES.md](PROJECT/2-WORKING/GH-367-RICHER-REMINDER-QUERIES.md).

## 1.4.213 - 2026-07-15
- **`ask-reminders` now filters deterministically before the model, with channel-privacy scoping (GH-367 v1).** Instead of dumping the whole workspace reminder set into the LLM, the command parses the question into a `ReminderQuery` (assignee `@mentions`, client via `client-mapping`, a time window resolved in the workspace timezone), assembles candidates from the active queue + completion history, and **narrows them deterministically — channel-privacy → time → user → client — before any model call**. An empty result short-circuits with a canned "no matching tasks" reply and **no LLM call**; otherwise only the narrowed, id-citing set is handed to the Complex model to phrase. New pure modules [src/reminder-query-engine.js](src/reminder-query-engine.js) (`FilterCandidates` + `ResolveTimeWindow`, workspace-agnostic, no I/O) and [src/reminder-candidates.js](src/reminder-candidates.js) (`AssembleCandidates`, active+completed → normalized shape, completed-wins de-dupe). **Channel-privacy is fail-closed**: private/DM reminders are excluded from a public-channel query (the command's own channel is always allowed; an unknown channel is treated as private) via the new `SlackApp.IsChannelPrivateAsync`. Topic (e.g. "plugin upgrades") is left to the model over the narrowed set, not a deterministic keyword gate. Plan refined by a Codex + agy `/consult` plan-QA adjudicated to AGENTS.md; the candidate assembler (P1.3) was built via the xyz marathon harness. Tests: query-engine (14), assembler (5), ask-reminders wiring incl. private-channel exclusion + empty short-circuit; full suite green (78 suites / 1316 + 30 node). Fixes a latent shape bug in the old `ComposeContext` (it read `.Id`/`.ReminderText`, not the real `ReminderID`/`ReminderMessageText`). See [PROJECT/2-WORKING/GH-367-RICHER-REMINDER-QUERIES.md](PROJECT/2-WORKING/GH-367-RICHER-REMINDER-QUERIES.md).

## 1.4.212 - 2026-07-14
- **New `code-task` relay — ask Sleuth on Slack to change its own codebase via Claude Code Cloud (admin-only, DEFAULT OFF).** The reverse of the snapshot relay (which forwards Claude Code desktop → Slack): an admin mentions `@Sleuth code-task <freeform request>`, Sleuth runs a **Claude synthesis** pass to clean the request into a structured TaskSpec, then dispatches it to **Claude Code Cloud**, which opens a PR; the session/PR link is posted back into the originating Slack thread. Supports two trigger paths via `CODE_TASK_TRIGGER_MODE` (`desktop` | `github` | `both`): the *desktop* path drops a `code-tasks/<id>.md` file into the shared private repo for a laptop watcher to run `claude --remote`; the *github-native* path opens a labeled issue on the target repo for a Claude Code GitHub trigger to pick up. New files:
  - **[src/code-task-synthesis.js](src/code-task-synthesis.js)** — pure synthesis + rendering: loads the new AI assets, calls `WorkspaceAI.ProcessMessageWithJsonResponseAsync` pinned to a Claude model (`CODE_TASK_SYNTH_MODEL`, default `claude-sonnet-4-6`), normalizes the result into a `TaskSpec`, and renders the task markdown / `claude --remote` prompt / shared metadata header used to route feedback.
  - **[src/code-task-relay-module.js](src/code-task-relay-module.js)** — GitHub transport (Contents API PUT + issue POST), `ResolveCodeTaskConfig`, `DispatchCodeTaskAsync`, and the feedback poller (lists `code-tasks/acks/`, posts session/PR links to the origin thread). Lifecycle (first-run seed, restart resume, exactly-once, seen-set at `data/runtime/code-task-relay-seen.json`) mirrors [src/snapshot-relay-module.js](src/snapshot-relay-module.js). Gated identically: workspace `neochrome` + `CODE_TASK_RELAY_ENABLED` + `SLEUTH_RAG_GITHUB_PAT`.
  - **[src/chat-commands/code-task-command.js](src/chat-commands/code-task-command.js)** — the admin-gated handler (synthesize → dispatch → confirm), wired as the `code-task` route in [src/chat-module.js](src/chat-module.js); catalog entry added to [data/static/ai/command-catalog.json](data/static/ai/command-catalog.json) as **admin/high-risk, `CanExecuteWithIfl: false`** so `rmm ifl` can only suggest it, never auto-run it.
  - **[tools/code-task-cloud-trigger.js](tools/code-task-cloud-trigger.js)** — the dependency-free desktop watcher (opt-in via `code_task_cloud=true`, reuses the snapshot drop dir) that runs `claude --remote` and writes acks back; **[RELAY-SYSTEM/2026-06-20/code-task-relay-design.md](RELAY-SYSTEM/2026-06-20/code-task-relay-design.md)** — the design doc.
  - New AI assets [data/static/ai/code-task-synthesis-instructions.md](data/static/ai/code-task-synthesis-instructions.md) + [code-task-synthesis-schema.json](data/static/ai/code-task-synthesis-schema.json) (registered in `validate:ai`). Startup/shutdown wired in [src/app.js](src/app.js). Safety: the cloud session opens a PR from a `code-task/*` branch and never pushes to `main`/`development`. Tests: +22 across [tests/code-task-relay-module.test.js](tests/code-task-relay-module.test.js) and [tests/code-task-synthesis.test.js](tests/code-task-synthesis.test.js); full suite **1146/1146** green, `validate:ai` + `validate:commands` pass, new files `tsc`-clean.

## 2026-07-13
- **"Make Sleuth smart" marathon (GH-360/361/362) shipped — all 6 lanes approved, `marathon.complete`.** The full chain landed: **p1** (#361A) client/project identity stamped at creation + operator-defaults config block; **p2** (#361B) `ask-reminders` — conversational Q&A over live authoritative data; **p3** (#360) whole-thread → N proposed tasks, propose-and-confirm, Complex-tier model; **p4** (#361C) durable deterministic-first client/project map with zero-LLM-call reuse; **p5** (#362P1) deterministic proactive digest signals (gone-quiet, deadline-window collision, aging-without-owner); **p6** the end-to-end net-improvement acceptance test ([tests/marathon-360-361-362-e2e.test.js](tests/marathon-360-361-362-e2e.test.js)), which passed all 5 scripted steps confirming the phases actually hand data to each other (not just that each ships in isolation). p3 needed a widened artifact scope (the identity-wiring fix required touching `src/reminders-module.js`, outside its original allowlist) and extra review rounds; p5 needed extra rounds to close out a timezone-parsing bug and a diverged duplicate implementation. Full suite green (73 suites / 1263 tests + 30 node:test cases). Merged into `main` from `marathon/smart-sleuth-360-361-362-2026-07-10`.
- **Synced `main` and `development`.** `development` had accumulated 38 commits since the last sync (PR #359) — GH-358 (admin bug-report reaction, both phases), GH-338/348/349/351/352 ledger/doc confirmation, a `.gitignore` update, and a PDDA hygiene fix — merged into `main` via PR #363. Resolved 7 doc-only conflicts (`ROADMAP.md`, `MYRIAD-WEEK-2026-07-06.md`, and the five `GH-338/348/349/351/352` completed-docs) where both branches had independently recorded the same shipped status; combined `development`'s more detailed text with `main`'s already-correct `PROJECT/3-COMPLETED/` paths.

## 1.4.211 - 2026-07-06
- **Fix: flaky `web-api-workspace.test.js` fixed-port collision (GH-351, backfilled 2026-07-09; shipped via marathon automation on 2026-07-06, commit `578b3e7`).** The suite bound a real Express server on a hardcoded port (`19877`); parallel jest workers or a leftover process holding that port threw `EADDRINUSE` and failed 3 cases intermittently, unrelated to the code under test. Now binds an ephemeral OS-assigned port (`TestPort = 0`) and reads the actual bound port via `API.PortNumber` for its requests. Test-only; no product behavior change. See [PROJECT/2-WORKING/GH-351-FLAKY-PORT-TEST.md](PROJECT/2-WORKING/GH-351-FLAKY-PORT-TEST.md).
- **New: automatic hyphen/space command normalization for argument-invariant commands (GH-349, backfilled 2026-07-09; shipped via marathon automation on 2026-07-07, commit `d91f7ed`).** Previously only 4 command families hand-tolerated both hyphenated and spaced forms; every other zero-argument command had to match its `CommandRouter` pattern exactly or fall through to the LLM-based `rmm` resolver for a plain formatting mismatch. [src/command-intent-resolver.js](src/command-intent-resolver.js) now auto-derives hyphen↔space tolerance from `BuildCanonicalCommand`'s existing canonical-string switch, using a `Proxy`-sentinel argument-invariance check so optionally argument-bearing commands (`search-projects`, `test-github-sync`) are correctly excluded from normalization. Redundant hand-written `show-channel-model`/`clear-channel-model` regexes removed from [data/static/ai/command-normalization.json](data/static/ai/command-normalization.json) now that the generic mechanism subsumes them. See [PROJECT/2-WORKING/GH-349-HYPHEN-COMMAND-NORMALIZATION.md](PROJECT/2-WORKING/GH-349-HYPHEN-COMMAND-NORMALIZATION.md).
- **New: `scripts/baseline-import.js` — P3 baseline-import for pre-ledger reminders (GH-355).** Unblocks the P3 Phase 2 `summarize-week` read-path cutover. Reminders created before the event ledger was born (2026-06-17) have no `ReminderCreated` event, so the projection fold yielded `null` assignee/sourceChannel for them — a shadow-diff mismatch that blocked flipping `SUMMARIZE_WEEK_COMPLETED_SOURCE=projection`. The new one-shot, **idempotent** import scans BOTH `data/runtime/reminders/<ws>_reminders.json` (active) AND `<ws>_completed.json` (completed), skips reminders already seeded by a `ReminderCreated` or `BaselineReminderImported` event, and appends a `BaselineReminderImported` event for the rest — carrying every projection-critical field (`assigneeId`/`sourceChannelId`/`targetChannelId`/text/`dueAt`/state) plus the Phase-4 rebuild fields (`originalSenderId`/`originalMessageId`/`originalThreadTs`/`originalChannelName`/`ignoreSnooze`), and preserving the reminder's original `CreatedOn` as the event `ts` (per-workspace monotonicity). Handles both PascalCase (active store) and camelCase (completed store) field names. CLI: `--repo-root`, `--workspace`, `--write` (default dry-run), `--json`. Also fixes the `BaselineReminderImported` case in [deploy/reminders-export/events-projection.js](deploy/reminders-export/events-projection.js) to map `githubUrls` (previously dropped, unlike the `ReminderCreated` case). Validated against real prod `neochrome` data: the summarize-week shadow-diff for two post-floor weeks went from 11 null-field mismatches to **0** (only the documented ±1ms `completedMs` divergence remains). Fixture-backed tests in [tests/baseline-import.test.js](tests/baseline-import.test.js) + [tests/events-projection.test.js](tests/events-projection.test.js); full suite green (1198). Built via marathon swarm (codex builder + agy reviewer). **Not yet a cutover** — the read path stays on `CompletionStore`; running the import on prod + flipping the flag is a separate, human-gated step. See [PROJECT/1-INBOX/GH-355-P3-BASELINE-IMPORT.md](PROJECT/1-INBOX/GH-355-P3-BASELINE-IMPORT.md).

## 1.4.210 - 2026-07-06
- **Fix: snapshot relay enable flag is now redeploy-durable (`SNAPSHOT_RELAY_WORKSPACES`).** The P2 snapshot→Slack relay was gated only on the per-workspace `SNAPSHOT_RELAY_ENABLED` field in `data/runtime/workspaces/<ws>.json` — a file a redeploy/restore can overwrite, silently reverting the relay to OFF (observed 2026-07-06: the flag set live on 2026-06-18 had reverted to `false`). [src/snapshot-relay-module.js](src/snapshot-relay-module.js) now honors a durable env allowlist **`SNAPSHOT_RELAY_WORKSPACES`** (comma-separated workspace names) as the canonical enable path — env lives in the server's `.env.runtime`, which survives redeploys — while keeping the legacy `neochrome` + `SNAPSHOT_RELAY_ENABLED===true` config path for backward compatibility (`enabled = envAllowlist.includes(workspace) || (workspace==='neochrome' && WorkspaceInfo.SNAPSHOT_RELAY_ENABLED===true)`). No behavior change when the env is unset. [tests/snapshot-relay-module.test.js](tests/snapshot-relay-module.test.js) adds 3 cases (env enables despite flag=false; env excluding the workspace stays disabled; legacy flag path still works when the env is unset). Full suite green (1195).

## 1.4.209 - 2026-07-03
- **New: show-me renders ranked reminders as individual reactable, permalinked messages (GH-338, backfilled 2026-07-09; shipped same-day in commit `2c8ee7d`, missed at the time).** `show-me` previously posted one consolidated AI-text blob — items couldn't be checkmark-closed and had no link to the source task. Now reuses the same shared renderer as `show reminders for @user`: the AI returns an ordered `[{ ReminderID, rationale }]` list instead of prose, validated 1:1 against the input set with a deterministic deadline-specificity fallback on any hallucinated/dropped/duplicate ID; top 5 (named constant), bucketed by due date (today → upcoming → older), each posted via `PostBucketedReminderSectionsAsync` as its own reactable/permalinked message with the AI rationale appended as a sub-line. Backward compatible — the five other `PostBucketedReminderSectionsAsync` callers pass no annotation map and render byte-for-byte unchanged. Discovered already-implemented-but-undocumented during a 2026-07-09 marathon run; added 2 regression tests (hallucinated-ID fallback, per-reminder reactable metadata in ranked order) rather than duplicating working code. See [PROJECT/2-WORKING/GH-338-SHOWME-COMMAND.md](PROJECT/2-WORKING/GH-338-SHOWME-COMMAND.md).
- **New: GH-337 Phase 3 — compact digest excerpting (backfilled 2026-07-09; shipped same-day in commit `500470a`, missed at the time).** Daily digest / show-reminders / rebalance export now render a trimmed excerpt of the quoted original message instead of the raw blob: first ~3 sentences, greeting/signoff edge lines trimmed (#29), a minimum-useful-length floor so terse messages don't collapse to junk like "Hi. Thanks." (#193), and a trailing ellipsis only when actually truncated (#3). Excerpting lives in the shared display helper ([src/reminders-display-utils.js](src/reminders-display-utils.js): `ExtractQuotedOriginalExcerpt` + helpers) so digest, show-reminders, and rebalance export stay consistent; legacy reminders with no quoted block keep the old bullet/first-line rule. 3 Phase-3 tests added to `tests/reminders-display-utils.test.js`; 23/23 green, tsc-clean. Completes the GH-337 epic (all 4 phases now shipped); discovered undocumented via a marathon-plan preflight sweep. See [PROJECT/1-INBOX/GH-337-REMINDER-TASK-TEXT.md](PROJECT/1-INBOX/GH-337-REMINDER-TASK-TEXT.md).
- **Fix: `ask-self` ingest's architecture-summary generator had two silent bugs (GH-347).** Investigating whether the newly-installed `codebase-memory-mcp` graph index could improve `ask-self` (`src/rag/`) surfaced two gaps in `buildArchitectureSummary()` ([src/rag/ingest.mjs](src/rag/ingest.mjs)): (1) the module inventory only scanned top-level `src/*.js` plus a hardcoded `src/rag/` special case, silently missing `src/chat-commands/` (28 files — the single largest source package), `src/ai-providers/`, and `src/plugins/`; (2) the command feature-map regex-matched `if (/pattern/.test(...))` branches in `chat-module.js`, an extraction pattern that predates the CommandRouter/catalog refactor and matched **zero** real command branches today. Fixed with a recursive `collectSourceFiles()` walk (now finds all 84 `.js`/`.mjs` files across every subdirectory) and by reading `data/static/ai/command-catalog.json` directly (55 commands with real `Id`/`Permission`/`Description`) plus a new real HTTP route extraction from `src/web-api.js` (16 routes, with a `req.get('host')` false-positive caught and filtered). No change to the live query path (`src/rag/index.js`) or runtime behavior — offline ingest tooling only. Live graph-tool calls at ingest time turned out to be infeasible (`codebase-memory-mcp` is only reachable as an MCP tool inside an agent session, not importable by an offline script), so this uses graph-informed fixes rather than a live dependency; see [PROJECT/3-COMPLETED/GH-347-ASK-SELF-GRAPH-INTEGRATION.md](PROJECT/3-COMPLETED/GH-347-ASK-SELF-GRAPH-INTEGRATION.md) for the full research and two deferred follow-ups.

Handler: [src/chat-commands/send-to-github-command.js](src/chat-commands/send-to-github-command.js); routed in [src/reminders-app-mention-handler.js](src/reminders-app-mention-handler.js) alongside the other GitHub admin commands. Catalog entry + tests in [tests/send-to-github-command.test.js](tests/send-to-github-command.test.js).

## 1.4.205 - 2026-06-24
- **Reminder task-text quality — FYI synthesis + unified display source (GH-337, Phases 1/2/4).** A long FYI/status note used to schedule a reminder whose "Key task(s)" bullet was the *entire message verbatim*, while the `:wrench:` triage view of the same message showed the analyzer's clean extracted task — the two disagreed because the auto-scheduled path ran a mutating override that overwrote every candidate's `reminder_message` with the whole normalized message, and the triage path didn't.
  - **Phase 1 — unified task-text source.** Deleted the mutating `reminder_message` override in [src/reminders-module.js](src/reminders-module.js). The verbatim-vs-synthesized choice now lives entirely in `#SelectReminderTaskText`, the single chokepoint that the digest "Key task(s)" bullet, the "Tasks for …" feedback list, and the `:wrench:` triage candidate list all funnel through (passing the same normalized original text). Same message → same bullet on every surface, by construction. With synthesis off this reproduces the prior output byte-for-byte.
  - **Phase 2 — two-segment, length-aware synthesis settings.** Replaced the single `REMINDER_TEXT_SYNTHESIS` master flag with two independent segment flags in [src/reminders-ai-pipeline.js](src/reminders-ai-pipeline.js), routed by sentence count (`LONG_MESSAGE_SENTENCE_THRESHOLD = 4`): **`REMINDER_TEXT_SYNTHESIS_NORMAL`** (< 4 sentences, default **OFF** — keep everyday tasks verbatim) and **`REMINDER_TEXT_SYNTHESIS_LONG`** (≥ 4 sentences, default **ON** — collapse long FYI noise to the buried task). The synthesized text reuses the analyzer's existing brief (`reminder_message`, with the existing over-compression fallback to the quoted actionable span) — **no new LLM call**. The verbatim original is still preserved in the reminder blockquote regardless of setting. `REMINDER_TEXT_SYNTHESIS`, if explicitly set, is honored as a deprecated master override of both segments for back-compat. The force-schedule manual-task LLM call is now gated per-segment, so it's skipped (not wasted) on short messages.
  - **Phase 4 — telemetry to tune the threshold.** The existing `reminder display source:` log line now also emits `msg_len`, `sentences`, `segment`, `synthesis`, and a derived `actionable_span_ratio` — one structured line per scheduled message, no raw message text — so the 4-sentence split can be confirmed against real data before defaults are locked.
  - **Deferred:** Phase 3 (daily-digest excerpting / prefix-suffix trim / min-length floor, folding #193/#3/#29) is a separable display-layer change to the shared renderer in [src/reminders-display-utils.js](src/reminders-display-utils.js) (also used by show-me + the rebalance export) and lands on its own. Tests: rewrote the `IsTextSynthesisEnabled` suite into a `text-synthesis settings (GH-337 Phase 2)` block in [tests/reminders-ai-pipeline.test.js](tests/reminders-ai-pipeline.test.js) covering the master override, per-segment defaults, `CountSentences`, `IsTaskSynthesisEnabledForText` routing/boundary, and `DescribeSynthesisRouting`; existing verbatim integration tests (all short/normal-segment messages) stay green.
  - **QA:** ran an automated `/relay-xyz` Codex review of the change ([relay-system/2026-06-24/gh-337-task-text.md](relay-system/2026-06-24/gh-337-task-text.md)). Codex passed the routing/`actionable_span_ratio`/legacy-override/fallback logic and found two divergences in the `:wrench:` triage **diagnostic** surface (not the shipping digest/feedback path): a triage-vs-scheduled candidate-dedup gap (declined — triage deliberately shows raw candidates) and an enriched-reply triage source mismatch (deferred → #339). Added two force-schedule integration tests exercising `REMINDER_TEXT_SYNTHESIS_LONG` in both directions (a ≥4-sentence message synthesizes by default; `_LONG=off` keeps it verbatim with no LLM call). The long-unpunctuated-run-on heuristic and the enriched-reply triage parity are tracked in #339.

## 1.4.204 - 2026-06-23
- **Fix: asking *about* the `remember above` command no longer silently runs it.** The route matcher in [src/reminders-app-mention-handler.js](src/reminders-app-mention-handler.js) was unanchored (`/\bremember\s+above\b/i`), so any message merely *containing* the phrase fired a real thread-memory write — e.g. `@Sleuth ask-self what is and how does the remember above command work?` returned "Remembered. N messages…" and (because the reminders handler runs before ChatModule, [src/app.js](src/app.js#L306)) shadowed the anchored `ask-self` route entirely. Replaced it with `REMEMBER_ABOVE_PATTERN` — `/^\s*(?:<@[^>]+>\s*)?(?:please\s+)?remember\s+above\b[\s.!?]*$/i` — which requires the message to *be* the standalone command (after the bot mention + optional "please"), not contain it. Anchored both ends on purpose (not just the tail, like `TASK_ABOVE_SHORTHAND_PATTERN`) because a false positive here is a write, not a suggestion: `what is remember above?` and `ask-self explain remember above` no longer match. New regression suite [tests/remember-above-routing.test.js](tests/remember-above-routing.test.js) (14 cases) locks both directions; the 24 `tests/thread-memory.test.js` handler tests are unchanged and green.

## 1.4.203 - 2026-06-22
- **`remember above` confirmation now names the command that ran.** The success reply in [src/chat-commands/remember-above-command.js](src/chat-commands/remember-above-command.js) changed from `Remembered. N messages, M participants.` to `Remembered. N messages, M participants. (via \`remember above\`)`. Echoing the matched command makes it unambiguous that a real deterministic write happened (not a hallucinated chat reply) and which command triggered it — directly addressing a case where an `ask-self …` question that merely *contained* the phrase "remember above" was silently captured by the command. (Routing root-cause — the unanchored `/\bremember\s+above\b/i` matcher in [src/reminders-app-mention-handler.js](src/reminders-app-mention-handler.js#L340) — is noted separately and not changed here.) Updated the two affected assertions in [tests/thread-memory.test.js](tests/thread-memory.test.js).

## 1.4.202 - 2026-06-21
- **New: Sleuth MCP connector for Claude Desktop** ([mcp/](mcp/)). A standalone [Model Context Protocol](https://modelcontextprotocol.io) server, spoken over stdio, that lets Claude Desktop (or any MCP client) **query Sleuth reminders** and run **live read-only queries against the connected Slack workspace**. Separate process from the app — it never opens a Slack socket and never mutates state.
  - **Reminder tools** read straight from the JSON Sleuth owns (`data/runtime/reminders/<workspace>_{reminders,completed}.json`) rather than constructing a `RemindersModule` (which would need a live socket): `list_workspaces`, `query_reminders` (filter by state/assignee/channel/text/due-window), `get_reminder`, `list_completed_reminders` (completion history window), `reminder_stats`.
  - **Live Slack tools** use the workspace `LIVE_TOKEN` via a standalone `@slack/web-api` `WebClient` with a bounded timeout + capped retries (fail fast, never hang the client): `slack_list_channels`, `slack_channel_history`, `slack_thread_replies`, `slack_list_users`, `slack_user_info`. All read-only; full-text message search is intentionally omitted (needs a user token Sleuth doesn't hold).
  - **Consistency with the app:** reuses `src/workspaces.js` for path conventions, name parsing, and the validating loader. Workspace/reminder roots both honor a `SLEUTH_DATA_DIR` override so the connector can run off-checkout (e.g. a server deploy), defaulting to `<repo>/data/runtime`. Implemented in ESM under `mcp/` (outside `src/`, so the `tsc` include is unaffected).
  - **Tooling:** new deps `@modelcontextprotocol/sdk` + explicit `@slack/web-api`; new scripts `npm run mcp` (launch) and `npm run mcp:smoke` (self-contained stdio handshake + reminder-tool assertions against throwaway fixtures — 10/10 passing). Setup + Claude Desktop `claude_desktop_config.json` snippet in [mcp/README.md](mcp/README.md). `npm run build`, `validate:fsm`, `validate:commands`, `validate:ai` all clean; no existing app behavior changed.

## 1.4.199 - 2026-06-20
- **Phase 0 of the command near-miss plan — a no-LLM "near-miss probe" to measure the dead-end rate before building the AI recovery tier.** When an `@`-mention matches no command and every deterministic route + web-search auto-route declines, [src/chat-module.js](src/chat-module.js) now emits one best-effort log line at the generic-chat fallthrough seam (`#EmitNearMissProbeAsync`): workspace, channel, user, the top deterministic candidate id, and its `ScoreCommandEntry` score — the cheap signal that separates "wrong syntax for a real command" (high score) from genuine off-topic chat (~0). **Metadata only (no raw message text)**, no model call, no reply, no behavior change; fire-and-forget and `.catch`-guarded so a probe failure can never affect the chat fallthrough. Temporary measurement scaffolding — disable by reverting, not via a permanent flag.
- **New `RetrieveScoredCandidates(text)` in [src/command-intent-resolver.js](src/command-intent-resolver.js)** — surfaces the deterministic `{ Entry, Score }` pairs that `RetrieveCandidateCommands` computes but discards, loading catalog assets on demand. Used by the probe now and the planned Phase 2-lite deterministic "did you mean?" tier. Test in [tests/command-intent-resolver.test.js](tests/command-intent-resolver.test.js): the score is exposed, sorted high→low, and a wrong-syntax near-miss ("switch model to gpt-5") ranks well above genuine chat.
- **Rationale (worth-it review, [PROJECT/1-INBOX/COMMAND-NEAR-MISS-AI-FALLBACK.md](PROJECT/1-INBOX/COMMAND-NEAR-MISS-AI-FALLBACK.md)):** real usage is "occasional, a few users" with no miss-logging today, so the LLM tier's benefit was asserted, not measured. Phase 0 measures it for ~the cost of a log line; the count gates whether Phase 2-full / 3 / 4 earn their carrying cost. `/ponytail`-reviewed before commit (cut a kill-switch flag, a preview mode, and 4 speculative log fields — 60 lines → 18). Suites green (93/93 across resolver + chat-module + integration + rmm-regression); `tsc` clean for the touched files (6 pre-existing errors in the unrelated `snapshot-relay-module.js` remain).

## 1.4.198 - 2026-06-17
- **Quieted the recurring `not_in_channel` startup-notification error.** When the bot isn't a member of a workspace's reminder channel, `PostStartupNotificationAsync` ([src/app.js](src/app.js)) caught `not_in_channel` / `channel_not_found` and logged it at **ERROR** on every startup (19+ occurrences in prod for the `planning` workspace), raising false alerts. These are configuration conditions, not faults — they now log an actionable **WARN** ("the bot is not in #<channel>; invite it to enable startup notifications") while genuinely unexpected failures stay ERROR. Behavior is otherwise unchanged. NOTE: the actual resolution is operational — invite the bot to the channel (`C000EXAMPLE7` in `planning`); this change only stops the noise.

The projection path is wrapped so any error falls back to the authoritative store, and the "Still open" section continues to read the live pending queue (only completions are switched, since that's what the shadow-diff validates). New handler test proves the source switches under the flag (store row deliberately differs and must not appear); `tsc` clean; suite 1118/1118 + node:test 30/30.
- **Test: close the Phase 1 deferred gap — `ReminderSnoozed` ledger emission.** Added a fake-timer behavioral test to [tests/reminders-event-emission.test.js](tests/reminders-event-emission.test.js) that drives the natural (non-forced) 30s check cycle on a configured snooze day, so the snooze-day gate (`overdue → snoozed → advanced → scheduled`) fires, and asserts a `ReminderSnoozed` event (`payload.by`, `payload.until`) lands in the non-authoritative ledger. Force mode bypasses snooze, so this path was previously untested (deferred at Phase 1 per Reviewer). The emission suite now covers all five lifecycle types — Created/Scheduled/Completed/Snoozed/Cancelled — 4/4 green. Restores real timers before polling the ledger (the poll helper relies on `Date.now()`/`setTimeout`, frozen under fake timers).

## 1.4.197 - 2026-06-16
- **P3 Phase 2 — summarize-week projection + shadow-diff harness.** Additive only; no existing tracked behavior changed. Three new files:
  - **New [src/summarize-week-projection.js](src/summarize-week-projection.js)** — pure `summarizeWeekFromEvents(events, { weekStartMs, weekEndMs })` returning `{ completed: WeekCompletedRow[], open: ReminderRecord[] }`. Window is `[weekStartMs, weekEndMs)` (inclusive start / exclusive end), matching `CompletionStore.GetCompletedBetween` semantics. `completed` rows are field-compatible with `CompletionRecord` (`assigneeID`, `sourceChannelID`, `dueDate`, `completedMs`), sorted oldest-first. `open` is everything not cancelled or completed. No I/O, no Slack/GitHub calls — a pure fold over the event log via the existing `FoldReminders` from `events-projection.js`.
  - **New [scripts/summarize-week-shadow-diff.js](scripts/summarize-week-shadow-diff.js)** — dev CLI + exportable `computeShadowDiff(projected, stored)`. Loads events JSONL + CompletionStore JSON from disk, applies the same `[start, end)` window, runs both the projection path and the stored path, reports mismatches (missing / extra / differing rows by `reminderId`) as structured JSON. Exit 0 = clean diff; exit 2 = mismatch found (enables CI-style usage). Known expected divergence: `completedMs` may differ by milliseconds between `Date.now()` (CompletionStore call site) and `Date.parse(payload.completedAt)` (set instants earlier); a small `completedMs` diff is informational, not a data-loss indicator.
  - **New [tests/summarize-week-projection.test.js](tests/summarize-week-projection.test.js)** (`node:test`, 13/13) — covers: completed-in-window selection (inclusive start / exclusive end), completed row field mapping to `CompletionRecord` shape, inclusive start boundary, exclusive end boundary, open-set derivation (created/scheduled minus completed/cancelled), oldest-first sort, determinism, `computeShadowDiff` clean diff, missing row, extra row, field mismatch with both values, CLI structured-JSON output, CLI exit-2 on planted mismatch.
- **JSDoc typedef fix in [deploy/reminders-export/events-projection.js](deploy/reminders-export/events-projection.js)** — added `@typedef {Object} ReminderRecord` with all 18 fields; annotated `MakeReminderRecord`, `ApplyEvent`, `FoldReminders`, `foldActive`, `foldCompleted` with typed signatures; changed `Event.payload` from `{Object}` to `{any}`. Resolves all pre-existing `tsc` errors in this file that were introduced with Phase 1. `tsc` now clean.
- **CI fix: the three `node:test` suites no longer break `npm test`.** Jest's `testMatch` (`**/tests/**/*.test.js`) globbed in the Phase 1/2 `node --test` suites ([tests/event-store.test.js](tests/event-store.test.js), [tests/events-projection.test.js](tests/events-projection.test.js), [tests/summarize-week-projection.test.js](tests/summarize-week-projection.test.js)) and reported each as "suite failed to run" — red CI on `npm test`. Added `testPathIgnorePatterns` for those three files and a `test:node` script; `npm test` now runs Jest **then** `node --test` (30/30) so both runners gate CI. No source change.
- Next step (supervised, human-gated): run `summarize-week-shadow-diff` against a real calendar week of workspace data; if the diff is clean, proceed to the cutover of `#HandleSummarizeWeekAsync` to read from the projection. Phase 3/5 (authority flips) are behind the stop-and-re-decide gate.

## 1.4.196 - 2026-06-15

The reminder FSM now emits lifecycle events into the append-only ledger as a **best-effort side log**: emitted AFTER the in-memory mutation (mutate-first leads), fire-and-forget, never awaited, wrapped so an `append()` failure can **never block or fail a reminder transition** (no fsync; the log may lag/be lossy). Five additive edits in [src/reminders-module.js](src/reminders-module.js): `#TransitionReminderState` emits `ReminderScheduled`/`ReminderCompleted`/`ReminderSnoozed`/`ReminderCancelled` via a new `#EmitTransitionEvent` + `#EmitLifecycleEvent` helper (mirrors the `#RecordCompletion` fire-and-forget idiom — no-op before store init, try/catch + rejection handler), and `#MakeScheduledReminder` emits `ReminderCreated` **plus a paired `ReminderScheduled`** (guarded on `ShouldPostOn`, `via:'created'`) so a projection recovers a new reminder's initial scheduled state + due date from the log alone. Store created in `StartAsync` under `data/runtime/events/<workspace>_events.jsonl`; nothing reads it back yet (no boot dependency).
- **Built + reviewed via an automated cross-model relay** (`relay-system/2026-06-15/`): Claude Producer ⇄ **Codex** Reviewer, driven headlessly. The independent Codex review caught a real Blocker the same-model author had rationalized away — a born-`Scheduled` reminder emitting only `ReminderCreated` (no due date on replay) — which is the paired-`ReminderScheduled` fix above. Closed **Approved** at Round 2.

## 1.4.195 - 2026-06-15
- **P3 Phase 1 (NON-authoritative counter-view) — first production code: the EventStore + projection halves, unwired.** Per the [P3 plan](PROJECT/2-WORKING/P3-EVENT-SOURCED-CORE.md) Phase 1 counter-view (resume scope: non-authoritative side ledger, mutate-first stays, no fsync, no FSM wiring yet), this lands the two independently-testable, additive halves with **zero change to existing runtime behavior**:
  - **New [src/event-store.js](src/event-store.js)** — non-authoritative append-only event store writing `<workspace>_events.jsonl`. `createEventStore({ rootDir })`; `append(workspace, event)` is best-effort and **never rejects** (resolves `{ok:false, error}` on failure so a lagging/failed append can never block or fail a reminder transition), serializes per-workspace via the [src/completion-store.js](src/completion-store.js) write-chain idiom generalized to a `Map<workspace, Promise>`, auto-assigns `id`/`v`/`ts` when absent, validates against the closed event enum + required payload keys before writing (writes nothing on invalid shape), and `readAll(workspace)` is corrupt-tail-tolerant. No `fsync` (Phase 1 scope).
  - **New [deploy/reminders-export/events-projection.js](deploy/reminders-export/events-projection.js)** — pure deterministic `foldToRebalanceShape(events, opts)` (plus `foldActive`/`foldCompleted`), folding the event log into the rebalance export envelope (shape matched to the live `?format=rebalance` view); unknown event types are skipped with a warning, never thrown.
  - **New [scripts/replay.js](scripts/replay.js)** — read-only dev CLI: `node scripts/replay.js <events.jsonl> [--view rebalance|active|completed]` folds a log to a view as JSON (the `replay` inspector the plan calls for).
- **Built via the `xyz`/`tick` multi-agent coordinator** as a two-lane parallel build (event-store lane ∥ projection+replay lane), each lane coded to a pinned event-schema contract, 90% work-bounded concurrency, zero file collisions. The coupled piece — emitting events at `#TransitionReminderState` in [src/reminders-module.js](src/reminders-module.js) — was deliberately deferred (can't be lane-split; next step via a producer/reviewer relay).
- Tests: +2 new suites — `node --test tests/event-store.test.js` (9/9: append success, per-workspace ordering/isolation, invalid-event rejection, append-failure-non-fatal, corrupt-tail tolerance) and `node --test tests/events-projection.test.js` (8/8: fold determinism, completed-in-window, rebalance shape, unknown-type tolerance, replay CLI smoke). Additive new files only; no existing tracked file modified; no runtime behavior change.
## 1.4.194 - 2026-06-16
- **Fix: a single scheduled reminder rendered its task as N identical bullets.** When the GPT analyzer extracted multiple reminder candidates that all shared one `scheduling_trigger` (the normal shape for a single multi-item message — e.g. "WEEK & PROJECT items: 1.) … 2.) … all due Friday at 3 PM"), the confirmation message showed the **same task repeated once per candidate** — "1 Slack reminder has been scheduled" followed by 3 byte-identical bullets. Only one reminder was ever queued (the header count was correct); the duplication was purely in the rendered bullet list. Root cause: with AI title synthesis OFF (the default — `REMINDER_TEXT_SYNTHESIS` unset), the display-text override in [src/reminders-module.js](src/reminders-module.js) rewrites **every** candidate's `reminder_message` to the identical original message, so the per-candidate bullet loops in both the "Key task(s)" reminder body and the "Tasks for …" feedback message emitted the same line N times.
- **Dedupe candidates on their rendered identity.** Immediately after the display-text override, `AnalysisResult.reminders` is now filtered to unique `scheduling_trigger` + displayed-task-text pairs, so each distinct bullet appears exactly once across the queued reminder text and the confirmation message. No-op when synthesis is ON and candidates carry distinct titles; correctly collapses genuine duplicate extractions either way. The trigger-grouping, date extraction, dedup, and queueing downstream are unchanged — still one reminder per trigger group.
- Tests: +1 regression in [tests/reminders-integration.test.js](tests/reminders-integration.test.js) — three candidates sharing one trigger with synthesis OFF now produce a single confirmation bullet and one persisted reminder (verified failing pre-fix with `Received: 3`). Reminder suites green (86/86 across integration, module, and AI-pipeline).

## 1.4.193 - 2026-06-13
- **Version + changelog catch-up for the recall keyword-leg behavior change.** Commit `2de35ee` switched the hybrid recall keyword leg to match against the **channel name** instead of the message text ([src/thread-memory.js](src/thread-memory.js)) — a user-visible recall behavior change that shipped without a `package.json` bump. Formalizing it here per [AGENTS.md](AGENTS.md) versioning rules. Rationale: the channel a thread was captured in is the strongest topical signal for short entity queries (e.g. "Client B" surfaces every thread in `#client-a-client-b`, even ones whose text never spells the entity out, while NOT dragging in a `#…-devops` thread that merely name-drops an `ClientB-Analytics` repo).
- **Strip a leading `#` from the keyword query.** A Slack-style `#client-a-client-b` query previously missed the stored channel name `client-a-client-b` because `instr()` is a literal substring match and the extra leading `#` is not present in the stored name. The keyword leg now strips a single leading `#` before matching ([src/thread-memory.js](src/thread-memory.js)).
- **Test fix: the workspace-scoping channel-keyword test now actually exercises the channel-keyword leg.** Its embedder returned the same one-hot vector for both document and query (`VecForTopic(9)`), so identical vectors scored cosine similarity 1.0 and the semantic leg — not the channel-keyword leg — was what surfaced the hit; the test passed without testing what it claimed. The embedder now maps the query to slot 9 and documents to slot 0 (orthogonal), so the semantic leg can never clear the threshold and only the channel-name keyword leg can include a hit ([tests/thread-memory.test.js](tests/thread-memory.test.js)). Mirrors the correct pattern already used by the sibling "surfaces in-channel threads below the threshold" test.

## 1.4.192 - 2026-06-13
- Added shared outbound Slack write observability at the `SlackApp` write boundary in [src/slack-app.js](src/slack-app.js). `PostMessageTextAsync`, `PostMessageTextWithBlocksAsync`, and `UploadFileAsync` now emit stable audit attempt/success/failure logs and attach `SleuthAuditWriteID` / `SleuthAuditWriteKind` / `SleuthAuditWriteTag` metadata where Slack supports it, so suspicious posts can be correlated back to a single shared write path instead of individual callers.
- Refactored the audit hook into a durable, easy-to-disable shape: `SlackWriteAudit.Enabled` keeps the instrumentation in code but lets operators silence it later without deleting the plumbing, while `SlackWriteAudit.IncludePreview` leaves plaintext message previews off by default. Audit IDs are now opaque (`slk-...`) rather than embedding `WORKSPACE_NAME`.
- Tagged the highest-signal callers first: `startup`, `shutdown`, `startup-actions`, `daily-digest`, `reminders-feedback`, `reminder-triage`, `weekly-false-positive-report`, `reminder-delivery-target`, `reminder-delivery-origin`, `github-sync-heartbeat`, `github-sync-autocomplete`, `github-sync-thread-mirror`, `rebalance-export`, plus `show-reminders-*` via the shared reminder display helpers in [src/reminders-display-utils.js](src/reminders-display-utils.js), so the canonical `A.) ...` reminder lines no longer collapse into a generic unlabeled bucket.
- Hardened the exception path too: text posts and file uploads now log audit failures even when the Slack SDK throws before returning an `{ ok: false }` payload. Added focused Jest coverage in [tests/slack-app-observability.test.js](tests/slack-app-observability.test.js) for opaque IDs, preview suppression, and exception-path failure logging.

## 1.4.191 - 2026-06-13
- **Close the test-harness gap for the natural (non-forced) reminder check cycle.** The existing "startup with stale reminders" tests trigger the check via the `process reminders now` command, which calls `#CheckRemindersAsync(true)` — **force mode**, which deliberately bypasses BOTH the due-time gate (`now >= ShouldPostOn`) and the snooze-day gate, so neither was ever exercised by the harness. Added a `time-driven check cycle (fake timers, natural non-forced cycle)` describe block to [tests/reminders-integration.test.js](tests/reminders-integration.test.js) that drives the real recursive 30s `setTimeout` loop under `jest.useFakeTimers()` + `jest.setSystemTime()` and asserts (a) the **due-time gate** — a scheduled reminder does not post before `ShouldPostOn`, then posts on the first cycle after it passes — and (b) the **snooze-day gate** — on a configured snooze day a due reminder is suppressed and advanced to the next non-snooze day, while an `IgnoreSnooze` reminder still posts. Groundwork for the P3 event-sourced-core spike, whose lifecycle-event emission rides exactly this cycle ([PROJECT/1-INBOX/P3-EVENT-SOURCED-CORE.md](PROJECT/1-INBOX/P3-EVENT-SOURCED-CORE.md)).
- **README: documented the mock Slack harness for maintainers.** New "Testing & the Mock Slack Harness" section in [README.md](README.md) covering `MockSlackApp` (a Layer-2 wrapper mock — `Simulate*` event injectors, `SentMessages` capture), per-workspace isolation helpers, how to drive each FSM transition, and — the discovery-costly part — the **force-vs-natural** check-cycle distinction plus fake-timer gotchas (no immediate check on `StartAsync`; never `jest.runAllTimers()` on the self-rescheduling loop; snooze is day-based in the workspace `MAIN_TIMEZONE`; read reminder state back from disk). Added a concise "Validation strategy" note under Phase 1 of the P3 doc.
- Tests: +2 (due-time gate, snooze-day gate); no production code changed. Full suite 1105/1105; `validate:fsm` OK; `tsc` build clean. Docs/tests only — no runtime behavior change.

## 1.4.190 - 2026-06-12
- **Harden completion-history durability (code-review follow-up to 1.4.189).** Two gaps surfaced reviewing the Sleuth-owned completion store:
  - **Flush completions on shutdown.** Completions are recorded fire-and-forget from the FSM hook, but `RemindersModule.StopAsync()` did not wait for the queued write — a restart/deploy landing mid-write could drop a just-completed task from `<workspace>_completed.json`, a narrower recurrence of the original "nothing completed" bug. Added `CompletionStore.FlushAsync()` (awaits the serialized write chain) and call it from `StopAsync()` after saving reminder state ([src/completion-store.js](src/completion-store.js), [src/reminders-module.js](src/reminders-module.js)). Regression test: complete → `StopAsync()` → fresh `RemindersModule` for the same workspace → `GetCompletedRemindersBetween` still returns it.
  - **Durable load-time pruning.** `LoadAsync()` pruned >1-year-old rows from memory but not from disk, so a quiet workspace could keep stale rows in the JSON indefinitely. `#PruneExpired()` now reports whether it removed anything and `LoadAsync()` persists the trimmed set when it did.
- Tests: +3 (durable load pruning, `FlushAsync`, restart survives `StopAsync` flush). Full suite 1103/1103; tsc build clean.

## 1.4.189 - 2026-06-12
- **Fix: `summarize-week` always reported "nothing completed."** The completed-this-week section read history exclusively from per-user Slack Lists (`ListsModule.GetCompletedRowsBetweenAsync`), but per-user lists are never auto-provisioned — only created by a manual `ensure-user-list`. In any workspace without them (including production `neochrome`, confirmed via a live prod harness run: `userListCount: 0`), completing a task only touches the **shared** list, where the row is **deleted** on completion (`DeleteRowOnComplete = true` for non-user contexts). So no completion history was retained anywhere and the weekly recap could only ever say "nothing completed," regardless of how many tasks were crossed off. Not a timezone/timestamp bug — the week window was correct.
- **Sleuth now owns its completion history, independent of Slack Lists.** New [src/completion-store.js](src/completion-store.js) (`CompletionStore`, single responsibility: persist + query completed-reminder records) writes to `data/runtime/reminders/<workspace>_completed.json`, mirroring the existing reminders-persistence idiom. API: `LoadAsync`, `Record` (de-dupes by reminder ID, prunes records older than 1 year, serializes durable writes), `GetCompletedBetween(startMs, endMs)` (in-window, oldest-first). It knows nothing about Slack or the FSM — callers hand it plain records.
- **Capture at the single FSM chokepoint.** `RemindersModule.#TransitionReminderState(..., Completed, ...)` is the one path every completion funnels through — ✅ reaction, Slack List checkbox, chat command, and github-sync all route here — so recording the completion there ([src/reminders-module.js](src/reminders-module.js)) captures 100% of completions with no parallel paths. Fire-and-forget: in-memory state updates synchronously, the disk write runs async, and the FSM method stays synchronous. New public `RemindersModule.GetCompletedRemindersBetween(startMs, endMs)` reads the store; the app-mention handler consumes it via dependency injection.
- **`summarize-week` reads the Sleuth store.** [src/reminders-app-mention-handler.js](src/reminders-app-mention-handler.js) `#HandleSummarizeWeekAsync` now pulls "completed this week" from `GetCompletedRemindersBetween` instead of the Lists; the empty case reads "nothing completed yet." Display formatting and message chunking are unchanged. **The Slack Lists integration is left entirely intact** as requested — `GetCompletedRowsBetweenAsync` simply becomes unused by this command (candidate for a later cleanup). Behavior is going-forward only: completions recorded before this version were never persisted and cannot be recovered.
- Tests: +11 — `CompletionStore` unit suite (window boundaries incl. inclusive-start/exclusive-end, dedupe last-write-wins, 1-year prune on record+load, missing/corrupt-file tolerance, invalid-record rejection, durability across reload); FSM regression guard that completing a reminder records it with **no Slack Lists configured** (catches a future refactor dropping the capture hook); handler tests rewritten to read from the Sleuth store (in-window render, out-of-window exclusion, empty "nothing completed" case). Full suite 1100/1100.

## 1.4.188 - 2026-06-12
- **New `summarize-week` command** — a quick "what got done vs. what didn't" recap of the current calendar week (Sunday–Saturday, in the workspace `MAIN_TIMEZONE`). Posts the tasks **completed this week** followed by the tasks **still open**. Public, low-risk, read-only. Phrasings: `summarize week`, `summarize-week`, `weekly summary`, `week summary`, `this week summary`.
- **Reuses the existing command pipeline, no parallel paths.** The command is a normal reminders-domain route registered on the `RemindersAppMentionHandler` `CommandRouter` (alongside `show reminders` et al.) in [src/reminders-app-mention-handler.js](src/reminders-app-mention-handler.js), with a matching `summarize-week` entry in [data/static/ai/command-catalog.json](data/static/ai/command-catalog.json) (Reminders help section) so it shows up in help, the commands list, and `rmm` discovery. HELP.md regenerated; `validate:commands` route/catalog invariant holds.
- **Open tasks** come from the live pending-reminders queue (same source as `show reminders`), rendered via the shared `PostRemindersListAsync` formatter. **Completed tasks** come from the per-user Slack Lists history — the only retained record of finished reminders, since completing a reminder deletes it from the queue and from the shared list but keeps it as a history row on per-user lists (see `HandleReminderCompletedAsync`). New `ListsModule.GetCompletedRowsBetweenAsync(startMs, endMs)` ([src/lists-module.js](src/lists-module.js)) scans every registered per-user list, keeps completed rows whose `updated_timestamp` falls in the week, and de-dupes by reminder ID across lists. When Slack Lists isn't configured for the workspace, the completed section degrades gracefully with a note instead of failing.
- **Week-window math** is a new pure helper `DateUtils.GetCalendarWeekRange(timeZone, ref?)` ([src/date-utils.js](src/date-utils.js)) returning real-UTC `StartMs`/`EndMs` (localized Sun–Sat boundaries shifted back by the tz offset) plus the localized boundary dates for labels.
- Tests: +6 (calendar-week range incl. Sunday-edge and reference-containment; handler completed+open rendering and the lists-unavailable note with a fake `ListsModule`; `GetCompletedRowsBetweenAsync` in-window inclusion / out-of-window exclusion / reminder-id + assignee mapping). Full suite 1089/1089; `npm run build`, `validate:commands`, `validate:ai` clean.

## 1.4.187 - 2026-06-11

Features: searchable entry list with alias-count badges; inline editing of Description, Aliases (tag input), IntentPhrases (tag input), RegexAlias rows (Pattern / Flags / Route / Args), Settings (Permission / Risk / CanExecuteWithIfl), and Advanced fields (RegisteredRoutes, SyntaxExamples, visibility); dirty-state tracking with ⌘S save; rebalance dashboard look-and-feel. Files: [tools/catalog-server.js](tools/catalog-server.js) (HTTP server, GET+POST `/api/catalog`), [tools/catalog-editor.html](tools/catalog-editor.html) (single-file UI). Port registered in `~/bin/servers.md`.

## 1.4.186 - 2026-06-11
- **UNIFY-COMMANDS complete** — all 5 phases of the command-catalog unification project landed. `data/static/ai/command-catalog.json` is now the single place to audit NL-to-command mappings; no alias lives only in code without a documented exception. Highlights: `show-channel-model` NL identity aliases migrated to catalog; `version` command migrated out of `deterministic-responses.json` into a proper route + handler ([src/chat-commands/version-command.js](src/chat-commands/version-command.js)); three-file discovery boundary (catalog / normalization / deterministic-responses) documented in ARCHITECTURE.md and enforced by `validate-command-catalog.js`. Test suite expanded to 42 tests in [tests/catalog-regex-aliases.test.js](tests/catalog-regex-aliases.test.js).

## 1.4.185 - 2026-06-11
- **Catalog-driven natural-language regex aliases** — NL→deterministic-command mappings now live in [data/static/ai/command-catalog.json](data/static/ai/command-catalog.json) instead of hardcoded `Router.Register` blocks. Entries may carry a `RegexAliases` array (`Pattern`/`Flags`/`DescribePattern`/`Route`/`Args`); new module [src/catalog-regex-aliases.js](src/catalog-regex-aliases.js) registers each alias onto the command router during `ChatModule.StartAsync`, delegating to the already-registered route's handler. Adding a phrasing is now a JSON edit, no code change.
- **Args specs** map alias captures to handler arguments: `$N` (capture group), `{user}` (asking user as mention), `{channel}`, `a??b` (first non-empty alternative), or a literal. Aliases register AFTER all code routes (first-match-wins), so they can never shadow a primary command pattern — pinned by a test. Invalid aliases are skipped with a warning at startup and rejected at build time: `ValidateCommandCatalogShape` ([src/command-catalog.js](src/command-catalog.js)) now checks each alias compiles, its `Route` is in the entry's `RegisteredRoutes`, and `Args` are non-empty strings (`validate:commands` covers it).
- **Migrated 5 hardcoded alias blocks** from [src/chat-module.js](src/chat-module.js) to the catalog, patterns byte-identical: show-me's "what are my tasks?" self-referential, third-person "what are @user's tasks", and "for-today [@user]"; show-me-projects' self-referential and third-person phrasings. The model-identity aliases stay in code deliberately — their regex is shared with the thread-reply hallucination guard in `#OnMessageAsync`, which a catalog entry can't reach.
- **New phrasing per request: "show me PROJECTS"** (and "show me projects") now deterministically runs `show-me-projects` — the client/project bucketing command — via the migrated self-referential alias whose `show me my projects` segment was extended to make "my" optional. (Decision: bound to the grouping command, not `search-projects` keyword search.) Also added "show me projects" to the entry's `Aliases` for `rmm` reachability.
- Added [tests/catalog-regex-aliases.test.js](tests/catalog-regex-aliases.test.js) (23 tests): arg-spec resolution, registration/delegation, no-shadowing, invalid-alias skipping, and a live-catalog integration matrix asserting all 12 migrated+new phrasings dispatch to the right handler with the right mention argument (plus non-match guards). Full suite 1064/1064; `npm run build`, `validate:commands`, `validate:ai` clean; HELP.md unchanged.

## 1.4.184 - 2026-06-10
- **Unified the rebalance reminders presentation with the Slack `show reminders` digest** — same wording, same sorting, same filtering, single-sourced. Audit found three divergent pipelines: `show reminders` (summary extraction + due-date sections + canonical compact lines), the published rebalance export (raw text, file order, `activeOnly=true` filter), and `show-rebalance-reminders-formatted` (its own third format). All now share one formatter.
- **Shared builders extracted** into [src/reminders-display-utils.js](src/reminders-display-utils.js) (behavior-preserving — `show reminders` output is byte-identical, pinned by a parity test): `ExtractCompactSummary` (bullet-preferred line, skips mention/follow-up framing, token-safe 140-char truncation), `BuildCompactReminderLine` (the canonical `A.) <permalink|summary> (N days old) - <@user> - <!date…>` line), `REMINDER_DUE_SECTIONS` (the four due-date sections, single-sourced), and `ToWebSafeSummary` (mentions → `@DisplayName`, labelled links → label, channel/special tokens → text) for non-Slack consumers.
- **Rebalance export now carries pre-rendered `display` fields** ([src/web-api.js](src/web-api.js)): reminders are returned in the exact digest order (bucketed Due Today / Due after today / Due within last 7 days / Due older than 7 days in the workspace `MAIN_TIMEZONE`, chronological within each, global A/B/C labels) and each record gains `display: { label, sectionKey, sectionLabel, summary (web-safe), slackSummary, permalink, taggedUserId, assigneeName, ageDays }`, plus top-level `display: { timeZone, sectionOrder }`. The rebalance-OS dashboard can render these verbatim instead of duplicating the wording/sorting rules. NOTE: `display.ageDays` is baked at publish time — consumers should recompute age from `createdOn` (the Slack formatted command does).
- **Name/permalink resolution:** [src/app.js](src/app.js) now hands the web API a workspace→SlackApp map; the export resolves assignee/mention display names via the cached `GetUserDisplayNameAsync` and real permalinks via `GetPermaLinkAsync`, falling back gracefully to raw IDs and deterministically constructed `https://<workspace>.slack.com/archives/<channel>/p<ts>` archive URLs (same construction as the memories export's `threadUrl`) when no live SlackApp exists for the workspace.
- **Filtering parity:** the publisher default flipped to `activeOnly=false` ([deploy/reminders-export/publish-reminders-export.mjs](deploy/reminders-export/publish-reminders-export.mjs)) so the export carries the ENTIRE pending queue — exactly what `show reminders` displays (completed/cancelled reminders are already deleted from the queue, so "no filter" is what "pending" means). `SLEUTH_EXPORT_ACTIVE_ONLY` env override unchanged.
- **`show-rebalance-reminders-formatted` now renders the `show reminders` digest style** from the file's display fields: `Pending reminders (N total):`, the four section headers, and canonical compact lines (consolidated into one message rather than show-reminders' one-message-per-reminder, per design decision). Ages recomputed from `createdOn` at render time. Files published before this version (no `display` fields) fall back to the previous v1.4.183 bullet rendering. Slack-limit caps and "… and N more" overflow notes retained.
- Tests: +20 (display-utils builders incl. byte-parity for `BuildCompactTextForReminder`; export ordering/labels/sections, SlackApp name+permalink resolution, constructed-URL fallback, web-safe summaries; formatted-command digest rendering, stale-age recomputation, global cap, legacy fallback). Full suite 1041/1041; `npm run build`, `validate:commands`, `validate:ai` clean.
- **Dashboard follow-up (separate repo):** rebalance-OS should switch to rendering `reminders[].display` + `display.sectionOrder` verbatim to complete the parity story.

## 1.4.183 - 2026-06-10
- Added **`show-rebalance-reminders-formatted`** — same admin command and same published-file fetch as `show-rebalance-reminders`, but renders **Slack-formatted reminder/memory lines instead of the raw JSON blob**. The freshness summary (repo/path, pull time, `exportGeneratedAt` heartbeat, last commit — dual UTC/local stamps) is retained on top; below it, reminders are sorted by due time (undated last) and rendered one per line with state, due stamp in the workspace time zone (minute precision), assignee mention, trimmed text, and source channel; memories follow with captured date, channel, preview, and message count.
- **Slack-limit safe.** Line lists are capped (20 reminders / 10 memories) with an explicit `… and N more — use show-rebalance-reminders for the full JSON` note; if the first render still exceeds ~3900 chars the body re-renders at tighter caps (10/5), so the post never trips Slack's 4000-char message limit. Counts in the headers always reflect the full file. An unparseable published file degrades to the summary plus a warning pointing at the raw dump.
- **Routing order matters and is now documented + tested:** the existing `/^show-rebalance-reminders\b/i` pattern also matches the `-formatted` suffix (hyphen = word boundary), so the formatted route is registered first in [src/chat-module.js](src/chat-module.js) (first match wins, per `chat-command-router.js`'s own guidance), with a regression test pinning that ordering hazard.
- Implemented as a `{ Formatted: true }` option on the existing handler in [src/chat-commands/show-rebalance-reminders-command.js](src/chat-commands/show-rebalance-reminders-command.js) (new exported helpers `BuildFormattedBody`/`TrimToOneLine`), so the fetch/auth/error paths stay single-sourced. Wired through the documented 4 touchpoints: route registration, [data/static/ai/command-catalog.json](data/static/ai/command-catalog.json) entry (admin, `CanExecuteWithIfl:false`, with disambiguation vs the raw dump), `BuildCanonicalCommand` case in [src/command-intent-resolver.js](src/command-intent-resolver.js), and HELP.md regenerated (no diff — `IncludeInHelp:false`).
- Extended [tests/show-rebalance-reminders-command.test.js](tests/show-rebalance-reminders-command.test.js) with 16 tests: formatted routing pattern + the registration-order hazard, `TrimToOneLine`, `BuildFormattedBody` (sorting, missing-field labels, overflow caps, memories, empty sections), and formatted-mode handler behavior (no file upload, Slack-limit fallback re-render, invalid-JSON warning, non-admin denial). Full suite 1021/1021; `validate:commands`, `validate:ai`, and `npm run build` clean.

## 1.4.182 - 2026-06-10
- Added natural-language **model-identity aliases** for `show-channel-model`: "what model are you running on this channel?", "which model are you running right now", "what model are you using?", "what model are you", "which model is this channel using", and close variants now resolve deterministically to the channel-model status block. The shared anchored regex (`ModelIdentityPattern`, exported from [src/chat-commands/show-channel-model-command.js](src/chat-commands/show-channel-model-command.js)) is registered as a router fast-path in [src/chat-module.js](src/chat-module.js) — the LLM is never in the loop for these phrasings. Deliberately matches only the singular "model": plural availability questions ("what models are available right now") still route to `live-model-catalog-question`.
- **Verified-answer framing.** The `show-channel-model` response now ends with `:white_check_mark: _Verified answer — read directly from runtime configuration (channel-override store + workspace settings), not generated by the AI model._` so users can distinguish a config-grounded answer from chat-model self-description. The values themselves were always deterministic (`#BuildChannelModelStatus` reads `ChannelModelSettings` + `WorkspaceAI.DefaultModelName`); the framing makes that provenance explicit.
- **Thread-reply hallucination guard.** The one place a wrong model name could previously occur: asking "what model are you?" mid-thread, where the chat model would answer from its own (notoriously unreliable) self-knowledge. `#OnMessageAsync` now intercepts model-identity questions right after the deterministic-response check and answers from runtime config instead of forwarding to the AI — skipped when the message just loaded a context file, mirroring the existing deterministic-response guard.
- **RMM reachability.** Added the new phrasings as `Aliases`/`IntentPhrases` on the `show-channel-model` entry in [data/static/ai/command-catalog.json](data/static/ai/command-catalog.json), plus a `DisambiguationNotes` rule that model-identity questions must map here (never answered from self-knowledge), so fuzzy variants the regex misses still resolve via `rmm`. No new route or `BuildCanonicalCommand` intent was needed — the aliases reuse the existing `show-channel-model` route, keeping the `validate:commands` route↔catalog invariant balanced. `HELP.md` regenerated with no diff (aliases are not part of help text).
- Added [tests/show-channel-model-command.test.js](tests/show-channel-model-command.test.js): 13 matching phrasings, 9 deliberate non-matches (plural availability questions, model-switch commands, advice questions, mid-sentence mentions, "that channel"), and handler output checks (status block, override reporting, verified-answer framing). Full suite 1005/1005, `npm run build`, `validate:commands`, and `validate:ai` all clean.

## 1.4.181 - 2026-06-10
