- Added a new admin Slack command **`show-rebalance-reminders`** — a debugging dump of the **published** rebalance reminders export, i.e. the actual `sync/sleuth/reminders-<ws>.json` file in the private `example-org/export-repo` repo that the rebalance-OS dashboard consumes. Unlike the live in-process `?format=rebalance` payload (which always looks fresh), this fetches the **consumer-side ground truth** via the GitHub contents API, so a dead or lagging publisher is immediately visible. Handler: [src/chat-commands/show-rebalance-reminders-command.js](src/chat-commands/show-rebalance-reminders-command.js).
- **Always targets production, never dev.** `ResolveExportLocation()` mirrors the publisher's own resolution in [deploy/reminders-export/publish-reminders-export.mjs](deploy/reminders-export/publish-reminders-export.mjs) **exactly** — `SLEUTH_EXPORT_WORKSPACE || 'neochrome'`, repo default `example-org/export-repo`, path `sync/sleuth/reminders-<workspace>.json`, branch `main`. It deliberately does **not** derive the path from the live `WORKSPACE_NAME` (which is `neochrome` on the prod Vultr server but `neochrome-dev` locally), so an admin running the command from any environment reads the same production file the prod publisher writes. The Repo/Path/Branch are echoed in the output so the source is unambiguous.
- **Dual timestamps as requested.** The summary stamps the pull time, the file's own hourly `exportGeneratedAt` heartbeat, and the last git commit time — each in **UTC (offset 0)** and the **workspace-local zone** (`WorkspaceInfo.MAIN_TIMEZONE`, falling back to UTC), each with a relative age — so freshness/staleness is obvious at a glance. Timestamp formatting is built deterministically from `Intl.DateTimeFormat` parts (`FormatYmdHms`/`FormatDualStamp`/`FormatAge`).
- **Output.** The full file is attached **verbatim** via `UploadFileAsync` (avoiding Slack's message-size truncation) with the summary as the share comment; if the upload fails (e.g. missing `files:write` scope) it falls back to an inline, size-capped code block. Token resolution prefers `SLEUTH_RAG_GITHUB_PAT` (the same PAT the publisher pushes with, which is the only one with read access to that private repo) and falls back to the workspace `GITHUB_PAT`. A missing file (404), missing token, or GitHub error each produce a clear, self-explanatory message rather than a stack trace.
- **Wired through the full command registry (the documented 4 touchpoints):** (1) route registered in `#RegisterCommandRoutes` in [src/chat-module.js](src/chat-module.js); (2) catalog entry in [data/static/ai/command-catalog.json](data/static/ai/command-catalog.json) (`admin`, `IncludeInHelp:false`, `IncludeInCommandsList:true`, `CanExecuteWithIfl:false` so `rmm` suggests but does not auto-dump); (3) `show-rebalance-reminders` intent added to `BuildCanonicalCommand` in [src/command-intent-resolver.js](src/command-intent-resolver.js) for `rmm` reachability; (4) `HELP.md` regenerated. Balanced on both sides of the `validate:commands` route↔catalog invariant.
- Added [tests/show-rebalance-reminders-command.test.js](tests/show-rebalance-reminders-command.test.js): routing-pattern matches/non-matches, deterministic timezone formatting (UTC + EDT offset, midnight-hour normalization), age formatting, `ResolveExportLocation` defaults + env overrides, summary contents (dual stamps, heartbeat age, counts), and handler behavior (non-admin denial with no GitHub call, missing-token, 404 not-published, verbatim file upload with dual-stamp summary + temp-file cleanup, and the inline upload-failure fallback).
- Fixed a pre-existing `checkJs` build error in [src/thread-memory.js](src/thread-memory.js): the `run-diagnostics` thread-memory Gemini probe (`TestThreadMemoryPipelineAsync`) constructed its synthetic `ThreadMemoryRecord` without the `WorkspaceName`/`ChannelName` fields that v1.4.179 added to the typedef, leaving `npm run build` red since that release. Added synthetic `WorkspaceName: 'diagnostics-workspace'` / `ChannelName: 'diagnostics-channel'` values (behavior-safe — `SaveThreadMemoryAsync` already coalesced the missing fields to `''`, so the probe was unaffected; this only restores type-correctness). `npm run build` is now clean (exit 0) and the thread-memory suite stays green (21/21).

## 1.4.180 - 2026-06-09
- Added Anthropic Fable 5 model aliases to the `rmm`/`rmm ifl` normalization table in [data/static/ai/command-normalization.json](data/static/ai/command-normalization.json): `anthropic claude fable 5`, `anthropic fable 5`, `claude fable 5`, `fable 5`, and `fable5` all normalize to the official model ID `claude-fable-5`. A natural-language request like `@Sleuth AI rmm ifl switch this channel's model to Anthropic Fable 5` now normalizes to `claude-fable-5` before intent resolution, so the resolver can build and auto-execute `set-channel-model:'claude-fable-5'` (already pre-authorized via `CanExecuteWithIfl`). Validation against the live Anthropic catalog at execution time is unchanged.
- Deliberately did **not** add a bare single-word `fable` alias: aliases are applied in order as whole-phrase word-boundary regexes, and because hyphens count as word boundaries, a later bare-`fable` pass would re-match inside an earlier `claude-fable-5` replacement and corrupt it (`claude-claude-fable-5-5`). Multi-word matches are safe because `\s+` between tokens cannot match the hyphen. This same constraint is why the existing GPT aliases never use a bare token that appears inside any `Replace` value.
- Verified with [tests/command-intent-resolver.test.js](tests/command-intent-resolver.test.js), [tests/rmm-help-regression.test.js](tests/rmm-help-regression.test.js) (13 tests passing), and `npm run validate:ai`.

## 1.4.179 - 2026-06-08
- Added `memories` array to the reminders export file (`sync/sleuth/reminders-neochrome.json`). The `sleuth-reminders-export` publisher now joins thread memories captured via `remember above` alongside reminders at publish time. The key is always present (`"memories": []` when none exist).
- Each memory export record contains: `memoryId`, `channelId`, `channelName`, `capturedAt`, `capturedBy`, `participantIds`, `messageCount`, `preview` (first 200 chars of thread content), `githubRefs` (URL strings only), and `threadUrl` (workspace-specific Slack permalink).
- Extended the `thread_memories` schema with two new columns — `workspace_name` (the workspace slug, e.g. `neochrome`) and `channel_name` (resolved at capture time) — with a backward-compatible `ALTER TABLE` migration so existing databases upgrade automatically on next start.
- `CaptureThreadAsync` in [src/thread-memory.js](src/thread-memory.js) now calls `GetChannelNameAsync` and reads `WorkspaceInfo.WORKSPACE_NAME` from the Slack app at capture time, so new memories have both fields populated immediately.
- Added `ListThreadMemoriesForExport(workspaceName, dbPath?)` to [src/thread-memory.js](src/thread-memory.js): opens a read-only connection to the on-disk DB (safe for the publisher's separate process), returns the export-shaped records, and returns `[]` gracefully when the DB file doesn't exist yet.
- [deploy/reminders-export/publish-reminders-export.mjs](deploy/reminders-export/publish-reminders-export.mjs) now loads memories via `ListThreadMemoriesForExport` before building the export payload; a non-fatal warning is logged and memories default to `[]` if loading fails.
- [deploy/reminders-export/export-payload.js](deploy/reminders-export/export-payload.js) accepts an optional `ArgMemories` parameter and merges it into the payload.
- Added tests in [tests/reminders-export.test.js](tests/reminders-export.test.js) for the memories key always being present, memories round-tripping, and content changing when memories change. Added tests in [tests/thread-memory.test.js](tests/thread-memory.test.js) for `ListThreadMemoriesForExport` workspace isolation, export shape, preview truncation, and the no-DB fallback.

## 1.4.178 - 2026-06-08
- Added a durable production-safe diagnostic for the `remember above` / `recall` Gemini path. `@Sleuth run-diagnostics` now includes `Thread-memory Gemini pipeline: ...`, which performs a synthetic end-to-end probe in [src/thread-memory.js](src/thread-memory.js): it opens an in-memory sqlite-vec database, saves one fake thread memory through the real Gemini document-embedding call, runs a real Gemini query embedding, and confirms the synthetic memory is returned by recall search. This validates the live `GOOGLE_API_KEY`, the Gemini embedding endpoint, and the thread-memory sqlite/vector plumbing without writing any real memory rows to the shared store.
- Added regression coverage in [tests/thread-memory.test.js](tests/thread-memory.test.js) for the new end-to-end probe and in [tests/chat-module.integration.test.js](tests/chat-module.integration.test.js) to prove admin `run-diagnostics` output includes the thread-memory pipeline line when `GOOGLE_API_KEY` is configured. Updated [docs/thread-memory-user-guide.md](docs/thread-memory-user-guide.md) so operators have a durable production verification path.

## 1.4.177 - 2026-06-08
- Phase 6 of the reminder false-positive hardening plan: tightened the shared temporal gate so bare `asap` no longer counts on its own. [src/reminders-app-mention-handler.js](src/reminders-app-mention-handler.js) now keeps `asap` outside the base `SCHEDULING_TRIGGER_PATTERN` and only returns it from `GetSchedulingTriggerMatch(...)` when it appears with stronger reminder-intent context: a direct ask (`can you`, `could you`, `would you`), an explicit `please <action> ... asap` form, a first-person commitment (`I'll ... asap`, `I'm going to ... asap`), or a leading imperative / task-assignment shape (`review this asap`, `<@UTARGET> fix this asap`).
- This preserves the narrower Phase 1-5 protections while reducing the blast radius of `asap` across all shared consumers of the gate: message-event auto scheduling, app-mention task-assignment detection, and the vague-thread enrichment precondition. Non-committal aspirational language like `the goal is to be able to reactivate that plugin asap` and bare `asap please` no longer trip the gate.
- Added regression coverage in [tests/reminders-app-mention-handler.test.js](tests/reminders-app-mention-handler.test.js) for both the new positive `asap` shapes and the new negative ones, while the existing integration suite in [tests/reminders-integration.test.js](tests/reminders-integration.test.js) still proves the production-class weak reply remains suppressed.

## 1.4.176 - 2026-06-08
- Phase 5 of the reminder false-positive hardening plan: added a **narrow syntactic guard** on the enriched thread-reply path for clearly hypothetical subordinate forms such as `when I get to that ... asap`. This lives only in [src/reminders-app-mention-handler.js](src/reminders-app-mention-handler.js), before any context prepending or AI scheduling call, so it reduces cost and false-positive exposure without changing the shared temporal regex.
- The new guard is structural rather than a broad phrase blacklist: `TryEnrichVagueCompletionFromAboveAsync(...)` now detects subordinate `when/if I get to|work on|come back to|circle back to ...` shapes, logs `reminder enrichment guard`, and consumes the event locally so the weak reply does not fall through to either enriched or raw reminder scheduling. Legitimate enriched commitments like `I'll handle it tomorrow morning` are unaffected.
- Added regression coverage in [tests/reminders-app-mention-handler.test.js](tests/reminders-app-mention-handler.test.js) and updated [tests/reminders-integration.test.js](tests/reminders-integration.test.js) so the production-class weak reply now proves `TryScheduleRemindersAsync` / the AI pipeline are not called at all, while the stronger enriched commitment path still schedules and still emits the Phase 4 observability breadcrumbs.

## 1.4.175 - 2026-06-08
- Phase 4 of the reminder false-positive hardening plan: added **path-level observability** for the enriched reminder pipeline so future false positives are explainable from logs without reconstructing runtime artifacts. [src/reminders-app-mention-handler.js](src/reminders-app-mention-handler.js) now logs which structural enrichment matcher fired and the first matched temporal trigger, while [src/reminders-module.js](src/reminders-module.js) logs the scheduling path, the enriched-reply safety-check verdict, and the display-source decision for the quote body vs. task text.
- Added a shared trigger-match helper on `RemindersAppMentionHandler` so the same temporal cue can be reported consistently across the entry gate, enrichment path, and scheduling gateway instead of relying on ad hoc regex re-runs. The scheduling gateway now emits `reminder enriched-reply safety check` and `reminder display source` log lines that capture whether the live reply was treated as weak/hypothetical and whether the visible reminder text came from the live reply, message text, or synthesized task title.
- Extended regression coverage in [tests/reminders-app-mention-handler.test.js](tests/reminders-app-mention-handler.test.js) and [tests/reminders-integration.test.js](tests/reminders-integration.test.js) to assert those breadcrumbs are present for both the suppressed false-positive case and the allowed legitimate-commitment case, including synthesis OFF and ON paths.

## 1.4.174 - 2026-06-08
- Phase 3 of the reminder false-positive hardening plan: separated **analysis context** from the **visible reminder body** for enriched thread replies. When the vague-thread enrichment path prepends earlier messages to help the AI decide whether to schedule, Sleuth now uses the live reply text as the display source for the quoted reminder body and, when `REMINDER_TEXT_SYNTHESIS` is off, for the displayed task bullet as well.
- Implemented this in [src/reminders-module.js](src/reminders-module.js) by deriving a `DisplaySourceMessageText` from the raw live reply when enriched thread context was used, then feeding that text into both the synthesis-off `reminder_message` override and `#ComposeReminderMessageAsync(...)`. The enriched combined text still remains available for scheduling analysis, date extraction, dedup, and GitHub URL detection.
- Added regression coverage in [tests/reminders-integration.test.js](tests/reminders-integration.test.js) for both synthesis modes: with synthesis OFF, an enriched reminder now persists only the live reply in both the quote block and task bullet; with synthesis ON, the AI task title is preserved while the quoted reminder body still shows only the live reply instead of the prepended parent thread text.

## 1.4.173 - 2026-06-08
- Phase 2 of the reminder false-positive hardening plan: added a deterministic **post-enrichment safety check** in [src/reminders-module.js](src/reminders-module.js) so weak live thread replies no longer schedule reminders just because prepended context and the LLM lean `schedule`. The new backstop runs only on the enriched-thread path and only after AI analysis returns `schedule`; it suppresses scheduling when the **live reply text** is weak acknowledgment / hypothetical language (e.g. `keep that in mind`, `I'm assuming the goal is`, `when I get to that`) and lacks a stronger commitment or direct ask.
- Implemented this without touching the shared recall-biased temporal gate or the enrichment matcher. `TryEnrichVagueCompletionFromAboveAsync` now passes the raw live reply text and an `ArgUsedEnrichedThreadContext` flag through to the scheduling gateway, and `#TryScheduleRemindersAsync` applies `#ShouldSuppressWeakEnrichedReply(...)` only for that narrow path. This preserves legitimate enriched commitments like `I'll handle it tomorrow morning` while blocking the production-class false positive where enriched context overrode a weak acknowledgment reply.
- Also fixed the handler-to-module callback shape so the enrichment path now forwards its optional thread arguments all the way into the scheduling gateway instead of silently dropping them in the injected callback adapter. That was necessary for the safety check because the gateway needs both the prepended analysis text and the original live reply text.
- Added regression coverage in [tests/reminders-app-mention-handler.test.js](tests/reminders-app-mention-handler.test.js) proving enriched scheduling now forwards `thread_ts`, raw live reply text, and the enriched-context flag, plus two integration tests in [tests/reminders-integration.test.js](tests/reminders-integration.test.js): (1) a weak acknowledgment reply with earlier task-heavy context is suppressed even when the mocked AI says `schedule`, and (2) a real enriched commitment still schedules. Validation: targeted Jest suites and `npm run build`.

## 1.4.172 - 2026-06-08
- Phase 1 of the reminder false-positive hardening plan: strengthened [data/static/ai/reminders-instructions.md](data/static/ai/reminders-instructions.md) against weak acknowledgment / hypothetical **thread replies** that inherit earlier task-heavy context. The prompt now explicitly says earlier thread messages may help resolve `it` / `that` but do **not** automatically make the live reply schedulable; the reply itself must still contain a real commitment or direct ask.
- Added concrete negative guidance for the exact class of false positive seen in production: `I'll keep that in mind`, `when I get to that`, `I'm assuming the goal is...`, `the goal is to be able to...`, and the specific example `I'll keep that in mind when I get to that plugin. I'm assuming the goal is to be able to reactivate that plugin asap.` now map to `ignore` even if earlier thread context contains real tasks. Added explicit positive contrast examples (`I'll handle it tomorrow morning`, `I'll do it by 4 PM`, `Can you review it by EOD?`) so the model keeps scheduling real commitments.
- This phase intentionally does **not** change any structural gates (`SCHEDULING_TRIGGER_PATTERN`, vague-thread enrichment matcher) or the display/source-text path. The architectural intent is to fix the decision layer first, consistent with the pipeline's recall-biased design; deterministic post-enrichment safety checks and reminder-body source-text cleanup remain later phases in [PROJECT/2-WORKING/P1-REMINDER-FALSE-POSITIVE-HARDENING.md](PROJECT/2-WORKING/P1-REMINDER-FALSE-POSITIVE-HARDENING.md).
- Added a regression test in [tests/reminders-ai-pipeline.test.js](tests/reminders-ai-pipeline.test.js) asserting the reminder instructions retain the new thread-context / acknowledgment-language guidance. Validation: `npm run validate:ai`, targeted reminder-AI Jest suite, and `npm run build`.

## 1.4.171 - 2026-06-08
- Fenced the hand-authored "Command Catalog, Help, and RMM Intent Resolution" subsection in [ARCHITECTURE.md](ARCHITECTURE.md) with `BEGIN/END MANUAL SECTION: command-catalog-help-rmm` HTML-comment markers so the content is preserved if a doc generator (e.g. ask-self's architecture generator) is ever pointed at this file. Note: nothing in this repo currently regenerates ARCHITECTURE.md — `rag:ingest` only reads it and writes its architecture summary to the SQLite index, never back to the `.md` — so this is defensive/forward-looking, not a fix for active clobbering. Establishes the repo's first manual-section marker convention.

## 1.4.170 - 2026-06-08
- Added a new `search-projects` command — a shortcut for `search reminders PROJECT` that surfaces high-level PROJECT-tagged reminders. The plural command word maps to the singular `PROJECT` keyword; trailing keywords are appended so `search-projects client-a` runs `search reminders PROJECT client-a` (Option B / expandable). New route in [src/reminders-app-mention-handler.js](src/reminders-app-mention-handler.js) (registered before the base search route; `projects` is not a reminder/task noun so they never collide), routing to the existing `#HandleSearchRemindersAsync`. Two routing regression tests added (bare + narrowed); full suite 910 passing.
- Wired the command into all three discovery surfaces: added a catalog entry to [data/static/ai/command-catalog.json](data/static/ai/command-catalog.json) (`IncludeInHelp`/`IncludeInCommandsList` true, `HelpSection: Reminders`), regenerated [data/static/HELP.md](data/static/HELP.md), and added a `search-projects` intent to `BuildCanonicalCommand` in [src/command-intent-resolver.js](src/command-intent-resolver.js) so `rmm` can suggest/execute it (runnable with no trailing query since PROJECT is the implicit keyword). **rmm does not auto-discover routes** — it resolves against the curated catalog, so the catalog + resolver edits are what make it rmm-aware.
- Documented the command-catalog → help/commands/rmm architecture in [ARCHITECTURE.md](ARCHITECTURE.md) (new "Command Catalog, Help, and RMM Intent Resolution" subsection), including the bidirectional `validate:commands` invariant and the four places a new help/rmm-visible command must be touched. Also noted the distinction between deterministic `search-projects` and LLM-bucketing `show-me-projects`.
- **Known pre-existing drift (not introduced here):** `npm run validate:commands` is advisory (not in CI) and currently red because the `show-me`, `show-me-projects`, and `create-reminder-from-task-above-shorthand` routes (shipped in v1.4.161) lack catalog entries. The `search-projects` addition is balanced on both sides; restoring a clean validation pass is tracked separately.

## 1.4.169 - 2026-06-08
- Added `search-reminders` (hyphenated) as an alias for the `search reminders` command, matching the hyphen convention already used by other commands (`sync-github`, `generate-list`). The base search route in [src/reminders-app-mention-handler.js](src/reminders-app-mention-handler.js) now accepts a hyphen or whitespace between `search` and the noun (`\bsearch(?:-|\s+)…`), so `@Sleuth search-reminders PROJECT` behaves identically to `@Sleuth search reminders PROJECT`. Regression test added in [tests/reminders-app-mention-handler.test.js](tests/reminders-app-mention-handler.test.js); suite 73/73.

## 1.4.168 - 2026-06-08
- Raised the code-RAG ingest guards so the two largest source modules are no longer skipped from the index. `MAX_CODE_FILE_BYTES` 100 KB → 150 KB and `MAX_CODE_CHUNKS_PER_FILE` 24 → 36 in [src/rag/helpers.js](src/rag/helpers.js). Previously [src/lists-module.js](src/lists-module.js) (105 KB) and [src/reminders-module.js](src/reminders-module.js) (114 KB) tripped the byte guard; bumping bytes alone was insufficient because both also produce 25/27 chunks (> the old 24-chunk cap), so the chunk cap was raised in lockstep (a 150 KB file is ~36 chunks) to keep the two guards internally consistent. After re-ingest: code chunks 199 → 251, total corpus 531 → 583. The chunk cap exists to stop a single large module from dominating retrieval, so this is a deliberate (reversible) tradeoff. Helper tests reference the constants symbolically — 42/42 still pass.

## 1.4.167 - 2026-06-05
- Fixed thread context-memory file analysis silently ignoring everything except `.md` uploads. The detection in `ChatModule.#TryStoreThreadMemoryFileAsync` was a bare `name.toLowerCase().endsWith('.md')`, so a Slack **code snippet** (the common way users paste slow-query logs etc. — e.g. `shop2client-a-slowqueries`, which arrives with an extensionless name, `mimetype: text/plain`, and a language `filetype`) matched nothing, fell through to the plain AI path, and produced the contradictory "I don't see any files attached" reply even though a file was clearly present. Extracted the eligibility rules into a new pure module [src/context-file-classifier.js](src/context-file-classifier.js) (`SelectContextMemoryFile`, `IsTextLikeContextFile`, `IsBinaryMediaFile`, `LooksLikeHtmlErrorPage`). A file now counts as text when **any** of its MIME type (`text/*` or a known textual `application/*`), Slack `filetype`, or name extension (Markdown, plain text, code, logs, CSV/JSON/YAML, SQL, etc.) indicates text — signal-redundant so extensionless snippets are caught. Added `filetype` to the `SlackFileInfo` typedef and `NormalizeSlackFileInfo`.
- Added size/type guards (per request): the 200 KB cap is preserved, and when a file is attached but none are text-readable (image/PDF/archive) the handler now posts a clear "I can only read text-based files…" message instead of silently producing an ungrounded answer. The HTML-error guard that rejects Slack login/preview pages was tightened from "content starts with `<`" to "content starts with `<!doctype html`/`<html`", so genuine text uploads that legitimately start with `<` (XML, SVG, HTML fragments, JSX) are no longer misclassified as a failed download.
- Added regression coverage: [tests/context-file-classifier.test.js](tests/context-file-classifier.test.js) (unit tests for extension/MIME/filetype/snippet/binary/HTML-page classification) and 6 new integration cases in [tests/chat-module.integration.test.js](tests/chat-module.integration.test.js) proving a no-`.md` snippet is loaded and analyzed, a `.log` upload is loaded, a binary attachment gets the clear rejection without an AI call or download, an `<?xml`-leading file is not rejected as an HTML page, and an actual Slack HTML page still is. Internal return field `FoundMarkdownFile` → `FoundContextFile` at both call sites. Full suite: 907 passing; `npm run build` clean. The diagnosis used the `ask-self` RAG index plus `CHANGELOG.md`/`ARCHITECTURE.md`.

## 1.4.166 - 2026-06-05
- Hardened the reminders-export publisher for testability and reproducible deploys (from the rebalance-OS-side review). Extracted the pure payload logic into [deploy/reminders-export/export-payload.js](deploy/reminders-export/export-payload.js) (`BuildExportContent(apiData, nowMs)` — strips `fetchedAt`, stamps the hourly-rounded `exportGeneratedAt`, returns deterministic content) and added [tests/reminders-export.test.js](tests/reminders-export.test.js): 7 jest cases covering fetchedAt-stripping, hourly heartbeat rounding, same-hour idempotency (no commit), cross-hour advance, data-change detection, input-immutability, and valid trailing-newline JSON. The ESM publisher now imports this module. `deploy/` stays out of `tsconfig` include, so the build is unaffected; redeployed + verified on prod.
- Added [deploy/reminders-export/install.sh](deploy/reminders-export/install.sh) — a one-command, idempotent installer (script + `export-payload.js` + systemd units → `daemon-reload` + `enable --now`) so a box rebuild reproduces the publisher without hand-running the README steps. README updated to lead with it.

## 1.4.165 - 2026-06-05
- Reminders export now stamps an **hourly-rounded `exportGeneratedAt` heartbeat** in place of the volatile `fetchedAt` ([deploy/reminders-export/publish-reminders-export.mjs](deploy/reminders-export/publish-reminders-export.mjs)). It advances even when the reminder data is unchanged, so a **dead publisher becomes visible** to consumers (rebalance-OS persists it and `rebalance doctor` warns when the source timestamp goes stale — previously a broken publisher plus a working local sync loop looked "fresh" forever). Rounding to the hour keeps no-op commits at ~24/day instead of one per 5-minute run; within an hour, unchanged data still produces no commit. Addresses the blocking "no source freshness signal" finding from the rebalance-OS-side review. Redeployed + verified on prod (`exportGeneratedAt` present in the published file). No app/runtime change — publisher script only.

## 1.4.164 - 2026-06-04
- Added a **push-based reminders export** so downstream consumers (rebalance-OS) no longer need inbound access to this server. A systemd timer (`deploy/reminders-export/`) runs `publish-reminders-export.mjs` every 5 minutes: it GETs the local API on loopback (`http://127.0.0.1:2020/workspace/<ws>/reminders?format=rebalance&activeOnly=true`) and PUTs the JSON to a **private** git repo (`example-org/export-repo` at `sync/sleuth/reminders-<ws>.json`) via the GitHub contents API. This **replaces the SSH-tunnel pull** (firewalled `:2020` → `127.0.0.1:12020`): there is now **no inbound port, no SSH key, and no tunnel** required on the consumer side — both sides use credentials they already have.
- Reuses existing creds from `/root/sleuth-app/.env.runtime` (`WEB_API_BEARER_TOKEN` to read, `SLEUTH_RAG_GITHUB_PAT` — classic `repo` scope, already has write — to push); no new secrets. The publisher is **idempotent**: it strips the volatile `fetchedAt` and only commits when the reminder data actually changed (git commit time then doubles as "reminders last changed at"). The script + units live **outside** the app's git tree (`/root/sleuth-reminders-export/`, `/etc/systemd/system/`) so app `git pull`s can't conflict; copies are versioned under `deploy/reminders-export/` with install/operate docs. Deployed and verified on prod (27 reminders published, idempotent re-run, timer scheduled `OnCalendar=*:0/5`).

## 1.4.163 - 2026-06-04
- Reminders now **preserve the original Slack message text verbatim** instead of an AI-rewritten "task title", behind a reversible flag `REMINDER_TEXT_SYNTHESIS` (default **OFF** = raw text; set to `on`/`true`/`1`/`yes`/`enabled` to restore the old synthesis behavior). Previously, when a message became a reminder, an LLM rewrote the user's wording into a concise title (e.g. "Ok, I'll restore Production to Dev now" → "Restore Production to Dev") that was shown in the "Key task(s)" bullet of `ReminderMessageText` and surfaced as the one-line summary in the daily-tasks digest. The verbatim original was already kept in the reminder's `>` blockquote; this change makes the **displayed** task text match it too.
- **Single authoritative chokepoint.** Added `RemindersAIPipeline.IsTextSynthesisEnabled()` and `RemindersAIPipeline.NormalizeOriginalReminderText()` (trim + collapse interior whitespace/newlines to single spaces). In `reminders-module.js` `#TryScheduleRemindersAsync`, when synthesis is OFF, a single post-analysis loop overwrites every reminder's `reminder_message` with the normalized original — covering **both** the auto-detected path (`AnalyzeMessageForRemindersAsync`) and the force-scheduled path (`ExtractManualReminderTaskAsync`), so any future reminder source inherits the behavior. The force-path LLM title call is now **skipped entirely** when synthesis is OFF (no wasted OpenAI request), falling back to the original message on synthesis errors as before.
- **Detection logic untouched.** Is-this-a-reminder analysis, date/time extraction (`ExtractDateWithGptAsync`), duplicate collapsing (`CheckForDuplicateReminderAsync`), and triage (`GetReminderTriageAsync`) still run against the original message text and are unchanged — only the *rewriting of the displayed text* is suppressed. Verified the full suite (879 tests) green with the new default.
- **Digest truncation.** Synthesized titles were short by construction; raw originals can be long, so `BuildCompactTextForReminder` now truncates the compact summary to 140 chars with an ellipsis via the new `TruncateCompactSummary` helper, which is **Slack-token-aware** (never severs a `<@mention>` or `<url|label>` mid-bracket — it drops back to before an unclosed `<`).
- **`?format=rebalance` confirmed safe.** The rebalance dashboard's `compact_sleuth_reminder` (rebalance-OS `src/rebalance/ingest/slack_users.py`) **prefers the `>` blockquote** (the verbatim original) and only falls back to the "Key task(s)" bullet when no blockquote exists, which `#ComposeReminderMessageAsync` always emits. Changing the bullet to the original therefore does not regress the rebalance display; `web-api.js` still returns the full `ReminderMessageText` unchanged in shape.
- **Tests.** Added unit coverage for `IsTextSynthesisEnabled` (default-OFF, blank/`off`/`false`/`0`/`disabled` → OFF, explicit `on`/`true`/`1`/`yes`/`enabled` → ON, case/space-insensitive), `NormalizeOriginalReminderText` (whitespace collapse, null/empty, verbatim wording), and `TruncateCompactSummary` (short pass-through, exact-limit, ellipsis + trailing-space trim, no mid-token sever). Added an integration test proving the alarm-clock force-schedule path preserves the original verbatim **and skips the synthesis LLM call** when OFF; the two existing synthesis-asserting integration tests now explicitly opt in via `REMINDER_TEXT_SYNTHESIS=on` (restoring the flag in `finally`).

## 1.4.162 - 2026-06-03
- Made the `ask-code` command resilient to **truncated** upstream answers so a cut-off remote response no longer surfaces as a raw `{ "answer": "...` JSON blob in Slack. Root cause observed on the Client B rag-agent: it **double-encodes** its answer (serializes `{answer:"..."}` into the `answer` string — the same contract drift handled in 1.4.136) **and** truncates it mid-stream (no closing quote/brace). The 1.4.136 unwrap requires the inner JSON to be well-formed, so on a truncated payload `JSON.parse` threw and `ExtractAnswerText` fell back to posting the raw, partial blob. This is an upstream truncation (not a Sleuth bug, and unrelated to the `REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS` 30s→90s bump in `c3fbb98`), but Sleuth should degrade gracefully.
- Added `RecoverPartialNestedAnswer` + `UnescapeJsonStringFragment` in `src/chat-commands/ask-code-command.js`. When the nested-JSON parse fails, Sleuth now best-effort extracts the inner `"answer": "<value>` via regex (the value pattern `(?:[^"\\]|\\.)*` runs to end-of-input when the closing quote was truncated away), unescapes the common JSON escapes in a single pass (dropping a dangling trailing backslash from a mid-escape cut), and posts the recovered prose with an explicit `:warning:` truncation notice instead of the raw JSON. Falls back to the original raw text only when no `answer` field can be located at all. Logs a warning so operators can see the upstream is truncating.
- Added a regression test in `tests/chat-module.integration.test.js` reproducing the exact observed payload (`{answer:'{\n  "answer": "…Phase 14 (the /analysis Reflex page'}`): asserts the inner prose is surfaced up to the truncation point, the raw `"answer":` wrapper is not shown, the message doesn't start with `{`, and a truncation notice is present. `npm run build` clean.

## 1.4.161 - 2026-06-03
- Added `@Sleuth show-me-projects @user` — groups a user's open reminders into **client** and informal **project** buckets, then orders projects and tasks by the same urgency ladder `show-me` uses. Backed by the 2026-06-03 production spike (`PROJECT/2-WORKING/P2-TASK-BUCKETING.md`) which confirmed the main LLM reliably infers both dimensions from signals already stored on each reminder (channel name, GitHub repo owner, product/domain, and the `Key task(s):` text) — no thread re-fetch needed.
- **No parallel read path / DRY:** extracted the shared reminder-acquisition logic out of `show-me-command.js` into new `src/chat-commands/show-me-context.js` (`ResolveMentionToUserId`, `GetActiveRemindersForUser`, `BuildGitHubContextAsync`, `ReminderHasActivePR`, plus the moved GitHub-activity helpers). Both `show-me` and `show-me-projects` now consume one read path and one GitHub-enrichment implementation. `show-me-command.js` re-exports `FetchUserGitHubActivityAsync` to preserve its historical public surface; behavior is byte-identical (all prior show-me tests pass unchanged).
- **Grouping = model, prioritization = deterministic.** `show-me-projects` sends compact `{id, channel, repos, task}` cards to `WorkspaceAI.ProcessMessageWithJsonResponseAsync` on the **complex** model (`WorkspaceAI.ComplexModelName`, matching the date-extraction convention) with a strict `{projects:[{projectName,client,taskIds}]}` schema. The model only groups/names; the handler ranks via the shared urgency ladder (`overdue > due > scheduled > snoozed`, with an open authored PR boosting deadline-state items) and renders titles from stored reminder data — never the model — so titles stay verbatim (same protection as 1.4.159). A completeness guard appends any reminder the model failed to place under an "Ungrouped" section and logs a warning, so nothing is silently dropped.
- Registered three routes in `src/chat-module.js` mirroring the show-me alias families: primary `show-me-projects @user` / bare self-referential, `what are my projects` / `show me my projects` / `group my tasks` (self), and `what are @user's projects` / `show me @user's projects` / `group @user's tasks` (third-person). `show-me-projects @user` does not collide with the `show-me` route (the show-me pattern requires whitespace after `show-me`).
- Added `tests/show-me-projects-command.test.js` (44 tests, 867 total): routing patterns + show-me non-collision, handler guards (no module / bad mention / empty / wrong-user / terminal-state), the grouping path (single JSON call pinned to the complex model, loading message ordering, rendered output, Ungrouped fallback + warn, graceful AI-failure), and unit coverage for `ExtractReminderTitle`, `GetReminderUrgencyRank`, and `RenderProjects` (project + within-project ordering, dedup of duplicate/out-of-range ids, `[Active PR]` badge, header counts). `npm run build` (checkJs) clean.

## 1.4.160 - 2026-06-02
- Extended thread-context pronoun resolution from "it" as a **direct object** to "it" as the object of a **preposition or communication verb**, so reminders no longer get titled with a literal, unresolved "it". The reported case — `I'll talk to @Alex Rivera more about it tomorrow` — produced a reminder named *"Talk to @Alex Rivera more about it"* because the only existing vague-reference gate (`VAGUE_COMPLETION_IN_THREAD_PATTERN`) matched `do|handle|finish|… + it/this/that` as a direct object. Here the verb is "talk" and the pronoun is the object of "about" (`about it`), so nothing fired and the message fell through to the general LLM with the pronoun intact. This mirrors the earlier "above" hardening (1.4.137/1.4.140/1.4.141) but for the broader "it" reference class.
- Added `VAGUE_REFERENCE_IN_THREAD_PATTERN` in `src/reminders-app-mention-handler.js` covering two shapes the completion pattern omitted: (1) pronoun as the object of a preposition — `about|on|onto|into|with|around|regarding|re|over` + `it|this|that` (e.g. "follow up on it", "look into it", "deal with it", "go over it", "circle back on this"); and (2) pronoun as the direct object of a review/comms verb — `discuss|revisit|review|send|check|address|present|share|update` + `it|this|that` (e.g. "discuss it next week", "send it monday"). `this`/`that` carry a negative-lookahead temporal guard so `on this week` / `into that morning` are treated as **time**, not references; `it` carries no such guard (otherwise `send it monday` would be wrongly dropped, "monday" being the schedule).
- `TryEnrichVagueCompletionFromAboveAsync` now fires when **either** the original completion pattern or the new reference pattern matches, and prepends the **last 1–3 preceding human thread messages** (was: only the single nearest) because the referent of "it" is often a few turns back, not just the immediately prior message. Introduced `#CollectPrecedingHumanThreadMessagesAsync(slackApp, eventInfo, maxCount)` which walks backward skipping bot/empty messages, stops at `maxCount`, and returns them in chronological order (oldest first) so the AI reads the conversation naturally. The single-message helper `#FindPrecedingHumanThreadMessageAsync` (still used by the app-mention demonstrative-"this" path and the "task above" path) now delegates to the collector with `maxCount = 1`, so both lookups share one traversal/filter implementation — behavior-preserving for those callers.
- The enrichment remains gated by the same preconditions as before (thread reply + `SCHEDULING_TRIGGER_PATTERN`) and the LLM still makes the final scheduling/titling decision, so the broader pattern errs toward recall consistent with the module's stated philosophy ("false positives are harmless — the model still decides"). Added 6 tests in `tests/reminders-app-mention-handler.test.js` (72 total): the exact reported Slack scenario (NMI/Stripe thread → "talk … about it tomorrow" resolves from both preceding messages in chronological order), the 3-message cap with bot-message exclusion, a comms-verb direct object ("discuss it next week"), the `it`-has-no-temporal-guard case ("send it monday"), and the `this/that` temporal guard ("about this week" is not a reference).
- **Tooling — ask-self discovery:** added `scripts/ask-self-query.sh` and `scripts/ask-self-ingest.sh` so the global `/ask_self` and `/reingest` Claude commands discover this repo's RAG. Those commands probe a 4-rung detection ladder whose first rung is `scripts/ask-self-query.sh` / `scripts/ask-self-ingest.sh`; sleuth previously had none of the rungs (it uses its own bespoke Gemini/sqlite-vec RAG, not the standard ask-self Python tool), so `/ask_self` fell through to "ask-self does not appear to be set up" and silently degraded to a manual `npm run rag:query`. The query shim bridges to `scripts/rag-query.js --query "<q>" --answer`; the ingest shim bridges to `src/rag/ingest.mjs` and emits the `{ok,total_chunks,db_path,elapsed_seconds,by_source}` JSON `/reingest` parses (it consumes the skill's `--mode`/`--json` flags, which sleuth's full-rebuild ingester does not take, and honors `--no-prs` by unsetting `SLEUTH_RAG_GITHUB_PAT`). Both resolve `GOOGLE_API_KEY` automatically (env var → `~/secrets/client-a-rag.env` → Google Secret Manager via `gcloud secrets versions access latest --secret=client-b-gemini-api-key`, override with `ASK_SELF_GEMINI_SECRET`) and guard empty-array expansion for bash 3.2 (macOS default) under `set -u`.
- **Safety:** the ingest shim only ever rebuilds the internal `data/rag/sleuth-rag.sqlite` (same destroy+rewrite as `npm run rag:ingest`); it never reads `config/remote-rag-projects.json` or contacts a remote RAG agent, so the `ask-code` external/remote RAG and the runtime `ask-self` Slack command (read-only DB consumer) are unaffected. Verified end-to-end: success path emits valid JSON with `ok:true, total_chunks:508` (exit 0); the no-key failure path emits valid JSON with `ok:false` and a clear error (exit 1). Documented both shims in `AGENTS.md`.
- **Shim tests + hardening:** added `tests/ask-self-shims.test.js` (9 tests, 823 total) — the first coverage for the developer-facing RAG CLI layer, which previously had none (only `src/rag` helpers/tenancy and the `ask-code` path were tested). It runs the shims as real child processes with a fake `node`/`gcloud` on `PATH` (so no Gemini calls): query shim — no-question (exit 2), no-key (exit 1), question-forwarded-as-single-`--query`, and flag+value stay paired; ingest shim — valid success JSON parsed from the ingester log, valid failure JSON on a failed ingest, valid failure JSON on no-key, and `--no-prs` unsetting `SLEUTH_RAG_GITHUB_PAT` (with a control proving it is otherwise preserved). Writing the tests surfaced two query-shim fixes: (1) the first arg is now treated as the whole question with the rest forwarded verbatim (previously positional words were joined and flags split out, which would split a flag from its value, e.g. `--as-of 2026-01-01`); (2) input validation (no-question → exit 2) now runs before key resolution so a missing question isn't masked by a missing key.

## 1.4.159 - 2026-06-02
- Fixed `show-me` task titles appearing to be hallucinated. The SYSTEM_INSTRUCTIONS said "Task title **or brief summary**", which gave the LLM license to rephrase reminder names instead of quoting them verbatim — making real reminders look invented. Changed the format directive to `[exact task title from the reminder — do not paraphrase or invent]` with an explicit `CRITICAL` prohibition against inventing, shortening, or rephrasing task names. The underlying reminders were always real (the command passes actual `ReminderMessageText` to the LLM); only the displayed title could drift.

## 1.4.158 - 2026-06-01
- Fixed `show-me` output showing raw Slack user IDs (e.g. `@U000EXAMPLE1`) instead of resolved display names. The LLM response was already passing through `NormalizeModelMarkdownForSlack` but not `NormalizeUserMentionsToMrkdwn`. Added the missing call so bare `@U…` tokens are converted to `<@U…>` mrkdwn before posting, which Slack then renders as clickable mentions.
- Added `for-today` as an alias for `show-me`. Accepts both `for-today @user` (third-person, same behaviour as `show-me @user`) and bare `for today` / `for-today` with no mention (self-referential, same as `what are my tasks?`). The pattern `/^for[-\s]today(?:\s+(<@[UW][^>]+>))?/i` handles both forms in a single route; when no mention is captured it falls back to `<@{caller}>`. Registered in `src/chat-module.js` between the third-person `show-me` alias and the `run daily digest` route.

## 1.4.157 - 2026-06-01
- Added a weekly false-positive report that automatically surfaces patterns from trashed reminders and posts LLM-generated prompt-improvement suggestions to `REMINDER_CHANNEL_NAME`.
- `RemindersModule` now starts a `#StartWeeklyReportScheduler()` alongside the daily digest on startup. The scheduler fires every Monday at 08:00 workspace timezone (computed with the same `DateUtils.GetCurrentDateInTimeZone` / `GetTimeZoneOffsetInMinutes` pattern as the daily digest). A `setTimeout` self-reschedules in the `finally` block so the timer survives errors.
- `#RunWeeklyTrashedExamplesReportAsync()` reads only **new** entries from `{workspace}_trashed_examples.jsonl` using a line-count cursor stored in `{workspace}_trashed_examples_cursor.json`. If there are no new entries since the last run the method returns silently without posting. The cursor is advanced only after a successful Slack post, so a failed run retries next week with the same examples rather than silently losing them.
- New AI pipeline method `RemindersAIPipeline.AnalyzeTrashedExamplesAsync(entries)` formats the trashed examples as a numbered list and calls `ProcessMessageWithTextResponseAsync` with the complex workspace model and the new `data/static/ai/trashed-examples-instructions.md` system prompt. The prompt asks the LLM to identify 1–3 patterns that caused false positives and suggest concrete new exclusion rules or "pay attention to" clarifications phrased to match the style of `reminders-instructions.md`.
- The Slack report header reads: `*Weekly false-positive reminder report* — N trashed reminders since last week` followed by the LLM analysis. Non-deterministic only in the LLM synthesis step; the underlying JSONL data is the stable ground truth.

## 1.4.156 - 2026-06-01
- Added false-positive training example capture on wastebasket (🗑️) reaction. When a user trashes a reminder feedback message, Sleuth now records the triggering message alongside the scheduled task text as a labelled "should be ignore" example for future prompt improvement.
- `RemindersReactionHandler.#HandleWastebasketReactionAsync` calls the new `#TrySaveTrashedExamplesAsync` helper before the delete sequence. The helper resolves the original message text via `SlackApp.GetConversationMessagesAsync` (using `OriginalThreadTs` for thread replies, `OriginalMessageID` for top-level messages), then calls the injected `SaveTrashedExampleAsync` callback. All errors are caught and logged as warnings so a save failure never blocks the delete.
- `RemindersModule` wires `SaveTrashedExampleAsync` into the reaction handler dependency bag. The implementation appends one JSON object per line to `data/runtime/reminders/{workspace}_trashed_examples.jsonl` using `fs.appendFile` — the append is atomic so a partial write cannot corrupt earlier entries and no read-parse-rewrite cycle is needed.
- Each entry contains: `TrashedAt` (ISO timestamp), `OriginalMessageText` (raw Slack message or `null` if fetch failed), `OriginalChannelID`, `OriginalMessageID`, `OriginalSenderID`, `TrashedByUserID`, and `ScheduledReminderText` (the reminder post text, showing what Sleuth incorrectly scheduled).
- The JSONL file is per-workspace under the existing `data/runtime/reminders/` directory (no new directories). The format is intentionally line-oriented so it can be reviewed with standard tools (`jq`, `grep`) and fed directly into future prompt-improvement workflows.

## 1.4.155 - 2026-06-01
- Strengthened the `AnalyzeMessageForRemindersAsync` system prompt (`data/static/ai/reminders-instructions.md`) to prevent false-positive scheduling when temporal language appears only in past-tense or completed-action context.
- Added three new rules to the "pay attention to" section: (1) temporal words like `today`, `tonight`, `this morning` are `scheduling triggers` only when the surrounding language is **forward-looking** — when they appear in past-tense descriptions of completed or failed actions they are descriptive, not deadlines; (2) the phrase `for now` ("at the moment / temporarily") is never a `scheduling trigger`; (3) informal handoff/FYI messages with conditional language (`if you have time`, `passing this along`) carry no firm commitment and should be `ignore`.
- Added two new `exclusion language` bullets: past-tense completed/failed-action reports where a time word describes when something happened rather than when future work is due (e.g., `I ran out of time today`); and informal delegation messages with no firm deadline (e.g., `I wanted to make sure you had this so you can work on it if you have time`).
- Root cause: a message from a team member saying "I ran out of time today … I just stuck all of it in 1 issue for now" triggered auto-scheduling because the deterministic `HasSchedulingTrigger` gate matched `today` (by design — that gate errs toward false positives so the LLM can decide), but the LLM then scheduled instead of ignoring. The triage diagnostic (run retroactively via `:wrench:`) correctly said `ignore`; the scheduling LLM did not. These new rules close the gap between the two prompts for this class of message.

## 1.4.154 - 2026-06-01
- Phase 3 of the show-me feature: full synthesis hardening — stronger prompt, `[Active PR]` cross-reference annotation, and explicit fallback wording.
- Updated `SYSTEM_INSTRUCTIONS` in `src/chat-commands/show-me-command.js`: reframed opening to "Given this user's GitHub activity last week (when provided) and their current open reminders, recommend which 3 items they should tackle first today". Added rule 1 as the highest-priority case: reminder overdue or due today AND marked `[Active PR]` — in-flight work with a deadline. Added explicit wording guidance: cite GitHub context in the explanation when it supports the ranking; when GitHub activity is not provided, reason from reminder signals only and do not reference GitHub.
- Added `[Active PR]` annotation to reminder prompt lines: after the GitHub activity fetch, extract PR URLs from `OpenPRs` entries (format: `"Title (url)"`), build a URL set, and mark each reminder whose `GitHubUrls` contains a matching URL with `[Active PR]`. Only authored open PRs trigger the annotation; review-requested PRs do not.
- Added 4 Phase 3 regression tests in `tests/show-me-command.test.js` (65 total): `[Active PR]` annotation present when GitHub URL matches; no annotation when URL doesn't overlap; no annotation in reminder-only fallback path; annotation only on authored-PR reminders, not review-requested ones.

- Phase 2 of the show-me feature: GitHub activity enrichment via `GITHUB_USER_MAP` workspace field.
- Added `GITHUB_USER_MAP` optional workspace field — a JSON string object mapping Slack user IDs to GitHub usernames (e.g. `{"U000EXAMPLE1": "octo-dev"}`). Validated in `src/workspaces.js` alongside existing `GITHUB_PAT` validation: must be valid JSON, must be a non-null object, and every value must be a non-empty string. Added to `config/workspace-template.json` with a comment and to both request examples in `docs/web-api.md`.
- Added `FetchUserGitHubActivityAsync(pat, username, days = 7)` in `src/chat-commands/show-me-command.js`: fires three parallel requests using the existing `GITHUB_PAT` — open PRs authored (`/search/issues?q=is:pr+author:…`), PRs awaiting review (`/search/issues?q=is:pr+review-requested:…`), and recent push events (`/users/…/events`) filtered to `PushEvent` entries within the lookback window and deduplicated by repo. Uses `AbortController` timeout (5 s) consistent with the pattern in `github-actions-startup-summary.js`.
- Added `LookUpGitHubUsername(workspaceInfo, slackUserId)` helper: safely parses `GITHUB_USER_MAP` and returns the mapped GitHub username or `null`.
- Extended `HandleShowMeCommandAsync` to attempt enrichment when both `GITHUB_PAT` and a mapped GitHub username are available. If enrichment succeeds, a `GitHub activity (last 7 days)` section is appended to the AI prompt listing open PRs, review-requested PRs, and repos pushed to. Falls back silently to Phase 1 behavior (reminder-only prompt) when either credential is absent, the user is unmapped, or the fetch fails. All fetch errors are logged as warnings, not errors, and never surface to the user.
- Expanded `tests/show-me-command.test.js` from 51 to 61 tests: `HandleShowMeCommandAsync — GitHub activity enrichment` (6 tests covering no-map, no-PAT, unmapped user, successful enrichment, all-null response, and fetch-throw fallback) and `FetchUserGitHubActivityAsync` unit tests (4 tests covering all-fail null return, partial success, cutoff date filtering, and repo deduplication).

## 1.4.151 - 2026-06-01
- Added `@Sleuth show-me @user` — a new chat command that pulls all open reminders assigned to a tagged Slack user and asks `WorkspaceAI` to rank the top three priorities for today. Ranking prompt prefers `overdue > GitHub-linked + due > due today > scheduled soon > snoozed`, with a one-sentence rationale per item. Accepts both the shorthand `show-me <@user>` and the longhand `show-me what tasks <@user> should work on today`. No admin gate — any workspace member can query any user; no credentials are exposed.
- Handler lives in `src/chat-commands/show-me-command.js`. Reads reminders via the existing `RemindersModule.GetAllReminders()` filtered to `AssigneeID === targetUserId` and the active-state set (`scheduled`, `due`, `overdue`, `snoozed`) — no new persistence or indexes. `AssigneeID` already defaults to the sender at creation time, so self-assigned and explicitly delegated reminders are both covered without the broader noise of `GetRemindersInvolvingUserID`. Posts a loading message before the AI call; falls back gracefully with a user-facing error reply if the AI call throws.
- Route registered in `src/chat-module.js` `#RegisterCommandRoutes()` using pattern `/^show-me\s+(?:what\s+tasks?\s+)?(<@[UW][^>]+>)(?:\s+.*)?$/i`. Sits above the `run daily digest` route; resolves cleanly against the existing `show-channel-model` route with no overlap.
- Added `tests/show-me-command.test.js` (23 tests): routing pattern positive and negative cases; guard cases for null `RemindersModule` and unparseable mentions; empty-result path (no reminders, terminal-state reminders, reminders for a different user — all skip the AI); AI ranking path (correct user filter, GitHub URLs in prompt, today's date in prompt, loading message precedes AI call, AI response is the final post); AI failure path (graceful error reply, no re-throw).

## 1.4.152 - 2026-06-01
- Added two natural-language alias routes for `show-me`, both delegating to the same `HandleShowMeCommandAsync` handler with no changes to the handler itself.
- Self-referential alias: `what are my tasks?` / `what's my tasks` / `what is my task` / `show me my tasks` / `what should I work on today` / `what should I do today` / `what should I focus on today`. The route synthesises `<@{caller.user}>` as the target mention so users can ask about their own queue without knowing the `show-me @user` syntax.
- Third-person alias: `what are @user tasks` / `what are @user's tasks` / `what is @user's task` / `show me @user's tasks` / `what should @user work on today` / `what should @user do today` / `what should @user focus on today`. Captures the `<@UID>` mention across three pattern alternations; the handler receives whichever group matched via `ArgM1 || ArgM2 || ArgM3`.
- Expanded `tests/show-me-command.test.js` from 23 to 51 tests: added `"my tasks" self-referential routing pattern` (9 positive, 4 negative, 1 handler integration) and `"what are @user tasks" third-person routing pattern` (9 positive, 4 negative, 1 handler integration) suites.

## 1.4.150 - 2026-05-29
- Fixed a false-positive past-time warning in the manual `:alarm_clock:` reminder flow. When the reaction had to synthesize a fallback schedule because the source message contained no explicit date/time, Sleuth could still inherit a `wasAdjustedForward` flag from the fallback extraction and tell the user their requested time was in the past even though they never requested one. Synthetic `:alarm_clock:` fallbacks now suppress that warning while keeping the existing warning for real user-authored past-time phrases.
- Added regression coverage in `tests/reminders-integration.test.js` for the exact manual-reaction case where the fallback extraction lands on the current wall-clock minute and would previously post the misleading "requested time was in the past" note.

## 1.4.149 - 2026-05-28
- Extended `STARTUP_MESSAGE_INCLUDE_CHANGELOG` (added in 1.4.148) so it now also gates the delayed GitHub Actions CI run summary follow-up. Previously `GITHUB_ACTIONS_REPO` alone was sufficient to trigger the ~4-second-delayed second Slack post showing the latest workflow run status; that post now additionally requires the same verbose-extras opt-in as the changelog excerpt. Workspaces that want the compact startup post now get a compact startup post — no changelog body, no CI follow-up. Workspaces that have already configured `GITHUB_ACTIONS_REPO` will lose the CI follow-up until they also opt into `STARTUP_MESSAGE_INCLUDE_CHANGELOG`. The flag name is intentionally preserved despite the broader scope — the workspace field hadn't shipped anywhere yet, but the convention of treating the field as the umbrella verbose-startup switch is more durable than another rename round-trip.
- Gating implemented at `src/app.js` by reusing the `IncludeChangelog` boolean already computed at the top of each per-workspace loop iteration. `ScheduleStartupGitHubActionsSummaryFollowUp(...)` is now wrapped in `if(IncludeChangelog) { ... }`. No new helper introduced — both call sites consume the existing `ShouldIncludeStartupChangelog(WorkspaceInfo)` predicate from `src/startup-message.js`, whose JSDoc has been updated to explain that despite the function name (kept for compatibility with the `STARTUP_MESSAGE_INCLUDE_CHANGELOG` field), it gates BOTH verbose extras.
- Updated `src/workspaces.js` typedef, `config/workspace-template.json` (`_comments` entry), and `README.md` workspace-configuration section so all three explicitly call out the dual-gating behavior and the "name preserved despite broader scope" rationale. The existing 23 tests in `tests/startup-message.test.js` already cover the four-row truth table for `ShouldIncludeStartupChangelog`; no new tests were added because the helper itself is unchanged — only its consumer count went from 1 to 2.
## 1.4.148 - 2026-05-28
- Split the startup Slack post into compact-by-default + opt-in changelog excerpt. `STARTUP_MESSAGE: 'true' | 'yes'` now posts only the deploy header — `Sleuth has been updated to <version> from the <branch> branch` — followed by `Sleuth is shutting down.` on shutdown. To also receive the changelog excerpt body (the behavior workspaces saw before this change), workspace owners must explicitly set the new optional `STARTUP_MESSAGE_INCLUDE_CHANGELOG: 'true' | 'yes'` field. **Behavior change for existing workspaces:** any workspace that had `STARTUP_MESSAGE` set will see the compact startup post and stop receiving the changelog excerpt until the new field is opted in. Shutdown post unchanged.
- The new field is subordinate to `STARTUP_MESSAGE` — if the outer flag is not truthy, the changelog flag has no effect. Sleuth never posts the changelog excerpt without the deploy header above it, so a typo on `STARTUP_MESSAGE` cannot silently leak the excerpt on its own. The subordination invariant is asserted explicitly in `tests/startup-message.test.js` as the four-row truth table (off/off, on/off, on/on, off/on).
- Factored the decision logic into `src/startup-message.js` — three small pure helpers (`IsTruthyFlag`, `ShouldPostStartupMessage`, `ShouldIncludeStartupChangelog`) — so the per-workspace gating is unit-testable without booting the `src/app.js` IIFE. `PostStartupNotificationAsync` in `src/app.js` now imports those helpers and also short-circuits the `CHANGELOG.md` file read entirely when no workspace in the current process has opted in (small I/O win on every startup for the common case).
- Updated `config/workspace-template.json` (new field plus updated `_comments` block), `README.md` (workspace configuration section now documents both flags and the subordination rule), and the `WorkspaceInfo` JSDoc typedef in `src/workspaces.js`. The new field is intentionally not added to the validation block — like `STARTUP_MESSAGE`, it is a freeform string read with case-insensitive `'true' | 'yes'` matching, so unrecognized values silently mean "off" rather than fail-fast (matches the existing convention for boolean-string workspace flags).
- Added `tests/startup-message.test.js` (23 tests): `IsTruthyFlag` parameterized cases (`true`/`yes`/whitespace/case-insensitive accepted; `no`/`false`/`1`/`on`/`truthy`/empty/non-strings rejected); `ShouldPostStartupMessage` (truthy/missing/non-truthy/null-workspace); and the full subordination truth table.

## 1.4.147 - 2026-05-28
- Fixed the startup-message changelog excerpt cutting mid-word. The previous `ChangelogContent.substring(0, 300)` sliced the 1.4.144 entry at "...spread **last** so a calle" — visually indistinguishable from a bug. Switched to a word-budget cut (`SlackFormatUtils.TruncateToWords(content, 50)`) that ends on a clean whitespace boundary, and appended a horizontal-ellipsis character ("…") so the truncation reads as intentional. The 50-word budget is tuned to roughly match the prior visual length; the helper preserves original newline structure so markdown headings (`## 1.4.x`) still render on their own line in Slack. Word boundaries treat tokens like `` `#MakeScheduledReminder` `` or `**State**` as one indivisible unit, so the cut can no longer land mid-pair or inside an inline-code span.
- Added `SlackFormatUtils.TruncateToWords(ArgText, ArgMaxWords)` as a general-purpose helper alongside the existing markdown utilities — kept narrow (no smart-cut on sentence end, no max-character secondary cap) on the principle that the simplest correct version of this can be extended later if a second consumer needs different behavior. Added 7 tests in `tests/slack-format-utils.test.js` covering: under-budget passthrough, clean cut at the word boundary, no mid-word slice of bold/backtick tokens, newline preservation inside the kept prefix, no ellipsis when only trailing whitespace remains, empty/null/zero-budget safety, and whitespace-only input.

## 1.4.146 - 2026-05-28
- Promoted the reminder FSM contract from prose to structural enforcement. The three rules in `ARCHITECTURE.md` § *Reminder FSM and Write-Path Contract* (creation through `#MakeScheduledReminder`, scheduling via `#TryScheduleRemindersAsync`, state changes via `#TransitionReminderState`) were previously enforced only by JSDoc, comments, and reviewer discipline — nothing prevented a single edit from reintroducing a direct `reminder.State = X` assignment or a hand-rolled `ReminderInfo` literal somewhere else in `src/reminders-module.js`. Added `scripts/validate-fsm-invariants.js` (and `npm run validate:fsm`) which scans `src/reminders-module.js` for two forbidden patterns and exits non-zero on any unannotated violation: (1) direct `.State = ` assignments outside `#TransitionReminderState` or `#MakeScheduledReminder`, and (2) inline `/** @type {ReminderInfo} */ ({ ... })` casts outside `#MakeScheduledReminder`. Legitimate legacy backfill paths in `#LoadRemindersAsync` (un-stated reminders → `Scheduled`, deprecated `Due` → `Overdue`) are annotated with a `// FSM-BACKFILL-OK` inline pragma so each exception is explicit and reviewable.
- Added `tests/validate-fsm-invariants.test.js` (6 tests) proving the validator actually catches the patterns it claims to catch — without this a regression that broke the validator would silently always pass: factory + transition cases exit 0; a synthetic `#SomeNewBypass(...)` assigning `.State` exits 1; a synthetic `#ShortcutScheduler` inline-casting `ReminderInfo` exits 1; the `FSM-BACKFILL-OK` pragma silences a violation; `===` and `==` comparisons against `State` are not flagged as assignments; the real `src/reminders-module.js` passes. Each test runs the validator as a child process against a tempdir mock repo so the script's CLI behavior (exit codes, stderr output, method-context tracking) is exercised end to end.
- Documented the shared-regex coupling that the 1.4.145 module-level lift exposed. The public `RemindersAppMentionHandler.HasSchedulingTrigger` wrapper now carries an explicit "shared-regex coupling" JSDoc warning: tightening it to fix a recall gap in the auto-scheduling gate will also tighten the @-mention task-assignment path (`OnAppMentionAsync`) and the vague-completion enrichment precondition (`TryEnrichVagueCompletionFromAboveAsync`), because all three consume the same `SCHEDULING_TRIGGER_PATTERN` constant. Added a sibling inline comment at the `OnAppMentionAsync` call site so the coupling is visible at both ends. If a future change needs gate-specific behavior, the convention is to factor a new pattern rather than mutate the shared one.
- Updated `AGENTS.md` § *Reminder FSM invariants* to point future contributors at `npm run validate:fsm` and the `// FSM-BACKFILL-OK` pragma convention. The rules themselves are unchanged.

## 1.4.145 - 2026-05-28
- Repaired the `npm run build` regression introduced in 1.4.144 (`8eb26e3`). The hardening pass added new prose JSDoc blocks **above** the existing `@param`/`@returns` blocks on `#TryScheduleRemindersAsync` and `#QueueReminderAsync`, leaving two stacked `/** */` comments per method. TypeScript's `checkJs` reads only the closest JSDoc block, so the parameter types collapsed to implicit `any` and the build broke with 8 errors (`TS7006` × 7 plus `TS2339` on `SkipListSync`). Folded the two blocks per method into a single JSDoc with the FSM-gateway prose at the top and `@param` annotations at the bottom. Also added the previously undocumented `ArgThreadTs` parameter to `#TryScheduleRemindersAsync`'s JSDoc — it was already typed inline but invisible to TS without a corresponding `@param`.
- Closed two recall and false-positive holes in the temporal-cue prefilter added in 1.4.142 (`RemindersAppMentionHandler#HasSchedulingTrigger`). The previous regex was a closed vocabulary (`by|before|until|due|deadline|eod|cob|end of|tomorrow|today|<weekdays>|next week|this week|tonight|morning|afternoon|evening|noon|midnight|\d{1,2}(?::\d{2})?\s*(?:am|pm)?`) which silently dropped legitimate reminder requests like `Send the contract on May 30`, `meeting June 1st`, `the 15th`, `in 2 hours`, `next month`, and `after the call` — and simultaneously *let through* false positives like `issue #15`, `version 12`, `chapter 3`, and `page 4` because the bare-digit arm made the am/pm suffix optional. (1) Recall: added calendar dates (`Jan|January..Dec|December` followed by `\d{1,2}(?:st|nd|rd|th)?`), ordinal day-of-month (`the 15th`), relative offsets (`in (a|an|N) min/hr/hour/day/week/month[s]`), `next/this/last` extended from `week` only to also cover `month|year|<weekday>|morning|afternoon|evening`, and the keywords `after`, `asap`, `yesterday`. (2) False positives: dropped the bare-digit alternative entirely — time arms now require either `hh:mm` (with optional am/pm) or `h[am|pm]`, so `issue #15` no longer trips the gate. (3) Lifted the pattern to a `SCHEDULING_TRIGGER_PATTERN` module constant programmatically composed from a named-piece array, replacing the long inline regex. The two stricter sibling matchers in the same file (`#HasStrongSchedulingTrigger`, `#HasExplicitScheduleForDefaulting`) remain unchanged — they intentionally enforce stricter rules for the @-mention task-assignment path and the "task above" defaulting path.
- `#lessonslearned`: when a refactor's stated purpose is to centralize a safety invariant, ship the bypass-detection test *in the same commit* as the new factory — not in the follow-up hardening commit. The original `#MakeScheduledReminder` extraction (1.4.143, `e3f90b4`) spread caller fields LAST and invariants FIRST, meaning a caller passing `State` or `IgnoreSnooze` in `ArgFields` would silently override the FSM invariants the factory was meant to enforce. The bug existed for ~9 minutes before 1.4.144 (`8eb26e3`) fixed the order and added the regression tests that would have caught it. A "centralization refactor" without bypass-detection tests is just an opportunity to move a bug from many sites to one.

## 1.4.144 - 2026-05-28
- Hardened the reminder FSM against future write-path bypasses with guard comments, documentation, and regression tests. (1) Fixed `#MakeScheduledReminder` field-order: invariants (`State`, `IgnoreSnooze`, `ReminderID`, `CreatedOn`) now spread **last** so a caller that accidentally passes them in `ArgFields` cannot silently override the FSM entry state. (2) Added a guard-comment block to `#QueueReminderAsync` listing the two approved callers and prohibiting direct construction of `ReminderInfo` objects without the factory. (3) Added a gateway-comment block to `#TryScheduleRemindersAsync` listing the pipeline steps that would be skipped by a bypass (AI analysis, dedup, date extraction, channel resolution, `#MakeScheduledReminder`). (4) Added a comment to the injected `TryScheduleRemindersAsync` callback explaining why it is injected rather than called directly. (5) Added a symmetric note to `RemindersAppMentionHandler.#TryScheduleRemindersAsync` field. (6) Replaced the single-sentence FSM note in `ARCHITECTURE.md` with a three-row enforcement-point table, approved-caller list, and decision rules for adding new reminder behavior. (7) Added three numbered FSM rules to `AGENTS.md` alongside the existing snooze guard. (8) Added `tests/reminders-fsm-invariants.test.js` (13 tests) covering: `ReminderState` is frozen; `CreateReminderFromListRowAsync` produces correct `State`/`IgnoreSnooze`/UUID/`CreatedOn` in-memory and in persisted JSON; two calls produce distinct `ReminderID`s; `CreatedOn` is set at call time; the temporal prefilter (1.4.142) blocks the LLM for messages with no temporal language and passes it through for messages that have one.

## 1.4.143 - 2026-05-28
- Refactored `ReminderInfo` construction to eliminate DRY/SOLID violations caused by multiple write paths. Two inline object literals in `CreateReminderFromListRowAsync` and `#TryScheduleRemindersAsync` each manually set the same four FSM entry-point invariants (`ReminderID`, `CreatedOn`, `State: Scheduled`, `IgnoreSnooze: false`). Extracted a `#MakeScheduledReminder(fields)` private factory that owns those invariants; both creation sites now spread their caller-specific fields into it. This ensures the FSM initial-state contract is enforced in exactly one place — any future scheduling path or new `ReminderInfo` field can be updated there without hunting across the file. Consistent with the existing `#TransitionReminderState` pattern, which already centralises all mid-lifecycle state changes.

## 1.4.142 - 2026-05-28
- Fixed a hallucination path where `RemindersModule#OnMessageAsync` called the LLM analyzer for messages that contained no temporal language at all. Without any time reference (tomorrow, tonight, EOD, a specific day/time, etc.) the model must invent both the scheduling trigger and the task title, which caused a fabricated reminder like "I need to work on 'On-going Project: X' by tomorrow" to be created from an informational file-share message ("Here's the plugin zip. Please note I'm not done testing and polishing it."). Added a `HasSchedulingTrigger()` public method to `RemindersAppMentionHandler` (wrapping the existing private `#HasSchedulingTrigger`) and a corresponding prefilter gate in `#OnMessageAsync` that returns early when no temporal keyword is present — before the LLM is ever called. This is consistent with the same guard already applied in `TryEnrichVagueCompletionFromAboveAsync`. The triage diagnostic correctly reported "ignore / lacks actionable language or a specific scheduling trigger" for the same message, confirming the LLM judgment was correct and the issue was the absence of a deterministic gate protecting the general auto-scheduling path.

## 1.4.141 - 2026-05-27
- When a thread reply contains a vague self-assignment phrase ("will do it at 10pm pt", "I'll handle it tomorrow", "gonna take care of it tonight") combined with a scheduling trigger, Sleuth now prepends the nearest preceding human message to the AI analysis context so the reminder title reflects the actual task rather than the placeholder pronoun. Previously, "@noel.saw will do it at 10pm pt" produced a reminder titled "Do it"; now the AI sees the full thread context and names the reminder after the real task. Implemented via a new `VAGUE_COMPLETION_IN_THREAD_PATTERN` constant and `TryEnrichVagueCompletionFromAboveAsync` method in `RemindersAppMentionHandler`, called from `RemindersModule#OnMessageAsync` between the existing "do above" shorthand intercept and the general AI analysis pass. Falls through gracefully when no schedule is detected, no preceding message exists, or the AI decides not to schedule.

## 1.4.140 - 2026-05-27
- Fixed "do above" and sibling shorthand phrases not working when `@Sleuth` is not mentioned in the message. The shorthand route added in 1.4.137 was registered in `RemindersAppMentionHandler` which only fires for `app_mention` events. Messages like "@Alex Rivera can you do above" have no `@Sleuth` mention, so they routed through `RemindersModule#OnMessageAsync` → AI analysis → "ignore" (no scheduling trigger found). Fixed by extracting the shorthand regex to a shared module constant (`TASK_ABOVE_SHORTHAND_PATTERN`), adding a public `TryHandleTaskAboveShorthandAsync` method to `RemindersAppMentionHandler`, and calling it from `#OnMessageAsync` before general AI analysis whenever the event is a thread reply. The deterministic path now intercepts these messages first, resolves the source task, and schedules the reminder — no AI involvement needed.

## 1.4.139 - 2026-05-27
- The `ask-self` RAG synthesis pipeline now injects the live `CHANGELOG.md` (most recent 50 version entries, read directly from disk) into the complex model's context immediately after the SQLite KNN chunks. Previously the model could only see indexed changelog chunks, which lag behind every un-ingested change. The live injection closes that gap without requiring a `rag:ingest` run and adds negligible latency (single synchronous file read). The returned `hits` and `context` fields in `queryRagAsync` remain pure SQLite results; only the synthesized `answer` incorporates the live changelog.

## 1.4.138 - 2026-05-27
- Fixed reminder hallucination triggered by attachment-only Slack messages (image/file shares with no caption text). `RemindersModule#OnMessageAsync` now returns early when `ArgEventInfo.text` is empty or whitespace-only, preventing empty-string context from reaching the AI analyzer. Previously, `file_share` events passed the subtype filter and the `text`-field type check with `text: ""`, causing the LLM to invent unrelated task descriptions (e.g. "Prepare the Q3 financial report") from zero context.

## 1.4.137 - 2026-05-27
- Broadened the "task above" reminder trigger to accept natural delegation shorthand in addition to the formal "make a Sleuth reminder for @user based on task above" syntax. Sleuth now deterministically catches phrases like "do above", "do the above", "handle above", "complete the above", "take care of above", "tackle above", "finish above", "follow up on above", and "knock out above" — with or without an optional leading or trailing @mention and schedule phrase.
- When no explicit @user is provided in a shorthand "do above" command, Sleuth now extracts the target @mention from the message text (looking for the first non-bot, non-sender mention that appears before the word "above"), falling back to the command sender if none is found.
- Fixed the "task above" reminder body: the AI-constructed task description now receives the source task's verbatim text quoted explicitly ("Create a reminder for @user to handle this task: '...'") rather than a generic "please remind to follow up on the task above" prompt. This prevents the LLM from naming the reminder after the delegation shorthand (e.g. "Do above") instead of the actual task content.

## 1.4.136 - 2026-05-26
- Hardened the `ask-code <project> <question>` Slack command against one specific upstream contract drift from remote RAG agents. Sleuth still expects the documented `{ answer: "..." }` response shape, but if a remote agent mistakenly serializes a second JSON payload into the `answer` string (for example `{"answer":"{...}"}`), `src/chat-commands/ask-code-command.js` now unwraps that nested layer once and posts the inner answer text instead of dumping raw JSON into Slack.
- The hardening is intentionally narrow: Sleuth only unwraps nested payloads when the parsed inner object has its own string `answer` field, and it logs a warning so operators can still see that the remote harness drifted from contract.
- Added regression coverage in `tests/chat-module.integration.test.js` for both the normal `ask-code` response shape and the malformed nested-JSON `answer` case that previously rendered as raw braces and `"answer": ...` in Slack.

## 1.4.135 - 2026-05-26
- Stopped a reminder-action hallucination path in Slack app mentions. When a user asks freeform chat to create or schedule a reminder and no deterministic reminder handler actually executed it, `ChatModule` now posts an explicit "I didn't create a reminder" guidance message instead of falling through to the LLM, which could previously invent success text like "I've set a reminder..." with no reminder queued.
- Added a real thread command for turning the message above into a reminder: `@Sleuth AI make a Sleuth reminder for @user based on task above`. `RemindersAppMentionHandler` now claims that route deterministically, resolves the nearest preceding human message in the thread, and schedules the reminder against the source message instead of letting the request drift into generic chat.
- The new thread command defaults to `tomorrow at 8:00 AM` only when neither the source task nor the command text contains a scheduling phrase. If the task above already includes a due-time phrase, Sleuth keeps that timing instead of overwriting it with the default.
- Hardened the general chat prompt against side-effect bluffing: `data/static/ai/chat-instructions.md` now explicitly forbids claiming that Sleuth created, updated, deleted, searched, sent, or changed anything unless the action was already executed by a deterministic tool path and the reply is reporting the confirmed result.
- Added regression coverage for the new route and the no-fake-success fallback in `tests/reminders-app-mention-handler.test.js`, `tests/reminders-integration.test.js`, `tests/chat-module.integration.test.js`, and `tests/chat-module.test.js`.

## 1.4.134 - 2026-05-26
