- Added `ask-code <project> <query>` Slack command. Tenancy-gated (Neochrome only). Proxies questions to registered remote ask-self RAG agents via HTTP, returning synthesized answers in-thread. Project registry lives in `config/remote-rag-projects.json`; secrets resolved from env vars (never stored in config). See `ASKCODE-INTEGRATION_COPY.md` for the checked-in setup guide copy.
- Added `rag_agent.py` to the ask-self repo — a Flask HTTP server wrapping `ask_self()` for remote querying. Binds to `127.0.0.1` by default; intended for nginx proxy. Shared-secret auth via `Authorization: Bearer` header (not body), 30s timeout, `/health` endpoint for monitoring.
- Fixed (pre-deploy): moved auth secret from POST body to `Authorization: Bearer` header so it doesn't appear in nginx/Flask logs. Bumped integration spec to v1.1. Also: dropped hardcoded dev-machine `ASK_SELF_PATH` default in wrapper script template (fail-fast instead), made nginx IP restriction the default, raised `proxy_read_timeout` to 90s, added ask-self server clone step and Gemini 429 troubleshooting to `ASKCODE-INTEGRATION_COPY.md`.

## 1.4.133 - 2026-05-22
- Fixed reminder task titles showing raw Slack user IDs like `@U000EXAMPLE1` instead of resolved display names. The AI sometimes returns plain `@U…` mentions; scheduling feedback and stored `Key task(s)` bullets now normalize those to Slack mrkdwn `<@U…>` tokens via `SlackFormatUtils.NormalizeUserMentionsToMrkdwn()` before posting.

## 1.4.132 - 2026-05-21
- Fixed a crash-loop where a single workspace with a missing or invalid AI API key would take down the entire app. The workspace loader in `app.js` now catches errors from `LoadWorkspaceInfoByNameAsync` per-workspace and skips the offending workspace with an error log, leaving all other workspaces unaffected.
- Clarified the agent runbook for the local `ask-self` / `rag:query` RAG flow: `AGENTS.md` now points future sessions to `~/secrets/client-a-rag.env` for `GOOGLE_API_KEY` and explicitly requires `set -a; source ~/secrets/client-a-rag.env; set +a` instead of a bare `source`, since the env file is plain `KEY=value` and otherwise leaves the Gemini key unexported.

## 1.4.131 - 2026-05-20
- Relaxed the workspace Web API / persistence contract so `POST /workspace` no longer hard-requires `OPENAI_API_KEY`. A workspace now needs at least one supported AI provider key: `OPENAI_API_KEY`, `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, or `GEMINI_API_KEY`. This unblocks Anthropic-only and Gemini-only sign-up flows from external provisioners like the Gravity Forms WordPress plugin.
- Added normalization for optional AI-key fields in `src/workspaces.js`. Blank `OPENAI_API_KEY`, `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, and `GEMINI_API_KEY` values are trimmed and removed before validation/persistence, so HTML forms and low-code integrations can submit one chosen provider key without brittle conditional payload shaping.
- Made `WorkspaceAI` provider-aware when picking built-in fallback models. If no persisted `DEFAULT_MODEL_NAME` / `COMPLEX_MODEL_NAME` is set, workspaces with an OpenAI key keep the existing `gpt-4o-mini` / `gpt-4o` defaults, Anthropic-only workspaces now fall back to `claude-sonnet-4-6`, and Gemini-only workspaces fall back to `gemini-3.5-flash` / `gemini-3.1-pro-preview`. This means newly created non-OpenAI workspaces can actually answer requests immediately instead of saving successfully and then failing on their first AI call.
- Added regression coverage in `tests/workspaces.test.js`, `tests/workspace-ai-routing.test.js`, and new `tests/web-api-workspace.test.js` for Anthropic-only and Gemini-only workspace creation plus blank-key normalization.

## 1.4.130 - 2026-05-20
- Scoped the Gemini model catalog to routable models. `GeminiProvider.GetAvailableModelsAsync()` previously returned every `generateContent`-capable model the Google API lists, including `gemma-*`, `lyria-*`, and `deep-research-*` names that the provider registry routes only by the `^gemini-` prefix — selecting one failed validation with a misleading "not found", or, if set without validation, dispatched to the OpenAI fallback and failed at runtime. The catalog is now filtered to the `^gemini-` routing prefix (reusing the registry's own `GeminiModelPattern`) so the provider only advertises models that resolve back to it.
- Refreshed the Gemini model names in the `models` command help text to current releases — `gemini-3.1-pro-preview`, `gemini-3.1-flash-lite`, `gemini-3.5-flash` — all verified present in the live API catalog. The previously listed `gemini-1.5-*` names were retired by Google.
- Documented the Gemini chat provider in `ARCHITECTURE.md`: the AI Pipeline / Provider behavior section now covers `gemini-*` routing, the REST transport, the `responseSchema` sanitization, and the catalog filter.
- Added a `tests/gemini-provider.test.js` case proving non-`gemini-*` `generateContent` models (`gemma-*`, `lyria-*`, `deep-research-*`) are excluded from the advertised catalog.

## 1.4.129 - 2026-05-20
- Added Google Gemini as a third AI chat provider alongside OpenAI and Anthropic, completing the provider registry that `1.4.120` deliberately left open for a future Gemini entry. A workspace can now switch its default or complex chat model to a `gemini-*` model with no code changes; names matching `/^gemini-/` route to the new `src/ai-providers/gemini-provider.js`, and `WorkspaceAI` consumes the provider registry generically so no dispatcher changes were needed.
- `GeminiProvider` implements the full `AIProvider` interface against the Google Generative Language REST API (`generateContent`) rather than a vendor SDK: text and JSON responses, model-catalog lookup, validation, and a connectivity probe. The API key is passed via the `x-goog-api-key` request header (never a URL query parameter), and `TestConnectivityAsync` returns the same typed `code` field (`auth_failed` / `rate_limited` / `overloaded` / `api_error`) as the Anthropic provider.
- Added the optional workspace field `GEMINI_API_KEY`, validated in `src/workspaces.js`. The Gemini chat provider reuses the key already used for grounded web search; switching to a `gemini-*` model rejects cleanly when the key is missing instead of failing later inside the request. The `models` command and `run-diagnostics` now include Gemini.
- The Gemini provider strips JSON Schema keywords that Gemini's `responseSchema` rejects (`additionalProperties`, `$schema`) before sending a structured-output request, so the shared OpenAI-shaped schema assets under `data/static/ai/` work unchanged across all three providers — without this every `gemini-*` JSON-response call would fail with a 400.
- Added `tests/gemini-provider.test.js`, a standalone suite that stubs `global.fetch` in isolation (so it cannot leak into the SDK-mocking provider tests) covering the REST transport: endpoint construction, the auth header, payload shape, schema sanitization, error paths, model-catalog parsing, and connectivity code mapping. Registry routing and `Build()` for the Gemini descriptor are covered in `tests/ai-providers.test.js`.

## 1.4.128 - 2026-05-20
- Fixed thread-context upload fallthrough after file errors. `ChatModule.#TryStoreThreadMemoryFileAsync(...)` no longer conflates "Markdown file was detected" with "Markdown file was successfully stored as context memory." Oversized uploads, Slack download failures, and HTML/error responses now return a handled-but-not-stored result so both `#OnAppMentionAsync` and `#OnMessageAsync` stop after posting the user-facing failure instead of continuing into an ungrounded AI answer.
- Redacted Slack private file metadata from chat logs. Thread-memory ingestion now logs only whether `url_private_download` was present plus the file host, and the app-mention event log now records a sanitized file summary (`name`, `size`, `host`, `hasDownloadUrl`) instead of serializing the raw Slack file object with its private URLs.
- Hardened `SlackApp.GetFileContentAsync(...)` against bearer-token leakage on redirects. The first fetch still uses `redirect: 'manual'`, but Sleuth now forwards the workspace `LIVE_TOKEN` only on same-origin redirects, follows cross-origin signed URLs without auth headers, rejects non-HTTPS redirect targets, and logs only the redirect host instead of the full redirected URL.
- Added regression coverage for both bug families: `tests/chat-module.integration.test.js` now proves failed Markdown uploads do not fall through to an AI answer in either the app-mention path or the thread auto-response path, and `tests/slack-app-file-download.test.js` covers cross-origin auth stripping, same-origin auth preservation, and non-HTTPS redirect rejection. Validation: targeted Jest suites plus `npm run build`.

## 1.4.127 - 2026-05-18
- Renamed the canonical workspace model-switch command to `switch-models`, while keeping `switch-model` and the older `model-switch` spelling as backward-compatible aliases. Help text, the `models` command output, route diagnostics, and RMM/help-mode command suggestions now all point operators at `@Sleuth AI switch-models:...` as the preferred syntax.
- Renamed the canonical GitHub sync command to `sync-github`, while keeping the older `github sync now` phrasing as an accepted alias. Command catalog metadata, registered reminder routes, and regression coverage now align on `sync-github` as the stable action-first name.

## 1.4.126 - 2026-05-17
- Added a dedicated live file-context harness at `scripts/slack-harness-file-upload.js`, wired as `npm run slack:harness:file-upload -- ...`. The harness boots Sleuth's real `SlackApp`, `StatsModule`, `RemindersModule`, `NotionModule`, and `ChatModule` in one-shot mode (no long-lived Socket Mode listener), uploads a local Markdown file to Slack, then invokes the normal app-mention handler chain locally against the live Slack file object so operators can debug thread context memory end to end from the CLI.
- Hardened the file-upload harness around the first live-review nits: it now rejects oversized local files up front using the same `200 KB` context-memory cap enforced by `ChatModule`, ignores `ENOENT` if two processes race while clearing a stale lock file, and preserves the primary harness failure if runtime cleanup also throws on the way out.
- Hardened the new file-upload harness with actionable Slack scope diagnostics. If the workspace bot token cannot upload files, the harness now reports the exact missing scope (`files:write`) and the currently granted scopes instead of failing with a bare `missing_scope` error string.
- Fixed the upload parser behind `SlackApp.UploadFileAsync(...)`. Slack's live `filesUploadV2` response is nested one level deeper than the original harness assumed (`resp.files[0].files[0]`), so the harness was normalizing uploaded Markdown files as blank metadata (`url_private_download: ""`, `size: 0`) and then failing to download them. The parser now unwraps the real file payload and keeps backward compatibility with simpler direct-file mocks.
- Fixed live harness thread correlation for repeated test questions. When the same `@Sleuth-dev ...` question text was reused across multiple uploads, the harness could match an older message with the same text and post the reply into that earlier thread. Upload-message resolution now prefers the uploaded Slack file ID over comment text so the operator sees the reply under the exact visible upload being tested.
- Live validation against the `neochrome-dev` workspace showed the current Slack bot token is missing the file scopes needed for this feature family: `files:write` blocks the new upload harness, and a direct `files.list` probe also reports missing `files:read`. That means the current development Slack app configuration cannot fully support document-context uploads until its OAuth scopes are expanded and the app is reinstalled.
- Fixed Slack file downloads returning HTML instead of raw file content. `GetFileContentAsync` was calling `url_private` (Slack's web-preview URL) which can return an HTML wrapper page for text files when fetched programmatically — even with a valid auth token. The fix switches to `url_private_download` (with `url_private` as fallback), which always returns raw file bytes. Added an HTML-response guard: if the downloaded content starts with `<`, the file is rejected with a user-facing error message and a server-side log entry instead of storing garbage HTML in thread context memory. Updated `SlackFileInfo` typedef to document both URL fields.

## 1.4.125 - 2026-05-17
- Fixed confirmation contamination (Codex Finding 2): when a user uploads a file with question text in the same message, the "I've loaded…" confirmation post was being included in the thread that `GatherThreadContextAsync` fetches for the same-turn AI response — appearing as prior assistant context before the model had answered anything. `TryStoreThreadMemoryFileAsync` now accepts a `SuppressConfirmation` flag; both `OnAppMentionAsync` and `OnMessageAsync` pass it as true when question text is present. The attribution footer (`Answer based on context from: filename`) covers the "which file was used" signal so no context is lost.
- Added 5 regression tests for thread context memory (Codex Finding 5): file-only upload posts confirmation without AI response; file + question suppresses confirmation and posts answer with attribution footer; follow-up reply reuses stored context and passes the Context Memory File block to the AI; attribution is absent when no context memory file exists; overwriting with a second file replaces the stored context for that thread. Extended `MockSlackApp` with `files` event field support and a configurable `GetFileContentAsync` jest mock. Suite: 584 tests, all passing (was 579).

## 1.4.124 - 2026-05-17
- Fixed thread context memory so Sleuth answers the question on the *initial* file upload instead of only posting the "I've loaded..." confirmation and ignoring the question. Previously `OnAppMentionAsync` and `OnMessageAsync` both returned immediately after storing the file, leaving the user's question unanswered. Both handlers now only return early when the message contains a file with no accompanying question text; when question text is present they fall through to the AI call, skipping command routing so admin commands are not accidentally triggered.
- Fixed context memory lookup for root messages: a root message has no `thread_ts`, so `GatherThreadContextAsync` was never called for it and the context memory block was not prepended to the AI prompt. Both handlers now derive `ContextThreadTS = thread_ts ?? (FileWasLoaded ? event.ts : null)` and use it as the thread root for the lookup — matching the key stored by `TryStoreThreadMemoryFileAsync` (`channel:event.ts`) so the block is included even for the first message in a thread.
- Added a grounding attribution footer to every AI response that was answered using a context memory file: `_Answer based on context from: *filename*_`. This gives the user a clear signal that the answer is grounded in the uploaded document, not the model's generic training knowledge. Web-search suggestions are suppressed when context memory was used (document analysis is not a web-search scenario).

## 1.4.123 - 2026-05-17
- Fixed a contradictory AI response pattern where Sleuth would simultaneously claim it could not see an uploaded document while also using content drawn from that same document (e.g. extracting party names like "Neochrome or BiomeScope" while saying "I can't see the agreement's contents"). Root cause: the system prompt did not tell the model that "the attached file" / "the uploaded document" in a thread where a Context Memory File block is present refers to that block — the model resolved the two reasoning paths independently and produced a mixed response. The chat system prompt (`data/static/ai/chat-instructions.md`) now explicitly instructs the model to treat the Context Memory File block as the document the user is referring to, to analyze it directly, and never to produce a response that both denies access to the document and uses information extracted from it.
- Added a new "Thread Context Memory" subsection to `ARCHITECTURE.md` documenting the full file-upload-to-AI-context pipeline: key derivation, immediate disk persistence, startup reload, block format prepended to thread text, and the system prompt guard that closes the "attached file" ambiguity.

## 1.4.122 - 2026-05-17
- Added Anthropic prompt caching to both `ProcessMessageWithJsonResponseAsync` and `ProcessMessageWithTextResponseAsync` in `src/ai-providers/anthropic-provider.js`. System instructions are now sent as an array with `cache_control: { type: "ephemeral" }` so repeated calls with the same schema-backed system prompt (reminder extraction, deduplication, date parsing) receive a cache hit and avoid re-paying input token costs.
- Hardened JSON parsing in both providers: `JSON.parse` is now wrapped in a try/catch in `anthropic-provider.js` and `openai-provider.js` so a truncated or malformed model response produces a descriptive error (`"Anthropic returned invalid JSON: ..."`) instead of a bare `SyntaxError` with no context on where it originated.
- Added error-code classification to `AnthropicProvider.TestConnectivityAsync`: the return value now includes a `code` field (`auth_failed` / `rate_limited` / `overloaded` / `api_error`) derived from the HTTP status so callers can distinguish a missing API key from a transient rate limit without parsing the error message string.
- Fixed Gemini API key exposure: `ProcessGeminiWebSearchAsync` in `src/workspace-ai.js` previously embedded the key as a URL query parameter (`?key=...`), making it visible in HTTP server and proxy logs. The key is now passed via the `x-goog-api-key` request header instead.
- Fixed a redundant model-name expression in `WorkspaceAI.ProcessMessageWithTextResponseAsync` (was passing `ArgModelName || this.#DefaultModelName` after already resolving it into `ModelName`; now passes `ModelName` directly).
- Added 8 new tests in `tests/workspace-ai-routing.test.js`: prompt caching blocks present for both text and JSON Anthropic requests, bare JSON Schema passthrough (no OpenAI envelope), descriptive error on malformed Anthropic JSON, descriptive error on malformed OpenAI JSON, `auth_failed` code on Anthropic 401, `rate_limited` code on Anthropic 429. Test suite grows from 569 to 577 passing tests.

## 1.4.121 - 2026-05-17
- Hardened the new multi-vendor `WorkspaceAI` surface so provider failures are no longer flattened into misleading "not found" or empty-catalog results. `GetModelAvailabilityAsync(...)` now preserves whether a model is valid, absent, blocked by missing provider config, or temporarily unverifiable because the provider catalog call failed. The `model-switch` and `set-channel-model` admin commands now surface those cases accurately instead of telling operators a model does not exist when the real problem is an unset API key or transient provider outage.
- Live model-catalog lookups now preserve per-provider fetch status instead of silently substituting `[]` on errors. The catalog-question flow refuses to answer from a partial cross-vendor catalog and names the failing provider(s), which keeps "Anthropic/OpenAI catalog fetch failed" distinct from "this workspace genuinely has no chat models available".
- Updated operator-facing workspace configuration docs and templates to document `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, and updated `ARCHITECTURE.md` to describe the more flexible multi-vendor AI layout: `src/workspace-ai.js` as the workspace-scoped dispatcher, provider implementations under `src/ai-providers/`, OpenAI-specific web search staying pinned to the OpenAI provider, and provider-aware validation/catalog status handling.

## 1.4.120 - 2026-05-16
- Added support for the Anthropic Claude API alongside the existing OpenAI integration, so a workspace can switch its default and complex chat models between `gpt-*` / `o*` and `claude-*` without code changes. `WorkspaceAI` is now a thin dispatcher that routes each Process* / GetAvailableModels / IsValidModel / TestConnectivity call to the provider that owns the requested model name. Routing is by prefix (`claude-*` → Anthropic, `gpt-*` / `o[0-9]-*` / `chatgpt-*` / `codex-*` / `computer-use-*` → OpenAI; unknown names fall back to OpenAI so custom IDs still work). Provider clients are built lazily, so a workspace that stays on OpenAI never instantiates the Anthropic SDK.
- Added a new optional workspace field `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` (validated in `src/workspaces.js`). Switching to a `claude-*` model via `model-switch:default='claude-...'` rejects the change when the key is missing instead of failing later inside the SDK — `IsValidModelAsync` returns false for the unreachable provider so the existing `model-switch-command.js` flow reports it cleanly.
- Refactored the AI integration surface into `src/ai-providers/`: `index.js` (registry + model-prefix resolution), `openai-provider.js` (extracted from the old `WorkspaceAI` body), `anthropic-provider.js` (new `@anthropic-ai/sdk` wrapper using the Messages API + structured outputs for JSON responses). The OpenAI `{name, strict, schema}` JSON-schema envelope is reused for Claude — the Anthropic provider unwraps it so the existing schema files under `data/static/ai/` work unchanged.
- The `models` command now lists common Claude models alongside the OpenAI ones and notes the `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` requirement. The live-model-catalog Q&A path (`@Sleuth AI what claude models are available?`) now fetches catalogs from every configured provider via the new `GetAvailableModelsByProviderAsync(...)`, grounds the answer in the union, and recognizes `claude`/`anthropic` as catalog-question keywords. `FilterLiveModelCatalogForChat` accepts `claude-*` IDs.
- `run-diagnostics` now probes every configured provider (via the new `TestProviderConnectivityAsync(...)`) and reports "not configured" rather than "FAILED" for providers without an API key. `src/app.js` startup probe now logs the configured default model so an admin can see at a glance which vendor was contacted.
- Gemini chat support is intentionally not implemented in this scope but the registry is structured so adding it is a single new file plus one `Providers` entry — no edits to feature modules required.
- Added `tests/ai-providers.test.js` (registry + Build) and `tests/workspace-ai-routing.test.js` (default model fallback, gpt-* → OpenAI, claude-* → Anthropic, JSON dispatch via structured outputs, missing-API-key rejection, runtime model-switch re-routing). Updated `tests/chat-module.integration.test.js` mock to expose `TestProviderConnectivityAsync` and `GetAvailableModelsByProviderAsync`. 569 tests pass.

## 1.4.119 - 2026-05-15
- Added a 🛑 stop-reaction to handsfree mode. When a user adds an `octagonal_sign` reaction to any message in a handsfree thread, Sleuth posts "Got it, I'll stop monitoring this thread unless you tag me again." and stops auto-responding to subsequent messages in that thread. The reaction can be placed on any message — root or reply — and `GetMessageThreadTsAsync` (new method in `slack-app.js`) uses `reactions.get` to resolve the thread root regardless. `#ShouldRespondToMessageAsync` now treats `octagonal_sign` identically to `no_bell` when scanning thread reactions, so the suppression persists across app restarts without any additional state. Adding a `bell` reaction after a 🛑 re-enables handsfree mode as expected.

## 1.4.118 - 2026-05-15
- Fixed a restart-time hole in the new Slack List title-edit sync. `1.4.117` correctly handled live `updated` events from a managed per-user row, but if the app restarted before the next poll, the first post-startup scan saw that row as `added`, merely re-adopted the `reminder_id`, and skipped summary reconciliation. `ListsModule` now reconciles the row summary during managed-row adoption too, so previously edited per-user rows self-heal back into the reminder JSON on the first poll after startup.
- This specifically closes the live dev-server case where the Slack List row already showed `test manual edit` but `data/runtime/reminders/neochrome-dev_reminders.json` still held the old title because the deployment restart reset the poll baseline before the summary write-back occurred. `#lessonslearned`: once an external poller uses diff-based change detection, startup "adoption" paths must enforce the same semantic reconciliation as steady-state update paths or restarts become silent data-loss windows.

## 1.4.117 - 2026-05-15
- Extended the per-user Slack Lists sync so manual title edits now round-trip back into Sleuth reminders instead of being silently ignored. When a managed row's `summary` field changes in a durable per-user list, `ListsModule` now treats that as an inbound edit, persists the new task title back into the reminder JSON, and mirrors the updated title to the shared list plus any other synced list contexts that track the same reminder.
- Added `SlackFormatUtils.RichTextToMrkdwn(...)` and `ReplaceReminderSummary(...)` so Slack Lists rich-text rows can be converted back into Slack mrkdwn without degrading mentions/links, and so Sleuth can rewrite only the mutable `Key task(s)` section of a reminder while preserving the original quoted-message envelope when the reminder came from a real Slack message.
- Added focused regression coverage in `tests/slack-format-utils.test.js` and `tests/lists-module.test.js` for summary round-tripping. `#lessonslearned`: once a per-user list becomes genuinely editable, "summary text is immutable" stops being a safe simplifying assumption; the persisted reminder model needs an explicit mutable-summary path rather than treating the List title as a disposable projection.

## 1.4.116 - 2026-05-15
- Fixed a real Slack Lists permissions bug in the new per-user sync flow. Sleuth was creating durable per-user lists and then only granting the invocation channel `read` access, which left the target user opening a Sleuth-owned list in Slack's "View only" mode even for workspace owners/admins. Per-user list creation now grants the target Slack user `write` access directly via `slackLists.access.set(... user_ids, access_level:'write')`, while keeping the originating channel on a secondary `read` share for discoverability.
- `EnsureUserListAsync` now re-applies those access grants on resync too, so re-running `@Sleuth AI generate-list for @user` repairs previously created view-only per-user lists in place instead of requiring manual list deletion/recreation.
- Tightened the access-grant contract in `src/lists-module.js` so list announcements happen only after the new access calls run, and user write access is treated as the required success condition for per-user list setup. Channel read-sharing is now best-effort instead of the gate, which matches the actual feature goal: the assignee must be able to edit the list even if the broadcast/share step is imperfect.
- Added regression coverage in `tests/lists-module.test.js` for the new permission model: per-user lists now expect a user-level `write` grant plus an optional channel-level `read` grant, and setup fails specifically when Slack rejects the user write grant. `#lessonslearned`: Slack workspace role is not the same thing as object-level Lists permission; if Sleuth creates the object, the sync design must explicitly grant the human editor rights instead of assuming admin/owner status will bypass the app-owned ACL.

## 1.4.115 - 2026-05-15
- Added a new live-operator Slack Lists harness at `scripts/lists-harness.js`, wired as `npm run lists:harness -- <command> ...`. The harness boots Sleuth's real Slack wrapper plus `RemindersModule` and `ListsModule` in one-shot mode (no long-lived Socket Mode listener) so an operator or VS Code agent can drive list testing against a real workspace without poking private internals by hand.
- The harness exposes explicit subcommands for the missing live-test surfaces we found on dev: `status`, `ensure-user-list`, `poll-now`, `list-rows`, `create-manual-row`, `complete-row`, and `delete-row`. Mutating commands require `--execute`; all commands return JSON so agents can chain them deterministically.
- Added a small set of operator-facing public methods to `src/lists-module.js` to support that harness cleanly: forced poll execution, sync-status snapshots, per-user row inspection, and test-only row creation/completion/deletion that intentionally bypass Sleuth's echo-suppression path so the next poll sees them as inbound Slack-side edits.
- This closes the live-test gap where dev had only the shared list active and no server-side way to create a per-user list, force a poll, or simulate list-row mutations programmatically. `#lessonslearned`: if a feature depends on an external UI surface like Slack Lists, ship a read-write operator harness alongside the feature or "manual QA only" becomes the hidden production contract.

## 1.4.114 - 2026-05-14
- Implemented bi-directional sync between Sleuth reminders and durable per-user Slack Lists. The existing shared "Sleuth To-do's" list remains output-only and unchanged. Per-user lists are a new parallel subsystem: `@Sleuth AI generate-list for @user` now creates a persistent `Sleuth Reminders — @username` list (no date stamp) and registers it under the user's Slack ID. Subsequent calls resync the list in-place rather than creating a new one.
- Added `src/list-context.js` — a data-holder abstraction for one Slack list's sync state (`Kind`, `ItemCache`, `EchoSuppressRowIds`, `DeleteRowOnComplete` policy, etc.). `ListsModule` now holds `#SharedContext` + a `#UserContexts` map and a `#ContextsByListId` dispatch index instead of flat scalar fields, enabling arbitrarily many simultaneous lists without code duplication.
- Extended the poll loop to cover all contexts sequentially with 250 ms inter-context spacing. Echo suppression (outbound writes mark the affected row ID; next poll skips it, then clears) prevents the bot's own writes from being re-processed as inbound user edits.
- Wired inbound poll handlers for user contexts: checking a row's completed state calls `RemindersModule.CompleteReminderFromListAsync` and leaves the row as a history record; deleting a row calls `RemindersModule.CancelReminderFromListAsync` and removes it. The shared list's inbound handlers remain no-op stubs.
- Split the reminder deletion path in `src/reminders-module.js` by reason: `#DeleteRemindersAsync(ids, reason)` now routes `'completed'` to `ListsModule.HandleReminderCompletedAsync` (marks done, keeps per-user row, deletes shared row) and any other reason to `HandleReminderRemovedAsync` (deletes the row from all contexts). Added public `CompleteReminderFromListAsync`, `CancelReminderFromListAsync`, and `CreateReminderFromListRowAsync` methods to `RemindersModule`.
- Added Phase 2 create-from-row: a hand-authored row in a user's list (summary + parseable due_date required; assignee + target channel optional) triggers `CreateReminderFromListRowAsync` and writes the assigned `reminder_id` back into the row. Rows missing required fields are marked with a new `needs-info` status choice instead of silently looping.
- Removed `ListsModule.GenerateUserSnapshotListAsync` and `#BuildUserSnapshotListNameAsync` (replaced by `EnsureUserListAsync`). Removed the `ListsModule` direct reference from `RemindersReactionHandler` (completion/cancel fan-out now flows entirely through `RemindersModule`). Rewired `reminders-app-mention-handler.js` `generate-list` response copy to reflect the persistent, synced behavior.
- Updated the v2 cache format (`{ version:2, shared:{…}, userLists:{userID:{…}} }`) with legacy-flat migration on load and atomic tmp-then-rename saves. Added 8 new tests (545 total, was 538) covering `EnsureUserListAsync` create/resync, inbound completion/cancel, echo suppression, Phase 2 valid/invalid row creation, and cache v2 migration.

## 1.4.113 - 2026-05-13
- Changed the startup GitHub Actions UX so Sleuth no longer appends the CI result after the changelog block inside the main startup message. `src/app.js` now posts the standard startup/changelog message immediately and schedules a separate GitHub Actions follow-up about 4 seconds later through `src/github-actions-startup-summary.js`, which makes the test result visible as its own Slack message instead of burying it below the changelog snippet.
- Added focused follow-up coverage in `tests/github-actions-startup-summary.test.js` for the delayed Slack post path and the no-config no-op path. Live dev-server inspection also showed the current `neochrome-dev` workspace is not yet configured with `GITHUB_ACTIONS_REPO`, so no startup Actions summary will appear there until that workspace JSON is updated.

## 1.4.112 - 2026-05-13
- Added `src/github-actions-startup-summary.js`, a small startup-only helper that fetches the latest GitHub Actions workflow run for a configured repo and returns one Slack-ready summary line. The startup notification path in `src/app.js` now kicks off these lookups in parallel before posting startup messages and waits up to about 3.5 seconds for results, so the normal "Sleuth has been updated..." post can include CI status when it arrives quickly without stalling boot indefinitely.
- Added optional workspace fields `GITHUB_ACTIONS_REPO`, `GITHUB_ACTIONS_WORKFLOW`, and `GITHUB_ACTIONS_BRANCH` in `src/workspaces.js`, `config/workspace-template.json`, `docs/web-api.md`, and `README.md`. The branch filter defaults to Sleuth's current git branch, and if that branch has no runs the helper falls back to the repo's latest overall run.
- Added focused coverage in `tests/github-actions-startup-summary.test.js` for branch-scoped fetches, workflow-scoped fetches, fallback to the latest overall run, and timeout behavior, plus validation coverage for the new optional workspace fields in `tests/workspaces.test.js`.

## 1.4.111 - 2026-05-13
- Hardened the new per-user Slack Lists snapshot flow so Sleuth no longer reports success when Slack rejects the channel access grant for the generated list. `ListsModule.GenerateUserSnapshotListAsync(...)` now aborts before row creation and returns an explicit error instead of posting a success message for a list the request channel cannot actually open. Added a focused regression in `tests/lists-module.test.js` covering the `slackLists.access.set` failure path.

## 1.4.110 - 2026-05-13
- Added an additive, on-demand Slack Lists snapshot command for per-user task exports: `@Sleuth AI generate-list for @user`. The reminder command lives in `src/reminders-app-mention-handler.js`, resolves the mentioned Slack user ID, pulls that user's assigned reminders from the existing assignee index, and asks `ListsModule` to create a brand-new one-shot list for just that user.
- Added `ListsModule.GenerateUserSnapshotListAsync(...)` in `src/lists-module.js`. The new path creates an isolated list with the same reminder schema as the live synced workspace list, grants read access to the channel where the command was invoked, waits for Slack's list-propagation window, writes the selected reminders into the snapshot, and returns the list permalink/title/counts without mutating the main synced list's `listId`, schema, or reminder-to-row cache.
- Refactored the shared reminder-list schema and item-create payload construction so the live synced list and generated snapshot lists use the same Slack field contract. This keeps Task/Status/Completed/Assignee/Source/Message serialization aligned instead of forking a second Lists payload implementation.
- Added route coverage in `tests/reminders-app-mention-handler.test.js`, snapshot-isolation coverage in `tests/lists-module.test.js`, and fixed `scripts/validate-command-catalog.js`'s local SlackApp stub so `npm run validate:commands` works again after the newer `HandleAction` and reminder dependency wiring. `npm run build`, `npm run validate:commands`, and the targeted `reminders-app-mention-handler`, `lists-module`, and `slack-app-user-identity` Jest suites pass.

## 1.4.109 - 2026-05-13
- Hardened the new Slack user-identity resolver against sequential API hammering when Slack is degraded. If `users.info` fails but Sleuth has a stale cached identity, `src/slack-app.js` now places that entry into a temporary error back-off window instead of immediately treating it as expired again on the very next lookup. This keeps the stale identity available while avoiding a per-request retry loop during outages or rate limiting.
- Added a focused regression to `tests/slack-app-user-identity.test.js` proving that, after a stale-cache fallback, a second sequential read within the back-off window reuses the cached identity without making another `users.info` call. `npm run build` passes and the targeted `slack-app-user-identity`, `github-comment-relay`, and `lists-module` Jest suites pass.

## 1.4.108 - 2026-05-13
- Promoted Slack user lookup into a first-class runtime resolver in `src/slack-app.js`. `GetUserIdentityAsync(...)` now owns `users.info` lookups with an in-memory cache, in-flight request coalescing, a normalized identity payload (`DisplayName`, `RealName`, `Username`, admin/owner flags, etc.), and stale-on-error fallback so one transient Slack API failure does not immediately collapse user labeling back to raw IDs.
- Rebased `GetUserDisplayNameAsync(...)` and `IsAdminOrOwnerAsync(...)` onto that shared resolver instead of each making their own ad hoc `users.info` calls. This keeps display-name reads and admin checks consistent and avoids duplicate Slack lookups for the same user inside one runtime window.
- Extended the test harness contract in `tests/mocks/mock-slack-app.js` with `SetUserIdentities(...)` / `GetUserIdentityAsync(...)` so feature tests can exercise richer Slack identity behavior without re-implementing lookup logic per module.
- Added focused regression coverage in `tests/slack-app-user-identity.test.js` for concurrent lookup coalescing, stale-cache fallback on Slack API failure, and cache sharing across display-name/admin checks. The Slack Lists mention-rendering fix from the current worktree remains covered by the targeted Lists suite. `npm run build` passes and the targeted `slack-app-user-identity`, `github-comment-relay`, and `lists-module` Jest suites pass. `#lessonslearned`: if Slack identity resolution matters to user-facing output, do not hide it behind one-off helper methods; make it a shared cached contract early so future features inherit consistency by default.

## 1.4.107 - 2026-05-13
- Fixed the Slack Lists API contract mismatches in `src/lists-module.js`. List creation no longer sends the undocumented `channel_id` argument or uses generic `files.share` / `files.sharedPublicURL`; Sleuth now creates the List via `slackLists.create`, grants reminder-channel visibility through the documented `slackLists.access.set` method, and posts the constructed List permalink into the reminder channel as an announcement.
- Fixed reminder row updates and deletions to use Slack's documented row/cell payloads. `slackLists.items.update` now sends `cells` with `row_id` + `column_id` typed values instead of the old `{ item_id, item }` shape, and `slackLists.items.delete` now sends the documented `{ list_id, id }` payload.
- Aligned the created List schema with the reminder write path by adding explicit `status` and `completed` columns, seeding those fields on item creation, and teaching update/readback code to use the new custom columns. Also replaced the stale `slackLists.info` assumption with a documented `slackLists.items.list` existence check and fixed `slackLists.items.info` readback to consume `record`.
- Added focused regression coverage in `tests/lists-module.test.js` for the create/access flow and the update/delete request shapes. `npm run build` passes and the targeted Lists suite passes. `#lessonslearned`: when an API family is new and evolving, "method exists" is not enough validation; lock the exact request and response shapes into tests or the integration will quietly drift onto undocumented payloads.

## 1.4.106 - 2026-05-12
- Switched the Stratalist view-mode completed-item glyph from the emoji `✅` (`U+2705`) to the Unicode ballot-box pair partner `☑` (`U+2611`). The incomplete-item glyph stays as `☐` (`U+2610`), so both states now come from the same Unicode "Ballot box" range. This renders with consistent stroke weight across platforms instead of the previous mix of a flat outline and a colored/rounded emoji. Affects `@Sleuth AI view stratalist <slug-or-url>` output only; the unrelated `✅` reaction emoji for reminder completion is untouched.

## 1.4.105 - 2026-05-12
- Added a "Search the web for: <query>" Block Kit suggestion button to freeform chat answers from `@Sleuth AI`. When a user asks a general question without tagging anyone else, the bot's answer now ships with a one-click button that re-runs the question through the OpenAI web-search provider. The button is suppressed whenever the inbound message contains a user mention other than the bot, on the assumption that the user is bringing a teammate into the conversation rather than asking for an external web lookup.
- Added a `HandleAction(actionId, handler)` infrastructure to `src/slack-app.js` plus a `BlockActionInfo` typedef. Bolt runs in Socket Mode so `block_actions` deliveries ride the existing WebSocket — no new HTTP route or signing-secret plumbing was needed. Each `HandleAction` call registers a dedicated Bolt action subscription at start; the dispatcher acks within Bolt's 3-second window before running the handler chain.
- Wired the new action layer into `ChatModule.#OnChatGoogleSearchActionAsync`, which re-routes the button's carried query through the same `HandleWebSearchProviderCommandAsync` used by the explicit `web-search` command, posting the result as a sibling reply in the original thread.
- Added 3 new integration cases in `tests/chat-module.integration.test.js` covering button render, suppression on teammate mention, and the click-through web-search flow. Mirrored `HandleAction` + `SimulateActionAsync` into `tests/mocks/mock-slack-app.js`. Requires a one-time toggle: enable Interactivity & Shortcuts in the Slack app dashboard (Socket Mode means no Request URL is needed). 517/517 Jest tests pass; `npm run build` passes.

## 1.4.104 - 2026-05-07
- Updated Stratalist view-mode checklist rendering to use the exact Unicode checkbox glyphs you requested: completed items now render with `✅` (`U+2705`) and incomplete items render with `☐` (`U+2610`) instead of ASCII `[x]` / `[ ]`. This affects the read-only `@Sleuth AI view stratalist <slug-or-url>` output only.

## 1.4.103 - 2026-05-07
- Added a maintainable first-class Stratalist read-only integration: `@Sleuth AI view stratalist <slug-or-url>`. The command lives in `ChatModule` rather than the reminder pipeline, fetches the public JSON companion endpoint, and renders the shared checklist back into Slack with `[ ]` / `[x]` state plus a link back to the original Stratalist page. This ships the low-risk viewing workflow first without committing the reminder-import path yet.
- Split shared Stratalist behavior into a reusable deterministic module at `src/stratalist.js` for slug/URL normalization, public-list fetches, payload validation, due-date display formatting, and Slack rendering. The earlier spike module `src/stratalist-import.js` now depends on that shared contract instead of owning its own fetch/parsing rules, which keeps future import work from forking the external-integration surface.
- Added regression coverage in `tests/stratalist.test.js` and `tests/chat-module.integration.test.js` for shared rendering, fetch/error handling, and the new chat command route. `npm test`, `npm run build`, and `npm run validate:commands` pass.

## 1.4.102 - 2026-05-07
- Promoted `data/static/ai/command-catalog.json` from an intent-only asset into the canonical docs/source-of-truth for chat-command help metadata. Added route mappings, help visibility/order metadata, and missing catalog entries for registered chat routes such as `rmm`, `rmm ifl`, `changelog`, `ask-self`, `ask-woo`, `web-search-advanced`, and the live model-catalog question path.
- Added a shared `src/command-catalog.js` renderer/validator plus two new operator scripts: `npm run generate:help` regenerates [data/static/HELP.md](data/static/HELP.md) into a much shorter command-first guide, and `npm run validate:commands` verifies catalog shape, enforces the 1-2 sentence help-description rule, checks that every registered `ChatModule` route is covered by a catalog entry, and fails if `HELP.md` is stale.
- Extended the same registry treatment to reminder app-mention commands. `src/reminders-app-mention-handler.js` now registers its deterministic reminder/search/admin routes through `CommandRouter`, the command-catalog `RegisteredRoutes` metadata now covers those reminder routes as well, and the validator instantiates both registries before checking for drift.
- Replaced the hand-maintained admin `commands` output with catalog-driven rendering and kept the `help` command deterministic by reading the generated `HELP.md` with a catalog-render fallback. Added regression coverage in `tests/command-catalog.test.js`. `#lessonslearned`: if you want real guardrails, do not put a live LLM in the documentation build path; keep the prose generation deterministic and validate the sentence budget structurally.

## 1.4.101 - 2026-05-05
- Tightened `help <query>` guidance for vague search requests. When a user says something underspecified like `@Sleuth AI help search things`, the help-mode path no longer overcommits to a weak `web-search things` suggestion. Instead it now presents a short menu of concrete exact commands for the main search families: OpenAI web search, Gemini/Google web search, reminder search, and Notion search.
- Updated the `rmm` / help-mode intent instructions so the complex-model resolver treats vague search language (`search things`, `help search`, `find something`, `look stuff up`) as ambiguous unless the user names a source or provides a real topic. Specific queries such as `help search web for automated web browsers` still resolve directly to the appropriate search command.
- Added regression coverage in `tests/chat-module.integration.test.js` for the new vague-search help behavior. `npm test`, `npm run build`, and `npm run validate:ai` pass.

## 1.4.100 - 2026-05-05
- Extended `help` / `features` into a guidance entrypoint without changing the existing bare-command behavior. `@Sleuth AI help` and `@Sleuth AI features` still render the static help document, but `@Sleuth AI help <request>` and `@Sleuth AI features <request>` now route into the same suggestion-only intent resolver used by `rmm`, returning the best exact command instead of the generic help file.
- Added a more specific `help/features + query` command route ahead of the bare help route in `ChatModule`, and updated `src/chat-commands/help-features-command.js` so this “help mode” never executes actions — it only suggests the matching command, along with permission, normalization, rationale, and confidence.
- Added regression coverage in `tests/chat-module.integration.test.js` for the new guidance-mode behavior: bare `help` still returns documentation, while `help switch models` and `features search web ...` produce intent-backed command suggestions. `npm test`, `npm run build`, and `npm run validate:ai` pass.

## 1.4.99 - 2026-05-05
- Expanded the `rmm` command-intent corpus from a narrow model/admin slice into a broader command-only retrieval layer. The curated catalog in `data/static/ai/command-catalog.json` now includes web-search providers, Notion search, reminder search/list commands, reminder channel toggles, GitHub sync/test commands, `show-stats`, and `ping`, so the complex-model resolver sees the commands users actually mean instead of only model-switch/diagnostics candidates.
- Extended the `rmm` structured schema with `query_text` and updated canonical-command building so intent resolution can return exact search-style commands such as `web-search <query>`, `gemini-search <query>`, `notion search <query>`, and reminder search commands, rather than clarifying simply because the earlier schema only had model-name fields.
- Improved command retrieval scoring by including syntax examples in the lexical match surface, and added regression coverage for the exact gap that motivated this pass: a request like `rmm search google for automated web browsers` now surfaces the web-search candidates to the resolver and can suggest `gemini-search automated web browsers`. `npm test`, `npm run build`, and `npm run validate:ai` pass.

## 1.4.98 - 2026-05-05
- Added an `rmm` / `rmm ifl` command-intent layer for Slack app mentions. `@Sleuth AI rmm <request>` now runs a dedicated structured-output intent pass through `WorkspaceAI`'s complex model against a curated command catalog in `data/static/ai/command-catalog.json`, then replies in-thread with the closest exact Sleuth command. `@Sleuth AI rmm ifl <request>` uses the same resolution path but will only auto-execute commands explicitly pre-authorized in the catalog (`CanExecuteWithIfl`) so the feature cannot turn into a freeform command executor.
- Added a shared normalization hints file at `data/static/ai/command-normalization.json` and wired it into direct command routing. Common model aliases such as `gpt5`, `gpt 5`, and `chatgpt 5` now normalize to `gpt-5`, and relaxed direct forms like `model-switch 'gpt 5'` and `set channel model 'gpt5 mini'` canonicalize before the `CommandRouter` runs. This fixes the missing-dash / missing-colon frustration without weakening the underlying handler contracts.
- Added new AI assets (`rmm-instructions.md`, `rmm-schema.json`) plus regression coverage in `tests/chat-module.integration.test.js` and `tests/command-intent-resolver.test.js` for fuzzy direct-command parsing, `rmm` suggestion mode, and `rmm ifl` pre-authorization gates. `npm test`, `npm run build`, and `npm run validate:ai` pass.

## 1.4.97 - 2026-05-05
- Hardened workspace config validation for persisted model overrides. `Workspaces.ValidateWorkspaceInfo` now rejects non-string or empty `DEFAULT_MODEL_NAME` / `COMPLEX_MODEL_NAME` values, so a malformed workspace JSON fails fast with a clear config error instead of leaking a bad model name into `WorkspaceAI` and failing later during startup or OpenAI requests.
- Restored fail-open behavior for optional client search mappings. `src/client-mapping.js` now returns an empty mapping set when `data/static/client-channel-mapping.json` is missing or contains invalid JSON, instead of throwing and potentially breaking `search reminders` flows in environments where that file is absent or corrupt.
- Added regression coverage in `tests/workspaces.test.js` for optional model-name validation and in `tests/client-mapping.test.js` for missing/invalid client-mapping config handling.

## 1.4.96 - 2026-05-05
- Manual `:alarm_clock:` reminders with no explicit time trigger no longer blindly copy the original message into the task bullet. Previously the force-schedule fallback in `src/reminders-module.js` replaced the AI analysis with a synthetic `"tomorrow morning"` reminder and set `reminder_message` to the raw Slack text, so question-style client asks like `How hard is it to make our Ground Advantage $5 shipping to $6` surfaced verbatim in the scheduled reminder. The fallback now makes a second, narrow AI pass through `RemindersAIPipeline.ExtractManualReminderTaskAsync()` using a dedicated prompt/schema pair (`manual-reminder-task-instructions.md` / `manual-reminder-task-schema.json`) to infer a concise imperative task title such as `Change Ground Advantage $5 shipping to $6`.
- Added regression coverage in `tests/reminders-integration.test.js` for the full `:alarm_clock:` force-schedule path and in `tests/reminders-ai-pipeline.test.js` for the new manual-task extraction prompt/example. `npm test`, `npm run build`, and `npm run validate:ai` pass.

## 1.4.95 - 2026-05-05
- `model-switch:'<model>'` now persists across service restarts. Previously the command only mutated the in-memory `WorkspaceAI` instance — every reboot reverted to the hardcoded defaults (`gpt-4o-mini` for chat, `gpt-4o` for date extraction), and admins had no way to make a new default stick without editing source. The handler now writes the new value to optional `DEFAULT_MODEL_NAME` and `COMPLEX_MODEL_NAME` fields on the workspace JSON via `Workspaces.SaveWorkspaceInfoAsync`, and `WorkspaceAI`'s constructor reads those fields at boot. Priority order: explicit constructor arg > workspace JSON field > hardcoded fallback. Backwards-compatible — workspaces that have never run `model-switch` keep using the hardcoded defaults exactly as before.
- The persist call happens before the user-facing "Default model switched to '<name>'" reply, so a persist failure (disk full, permission denied) surfaces as the existing "Failed to switch model: <error>" message rather than a misleading success.
- Added a new `tests/model-switch-command.test.js` suite (2 cases) asserting the persist callback fires with the mutated `WorkspaceInfo` after a successful switch and is *not* called when the requested model fails the live-catalog validation. Added one constructor test in `tests/workspace-ai.test.js` confirming `DEFAULT_MODEL_NAME` / `COMPLEX_MODEL_NAME` from the workspace JSON are honored at boot, with the hardcoded fallbacks still kicking in when those fields are absent. 452/452 Jest tests pass.

## 1.4.94 - 2026-05-05
- Fixed `ask-self` route matcher to tolerate punctuation between the command and the query. The previous regex `/^ask-self\s+(.+)/is` required whitespace as the only separator, so a phrasing like `ask-self, how does X work?` or `ask-self: explain Y` did not route to the RAG-grounded handler — instead it fell through to the freeform LLM chat path, which has no Sleuth corpus and produces hallucinated boilerplate answers about the system. The new regex `/^ask-self\b[\s,:;.!?]+(.+)/is` accepts whitespace, comma, colon, semicolon, period, exclamation, and question mark as separators while `\b` keeps `ask-selfanswer` and `ask-self-foo` from matching.
- Added 8 positive cases (whitespace, double-whitespace, comma, colon, semicolon, period, exclamation, question mark) and 4 negative cases (no separator, hyphen suffix, no query, trailing space only) as regression coverage in `tests/chat-module.test.js`. 448/448 Jest tests pass.

## 1.4.93 - 2026-05-05
- Web-search provider failures (`web-search`, `gemini-search`, and their aliases) now surface the underlying provider error message into the Slack reply instead of the generic `Sorry, I couldn't complete that web search. Check the logs for details.` The new reply is a one-line preamble plus the actual error in a code block, e.g. for a Gemini `API_KEY_INVALID` you now see the full Google `INVALID_ARGUMENT` payload directly in Slack instead of having to SSH into the server to read the journal. Truncated at 600 chars; any `key=<value>` substring (e.g. an embedded fetch URL) is rewritten to `key=<redacted>` before posting so a network-error message can never leak the workspace API key.
- Added `tests/chat-module.integration.test.js` regression cases covering both behaviors: surfaces the underlying error message verbatim, and redacts an `AIzaSy…`-style key embedded in a fetch error URL. 446/446 Jest tests pass.
- Added regression coverage for the previously fixed `model-switch` invariants (admin gate + route anchoring) — `test(model-switch): regression coverage for admin gate + route anchoring` (2 cases). The fixes shipped in v1.4.90 but lacked tests, so a future refactor of `CommandRouter` could have silently regressed them.

## 1.4.92 - 2026-05-05
- Expanded `@Sleuth AI search reminders <query>` so the haystack now includes the originating channel name and any GitHub issue/PR URLs on the reminder, in addition to `ReminderMessageText`. A reminder posted in `#client-a-eng` or linking `github.com/Acme/client-a-app/issues/42` now matches `search reminders client-a` even if the reminder text never mentions the client. Both exact and fuzzy (Levenshtein) match paths share the same haystack via `#BuildReminderSearchHaystack`.
- Added a client-tag layer at `data/static/client-channel-mapping.json` (loaded by `src/client-mapping.js`) mapping client names + aliases to channel IDs, channel-name patterns, and GitHub repo patterns. When a search query mentions a client name as a whole word, the search expands to include all reminders matching that client's mappings — letting `search reminders client-a` reach private/renamed channels whose names don't contain "client-a". The mapping file ships with one example Client A stub; populate `ChannelIDs`/`ChannelNamePatterns`/`GitHubRepoPatterns` per client.
- Added two reusable admin scripts to help populate the client mapping: `scripts/reconcile-channels.js` (paginated `conversations.list`) and `scripts/reconcile-users.js` (paginated `users.list`). Both resolve a Slack token from `--token` → `SLACK_BOT_TOKEN` env var → `--workspace <name>` (workspace JSON's `LIVE_TOKEN`), and write sorted JSON to stdout or `--out <path>`.
- Added Jest coverage in `tests/client-mapping.test.js` (resolution + match predicates) and three new cases in `tests/reminders-app-mention-handler.test.js` (channel-name match, GitHub-URL match, channel-ID-via-mapping match). All 442 tests pass.

## 1.4.91 - 2026-05-05
- Improved reminder extraction guidance for short follow-up clauses that inherit prior task context. Messages like `CBDAffs turned off on Client C. Will do Client A tomorrow morning.` should now preserve the carried-forward action and schedule `CBDAffs turn off on Client A` instead of over-compressing to `Do Client A`.
- Added a Jest guard to keep the carry-forward reminder extraction example in the prompt.

## 1.4.90 - 2026-05-01
- Hardened `model-switch` command: it is now restricted to workspace admins/owners, and command parsing is anchored to the start of the mention-stripped command text so conversational mentions of `model-switch:...` no longer route as a command.

## 1.4.89 - 2026-05-01
- Refactored `ChatModule` for SRP/OCP. Adding a new chat command no longer requires edits in two places (the dispatcher in `#OnAppMentionAsync` and `DescribeChatRoute`); both now consume a single `CommandRouter` registry populated by `#RegisterCommandRoutes`. Adding a new web-search provider no longer requires edits in 4+ places (dispatcher, route describer, two handlers, `commands` reference output); all consume the new `WebSearchProviders` registry in `src/web-search-providers.js` keyed by `{ Id, Aliases, Label, LoadingMessage, CommandsListDescription, InvokeAsync }`.
- Extracted all 16 chat command handlers from `src/chat-module.js` into per-command files under `src/chat-commands/` (e.g. `restart-command.js`, `ask-self-command.js`, `web-search-provider-command.js`, `model-switch-command.js`, etc.). Each handler is a free function with no `require('../chat-module')` and no access to `#`-private fields — the route registration in `#RegisterCommandRoutes` is the only binding glue, passing only what each handler needs from `this`. The two near-identical web-search handlers (`#OnWebSearchCommandAsync`, `#OnGeminiSearchCommandAsync`) collapsed into one shared `HandleWebSearchProviderCommandAsync` that takes the matched provider as a parameter.
- `static DescribeChatRoute` is now `#DescribeChatRoute` (instance method) since it reads from the instance-level router. Routes carry an optional `DescribePattern` so route classification can stay generous (e.g. `/^model-switch:/i` matches malformed commands too) while dispatch stays strict (`/model-switch:'([^']+)'/i`), preserving the original wrench-triage diagnostic behavior.
- `chat-module.js` shrank from 2390 to 1574 lines (−816). All 431 Jest tests pass; zero new TypeScript errors. No behavior change — pure refactor.
- #lessonslearned: Bulk regex-based extraction of class methods into separate files is brittle when the brace-counting regex eats sibling members or when handlers reach back into private state. The durable pattern is closure-binding at registration: handlers stay self-contained free functions, the registration site (`#RegisterCommandRoutes`) does the dependency injection, and `#`-private fields never leak. This refactor was attempted once before via a one-shot `unprivate.py` + regex extractor and broke the file completely; the working version landed in incremental commits with `npm test` green between each.

## 1.4.88 - 2026-04-30
- Added `google-search` and `search-google` as aliases for the `gemini-search` command — all four phrasings (`gemini-search`, `search-gemini`, `google-search`, `search-google`) now route to the same Gemini-powered handler.
- Fixed Gemini web search Slack rendering: previously the raw `searchEntryPoint.renderedContent` HTML/CSS widget was concatenated to the answer and dumped into the Slack message. The handler now extracts the structured `webSearchQueries` array instead and renders a Slack-friendly `_Related searches:_` footer of Google search links beneath the sources.

## 1.4.87 - 2026-04-30
- Added `@Sleuth AI gemini-search <query>` (and `search-gemini` alias) to support web searches powered by Google Gemini using the `gemini-flash-latest` model. This command uses the Gemini REST API and integrates Google Search Grounding to return sourced answers directly in Slack, complete with compliance HTML rendering.
- Added `GEMINI_API_KEY` as an optional per-workspace configuration setting required to authenticate Gemini-powered searches. The existing natural-language web search auto-routing continues to use the OpenAI API by default.

## 1.4.86 - 2026-04-30
- Auto-complete notifications for GitHub-linked reminders are now mirrored into the originating Slack thread in addition to the main reminder channel. After `#PostAutoCompleteNotificationAsync(...)` posts the existing top-level message to `TargetChannelID`, it calls a new `#PostOriginatingThreadNotificationAsync(...)` helper that posts the same notification text into `OriginalChannelID` threaded on `OriginalThreadTs ?? OriginalMessageID`. The mirror is skipped silently for legacy reminders that lack either coordinate, and any failure mirroring is isolated (warn-logged, never propagates) so the main-channel post outcome and the FSM transition both remain authoritative.
- Added regression coverage in `tests/github-sync-module.test.js` confirming the mirror posts when both coordinates are set, falls back from `OriginalThreadTs` to `OriginalMessageID`, and is skipped when the channel or both anchors are missing.

## 1.4.85 - 2026-04-30
- Expanded the GitHub comment-relay stop signal beyond the original 🛑 emoji. Thread replies that contain ⏹/⏹️ (`:stop_button:`, with or without the FE0F variation selector) or the case-insensitive text command `stop relay` now also mark all matching reminders as `GitHubRelayStopped`, persist that state, and acknowledge with the existing `:no_entry_sign:` reaction. Detection is centralized in a single `ContainsStopRelayTrigger(...)` helper so the trigger surface is one source of truth.
- Added regression coverage for the new triggers (⏹ alone, ⏹️ with FE0F, mixed-case `stop relay`) and a guard test confirming that messages mentioning `stop` without `relay` continue to be relayed normally.

## 1.4.84 - 2026-04-29
- Expanded natural-language web search aliases to cover casual domain-based phrasings: `check <domain.tld> ...` and `search <domain.tld> ...` now route to web search via `ExtractNaturalLanguageWebSearchQuery`. The presence of a recognizable TLD (`.com`, `.org`, `.net`, `.io`, `.co`, `.app`, `.dev`, `.ai`, `.uk`, `.us`) is the high-confidence signal; internal commands like `check if reminders are enabled` or `search my reminders` do not match. Full query text starting from the domain is passed to `ProcessWebSearchAsync` so the web search model has complete context.
- Added `search-web` as a reverse-order alias for the `web-search` command so `@Sleuth AI search-web <query>` routes through the same handler. Both spellings accept an empty or whitespace-only query and return the usage hint.
- Updated `data/static/HELP.md` web search entry to surface the domain-based phrasing examples alongside existing lookup aliases.

## 1.4.83 - 2026-04-29
- Fixed GPT-5-family chat model handling for per-channel overrides by recognizing dotted GPT-5 model IDs such as `gpt-5.5` and retrying once with temperature `1` when an unknown model rejects temperature `0`. This prevents Slack chat replies from silently failing on newer OpenAI model IDs while preserving the existing centralized model-configuration contract.
- Expanded `@Sleuth AI models` to show the current channel, that channel's override, the effective chat model in-context, the workspace default, the complex/date-extraction model, and the number of saved channel overrides. `show-channel-model` now reuses the same override/effective-model wording for consistency.
- Expanded `:wrench:` chat diagnostics beyond `ask-self`: Sleuth now posts generic chat triage for app-mention/Sleuth-owned chat threads, including routing classification, auto-response state, effective model selection, and stored thread context-memory status, while still falling through to reminder triage for unrelated messages and reminder metadata.
- Added chat AI latency logs that record success/failure timing, selected model, channel, and prompt character count for normal replies and override fallbacks. This makes slow GPT-5-family replies observable in production logs instead of looking like a Slack delivery problem.
- #lessonslearned: When model IDs are user-selectable at runtime, hardcoded family matching needs a defensive fallback path or operators will discover upstream naming changes before the app does.

## 1.4.82 - 2026-04-29
- Added shutdown persistence coverage for chat thread context memory by invoking `ChatModule.StopAsync()` during graceful SIGINT teardown.
- Fixed thread-memory upload responses to always reply on the root thread (`thread_ts` when present) so uploads inside existing threads do not fork into separate conversation threads.
- Hardened context-memory file upload handling by catching Slack file download failures, logging them server-side, and posting an actionable in-thread user message instead of failing silently.
- Removed `url_private` from Slack file fetch error text to avoid leaking private Slack file URLs into application logs.

## 1.4.81 - 2026-04-29
- Fixed `@Sleuth AI web-search` command parsing so a whitespace-only invocation like `web-search    ` now returns the usage hint instead of falling through to generic chat.
- Hardened Slack source link rendering for web-search responses by validating source URLs and escaping reserved Slack link characters before embedding them in `<url|title>` markup.

## 1.4.80 - 2026-04-29
- Added Phase 1 web search command: `@Sleuth AI web-search <query>` sends the query through `WorkspaceAI` to OpenAI's Responses API with integrated `web_search`, then posts the sourced answer back in-thread.
- Reserved `@Sleuth AI web-search-advanced <query>` for the planned buffered multi-step synthesis mode and added a clear placeholder response until that mode is implemented.

## 1.4.79 - 2026-04-29
- Natural-language questions about currently available OpenAI/ChatGPT models now fetch the live OpenAI model catalog available to the workspace API key and let the chat model answer from that grounded list. The exact `@Sleuth AI models` command still shows Sleuth's current model configuration.

## 1.4.78 - 2026-04-27
- Added per-channel OpenAI model overrides for chat replies. Workspace admins/owners can now run `@Sleuth AI set-channel-model:'<model>'` to use a more capable model in a specific channel (e.g. `gpt-4o` in a `#project-planning` channel) while leaving the workspace default unchanged elsewhere. Supporting commands: `@Sleuth AI clear-channel-model` and `@Sleuth AI show-channel-model`. The `models` and `commands` admin views were updated to surface these.
- Overrides are persisted per workspace at `data/runtime/workspaces/<WORKSPACE_NAME>_channel_models.json` so they survive restarts. Scope is limited to chat replies (app mention and hands-free message handlers) — reminder-detection AI continues using the workspace `ComplexModelName`. If a stored override fails at chat time (e.g. the model has been deprecated since it was saved), the request is retried once with the workspace default and the failure is logged.

## 1.4.77 - 2026-04-18
- Extended `GET /workspace/:name/reminders` for external integrations. The default response remains the raw reminders array, and callers can now request `?format=rebalance` for a normalized export envelope with metadata, stable lowercase state normalization, optional `activeOnly=true`, and `state=` filtering. This is intended for external consumers such as `rebalance-OS` that need to read the Neochrome reminders file over HTTP instead of copying JSON off disk.
- Web API startup now accepts `WEB_API_BEARER_TOKEN` and `WEB_API_PORT` environment overrides, while preserving the legacy `"test"` token and port `2020` defaults when those variables are absent. `sleuth-app.service` now supports an optional `/root/sleuth-app/.env.runtime` file so production deployments can set those values without editing `src/app.js`.

## 1.4.76 - 2026-04-16
- Added an admin-only `@Sleuth AI ask-woo <question>` command for querying a configured WP DB Toolkit RAG service from Slack. The integration is fail-closed per workspace, lazy-loaded so broken optional code cannot poison startup, and posts a short working acknowledgement before returning the upstream answer in-thread.
- Added per-workspace configuration fields for the WP DB Toolkit integration: `WPDBTK_RAG_ENABLED`, `WPDBTK_RAG_BASE_URL`, `WPDBTK_RAG_SERVICE_TOKEN`, and `WPDBTK_RAG_DEFAULT_SOURCE`.
- Added regression coverage for the new client helper, command gating, timeout/error handling, and lazy-load failure path.

## 1.4.75 - 2026-04-16
- Tightened the `ask-self` RAG ingest contract so source-code indexing is safer and easier to tune: code-file inclusion now follows recursive `src/**` rules while still excluding `src/rag/**`, raw code is ranked below regular docs during retrieval, and ingest skips any source file that exceeds the configured byte-size or chunk-count guards instead of letting one large module dominate the vector corpus.
- Canonicalized changelog handling in the RAG pipeline to uppercase `CHANGELOG.md` only. The lowercase duplicate filename is no longer treated as a changelog corpus source or citation label.

## 1.4.74 - 2026-04-16
- Startup changelog previews now reuse the shared Slack markdown normalizer used by `ask-self`, with a startup-specific plain-bold heading mode. `#` and `##` changelog headings are converted to Slack bold in the startup message instead of showing raw markdown or emoji-prefixed heading styling.

## 1.4.73 - 2026-04-16
- Fixed the startup changelog preview on case-sensitive systems by updating startup notification code to read the canonical uppercase `CHANGELOG.md` filename. This restores the shortened changelog snippet in Slack startup messages on Linux hosts.

## 1.4.72 - 2026-04-16
- Added a hardened one-shot Slack CLI harness for operator-driven real-workspace smoke tests: `npm run slack:harness:post -- ...`. It is dry-run by default, holds a single lock file to prevent concurrent runs, enforces a hard timeout, requires exact channel validation before posting, and authenticates through a new one-shot `SlackApp` client path without starting Sleuth's long-lived Socket Mode event loop.
- Added `PIPELINE.md` at the repo root to document the live Sleuth execution model: startup ordering, Slack event routing, reminder/chat/AI pipelines, file-based persistence, Web API responsibilities, the Jest and Mock Slack test layers, and the new one-shot Slack smoke-test harness.

## 1.4.71 - 2026-04-16
- Fixed release-blocking `npm run build` failures introduced with the `ask-self` RAG module by tightening JSDoc/checkJs types in `src/rag/`, adding a local `better-sqlite3` declaration for TypeScript resolution, and making Slack markdown normalization array inference explicit. No runtime behavior change intended.

## 1.4.70 - 2026-04-15
- Added `ask-self` app-mention command: `@Sleuth ask-self <question>` answers questions about Sleuth itself, grounded in a local RAG index built from the repo's docs, changelog, and recent GitHub PR bodies. Uses Gemini `gemini-embedding-001` for embeddings and `gemini-pro-latest` for synthesis. Index is stored in sqlite-vec under `data/rag/` (gitignored, rebuilt via `npm run rag:ingest`).
- Hard-gated to the Neochrome workspace via `NEOCHROME_TEAM_ID` allowlist. Non-Neochrome workspaces get zero response, zero RAG code entry, zero Gemini calls — the command is effectively invisible outside Neochrome. Fail-closed when the env var is unset (ask-self is inert everywhere, normal chat still works). Eight Jest regression tests in `tests/rag-ask-self-tenancy.test.js` cover the escape routes; CI static grep check in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` fails the build if the tenancy gate is removed from either gated file.
- `SlackApp.TeamId` is now captured at `StartAsync` from `auth.test` and exposed via a read-only getter for workspace-scoped feature gating.
- Containerized under `src/rag/` (ingest script, query module, external prompts) for future spin-off. Integration with the rest of Sleuth is limited to a `TeamId` getter on `SlackApp`, a new `ask-self` regex branch in `ChatModule`, and a `TeamId` getter on `MockSlackApp`.

## 1.4.69 - 2026-04-14
- Added `:mag:` discovery hint reaction: in channels where reminders are disabled, Sleuth now silently reacts to messages that look schedulable (direct ask + time trigger) so users know Sleuth is present and could help if enabled. Users can follow up with `:wrench:` for full channel/triage diagnostics. No AI calls — reuses the cheap regex shared with the deterministic fallback.

## 1.4.68 - 2026-04-14
- Workspace admins and owners can now enable/disable reminders in any channel, in addition to the channel creator. Unblocks bot setup in channels where the creator is unavailable.

## 1.4.67 - 2026-04-10
- `:wrench:` reminder triage diagnostic now reports channel status (auto-scheduling enabled/disabled, configured reminder target channel). When auto-scheduling is disabled in the current channel, the diagnostic includes a hint to enable it via `@Sleuth AI enable reminders`, closing a discoverability gap where triage would recommend `schedule` while the message was silently filtered from the auto-scheduling path.

## 1.4.66 - 2026-04-10
- Fixed GitHub comment relay not posting to GitHub when the original reminder message was a thread reply rather than a top-level channel message. Relay now stores `OriginalThreadTs` (the Slack thread root ts) at reminder creation time and matches against it; falls back to `OriginalMessageID` for top-level messages and legacy reminders without the new field.
- #lessonslearned: Slack's `thread_ts` always points to the thread root regardless of which reply in the thread triggered an action — store `thread_ts ?? null` separately from `ts` whenever the two need to be distinguished at query time.

## 1.4.65 - 2026-04-10
- Fixed GitHub comment relay crash when Slack message has no text (e.g. file-share-only or message_changed subtypes).

## 1.4.64 - 2026-04-10
- Added `:wrench:` reaction support for per-message reminder triage diagnostics posted in-thread.
- Triage diagnostics now guard against messages with no text (file shares, edits, block-kit-only) and post a clear "no text to triage" response.
- Triage diagnostic lines sanitize trigger/task values (escape quotes, collapse newlines, truncate) via new `SlackFormatUtils.SanitizeForInlineSlack` helper.
- Deterministic direct-ask fallback now rejects negation/cancellation phrases (e.g. "please don't deploy today") so the model's `ignore` recommendation is respected.
- Added deterministic fallback for direct request messages with explicit time triggers when reminder AI returns `ignore`.
- Improved reminder task text quality by falling back to actionable language when AI output is over-compressed.
- Added same-day handling for past `"this morning"` extraction to reduce accidental next-day scheduling.
- Updated reminder AI instructions for direct asks and multi-step task preservation.
- #lessonslearned: User-facing diagnostics exposed at the same reaction layer as manual scheduling are faster to adopt and easier to debug than log-only paths.
